# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1993

## Introduction by Ameyātmā dāsa

All glories unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is our very life and soul, and who has given us everything!

This year marks the sixteenth year since the physical departure of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda from this world, the ninety-seventh year since his all-merciful appearance, and the twenty-eighth year since he set foot upon the land of America and began his preaching in the Western countries. As time moves us closer to the hundredth anniversary of his appearance, all his spiritual children are eager to remember and glorify him more and more. I personally feel I have been so neglectful in this regard. That so few in this world are actually aware of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s life and teachings is to the discredit of neglectful devotees like myself. We have a grave mission ahead of us—to somehow or other inform the entire world of the absolute greatness of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s life and teachings.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is such a rare and exalted spiritual personality. My realization is that he will become more glorified in this world than Jesus Christ. It is simply a question of time. As his direct and second-generation disciples, we are duty-bound to see that this period of time is made as short as possible.

Each morning we sing *dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*. We must remember the glories and offer respectful obeisances to our spiritual master at least three times every day, at the three solar junctions—sunrise, noon, and sunset. And one day a year, on the most auspicious appearance day of our spiritual master, we especially remember and glorify him. In this way we become purified. And since it is not possible to repay the spiritual master for saving us from the repeated cycle of birth and death, on this day we try as much as possible to repay a token of what we owe him.

This Vyāsa-pūjā book is, therefore, a most important instrument and means for us to both glorify and remember Śrīla Prabhupāda. It gives us inspiration to read how much love we all share for him, how much Śrīla Prabhupāda means to all of us. And, of course, there are those all-blissful photos, which have captured the grace and purity of Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee, and which help our remembrance of him and affection for him come forth and increase.

I must thank all of you for tolerating someone as lowly as myself to introduce this volume of the Vyāsa-pūjā book, and let us all thank the many devotees who have worked hard over the years to produce these wonderful gems, which always bring so much personal happiness to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Ameyātmā dāsa

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Kīrtirāja dāsa

Since time immemorial, disciples have reflected on their relationship with their spiritual master, and they have compiled prayers, poems, and statements in glorification of him. They have understood perfectly the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā and have imbibed that in their hearts and manifested it in their daily lives. Today, this unqualified disciple of the greatest spiritual master to ever live is making his feeble attempt not only to understand the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā but to explain it to others so they might understand the meaning and importance of this most auspicious and glorious event.

The most important principle in spiritual life is the relationship between the spiritual master and the disciple. Śrīla Prabhupāda is not just a spiritual master: he is the fountainhead from which all spiritual masters in ISKCON today have come. To be a link in a bona fide disciplic chain is not an ordinary thing. Above and beyond being a link in this succession coming from Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself, Śrīla Prabhupāda is the foremost preacher of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the present age. And there’s no doubt that he will remain the foremost preacher of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for the next ten thousand years, no matter what anyone else may say or do.

Countless individuals throughout the world have come to know about Kṛṣṇa by the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Certainly, some people knew about Kṛṣṇa, and others would have come to know about Kṛṣṇa without Śrīla Prabhupāda. However, in most cases those people would be presented with some adulterated, offensive, or perverted understanding of who Kṛṣṇa is (or was). By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda, the true and bona fide representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the pure message of Kṛṣṇa and the true understanding of Kṛṣṇa is available to one and all.

We honor the spiritual master on this day, Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, the anniversary of the day he appeared in this world. Śrīla Prabhupāda didn’t have to appear in this world. But because of his compassion for the fallen conditioned souls and his intense desire to fulfill the prophecy of Lord Caitanya—that His message would be spread outside the boundaries of India, to every town and village—Śrīla Prabhupāda appeared in this world.

In this world, especially in the Western world, we take everything for granted. We’re spoiled. Some people say “spoiled rotten.” We expect that everything must be there when and where we want it, and if it’s not we become disturbed. Today we see so many terrible things happening in other parts of the world and we can understand a little that we take day-to-day necessities and occurrences for granted. Do we also take Śrīla Prabhupāda for granted? Do we take his appearance in this world for granted? Do we take his mercy for granted? What if Śrīla Prabhupāda hadn’t appeared in this world? What if he hadn’t come to save us from the hellish existence that surrounds us? What if we never came in contact with him, his instructions, and his movement? Where would we all be today? We’re all here in ISKCON today because of Śrīla Prabhupāda and because of the mercy he has given to us. We’re all a part of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava Sampradāya because of Śrīla Prabhupāda. We’re writing and reading Vyāsa-pūjā offerings because of Śrīla Prabhupāda. And, in the case of some of us, we’re probably still alive only because of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We must observe the Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony of Śrīla Prabhupāda to always remember that we’re his menial servants. We must offer prayers and obeisances and make offerings to our spiritual master for our purification, and today is a very special day on which to make these offerings. Today is a day to reaffirm our commitment to our spiritual master, and it is a day to remember all that we may have forgotten about our spiritual master throughout the year while we’re running here and there, doing this and that.

We serve and worship Śrīla Prabhupāda during the year, and depending on our love for and devotion to him, our dedication to his mission and our understanding of him, we become more dear to him. If we find ourselves operating on “automatic pilot” for most of the year, Vyāsa-pūjā day is a time to STOP and meditate on the significance of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s position in this Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava Sampradāya and on the profound influence he has had on our lives.

By offering obeisances to our spiritual master, we are offering obeisances to all the members of the disciplic succession. Today is a day to reflect upon the great ancestral tradition that we’ve been born into at the time of our initiation, to thank Śrīla Prabhupāda for bringing us into this family, and to thank the Lord for giving us Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, while eternally remaining with his sincere followers to instruct and guide them, has simultaneously gone on to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa in another location. When once asked who he really is, Śrīla Prabhupāda responded by saying, “If I told you, you wouldn’t believe me.”

Without Śrīla Prabhupāda, the knowledge of the *Vedas* would have remained a mystery to us. It might have happened that through our own speculative imagination or that of someone else, we may have thought we had come to understand the message of the *Vedas,* but thinking like that would mean that we were even further from the truth.

By this Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony we are honoring Śrīla Prabhupāda by glorifying him, by making offerings to him, by offering obeisances to him, and by telling the world of his transcendental position. All of these offerings to Śrīla Prabhupāda are being presented by him to the entire Guru Paramparā, starting with his own spiritual master. So often, Śrīla Prabhupāda would tell us that he was not accepting anything on his own behalf but that he was accepting it on behalf of and offering it to his spiritual master. He would always tell us that we were assisting him in his service to his spiritual master. This is the attitude that we should always remember and never forget. We are here by the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda, and everything that we have has come to us by his kindness and is meant to be used in his service and to further his mission of serving the instructions of his Guru Mahārāja.

Śrīla Vyāsadeva was most merciful because He compiled the *Vedas* for the benefit of future generations of devotees. And Śrīla Prabhupāda is equally merciful because he has taken the words of Śrīla Vyāsadeva and made them available for everyone’s benefit and purification. Śrīla Prabhupāda used to say that everyone is a devotee, only some have remembered and some have forgotten. Śrīla Prabhupāda has distributed the mercy of Śrīla Vyāsadeva to everyone, rememberer and forgetter alike, because he did not discriminate. Śrīla Vyāsadeva and Śrīla Prabhupāda are a perfect team: Śrīla Vyasadeva has compiled the *Vedas,* and Śrīla Prabhupāda has distributed them.

I pray that during this Vyāsa-pūjā celebration and during every day in the future we will always remember Śrīla Prabhupāda and never forget him.

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, our eternal master, father, and friend!

*I beg to remain his servant,*  
Kīrtirāja dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Badrinārāyan dāsa

Another year has passed, and as I grow older,  
time is a little less kind, a little bit colder.

The eyesight fades, the knees grow weak;  
the youthful energy, the bright pink cheek,  
the swagger, the bravado—all are gone for good.  
There’s a middle-aged man where a youth once stood.

I’m beginning to see that life’s not an endless ride;  
now what will I do with the time still on my side?

Will I dream of desires left in the past?  
Will I fan them, feed them, and try to make them last?

Or will I follow your spotless example,  
a “Vaikuṇṭha man,” a spiritual sample?

So many lives struggled in vain—  
what was the benefit, what was the gain?

One man built a house in which the whole world could live,  
one man, “*ātmārāma,*” wanted only to give.

Just one full moon lights up the while sky,  
your words are the only safe guide to steer by.

As marginal energy, we’ve got our free choice:  
will we follow Māya’s call or heed your clear voice?

As Kṛṣṇa chastised Arjuna to stand up and fight,  
there’s a purpose for your giving us spiritual sight.

The people are suffering their *karma’s* great weight—  
“Don’t live,” “Don’t die,” their unfortunate state.

“Causeless mercy” is the gift from you we’ve received.  
The measure of our gratitude, how many souls we’ve retrieved.

Your service gives sanity, peacefulness, and taste.  
To the degree that I please you, my life’s not a waste.

We all have to answer for the things that we’ve done;  
my life taken as the measure, will I be a good son?

We will meet again someday in a distant place and time—  
please bless me with the strength to offer you something sublime.

Thank you for keeping me in your service,  
Badrinārāyaṇa dāsa

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace as you continue to give shelter and bestow mercy to all those fortunate souls who fully surrender at your lotus feet.

With mixed feelings I am attempting to write this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā offering for your glorification and for my purification.

On the one hand I feel so much incompetent to represent you properly within the ISKCON Society. My faults, mistakes, and shortcomings seem to increase every day as I attempt to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When hearing your lectures and seeing how expertly you present the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I feel guilty and incompetent for my inability to comprehend and express things as clearly as you do.

Your books, your lectures, your conversations, your *darśanas—*all show you to be the most expert, the most learned, the most versatile, the most profound, the most articulate, the most powerful, the most determined, the most surrendered, and the most humble and dedicated preacher of the mission of your Guru Mahārāja. As Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura so nicely prays:

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyāṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.”**

All of these qualities and more that you possess establish you on the highest platform of pure devotional service. Yes, you are the real *paramahaṁsa,* the swanlike devotee of the Lord who must surely be fully pleasing to your Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, for you are always remembering his instructions.

In witnessing these symptoms within you, I feel guilty because they are not in me. Yet simultaneously I know for certain that this process of devotional service is the most sublime and nectarean of activities. I am absolutely convinced that what you are giving us is the highest of perfections. Recently a person asked me if I had found real happiness. I could convincingly reply, “Yes, I have found real happiness because I have found devotional service.” And that devotional service has been given by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. So you are actually the cause of my happiness.

In *The Nectar of Devotion* Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī explains three types of happiness—material happiness, transcendental happiness, and finally devotional happiness.

The whole world is suffering due to this lack of devotional happiness. The work of the ā*cārya* is to bring this devotional happiness to as many conditioned souls as possible. This, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have done, you are doing, and you will continue to do eternally.

On this the 97th Vyāsa-pūjā anniversary of Your Divine Grace, please help me develop some of the wonderful devotional qualities that you possess. Please help me advance in devotional service, and please help me help others become attracted to the process of pure devotion.

On this, your auspicious appearance day, I wish to re-dedicate myself fully to the preaching mission of your Guru Mahārāja and that of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

My only prayer is that you may help this insignificant soul become purified and advance somehow or other on the path of pure devotion.

Please engage me in your service as you please and allow me to become a menial servant of yours for eternity. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your aspiring servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhsya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear respected Vaiṣṇavas,

On this most auspicious day of the glorious Vyāsa-pūjā celebration of His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, let me speak something about the art and science of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the public at large—scientists, scholars, everyone—in the light of the teachings and examples set forth by Śrīla Prabhupāda and the *paramparā*.

The goal of preaching is to bring everyone to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. From the accounts of the preaching encounters of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu with Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī and Sarvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya, we can learn a great deal about how to preach:

*prabhura miṣṭa-vākya śuni’ sannyāsīra gaṇa*  
*citta phiri’ gela, kahe madhura vacana*

**“After hearing Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, all the Māyāvādī** ***sannyāsīs*** **were moved. Their minds changed, and thus they spoke with pleasing words” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā*** **7.99).**

In his purport Śrīla Prabhupāda writes: “The Māyāvādī sannyāsīs met Caitanya Mahāprabhu at Vārāṇasī to criticize the Lord regarding His participation in the *saṅkīrtana* movement, which they did not like. This demonic nature of opposition to the *saṅkīrtana* movement perpetually exists. As it existed in the time of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, similarly it existed long before that, even in the time of Prahlāda Mahāraja. …

Impersonalist Māyāvādīs always try to defy Vaiṣṇavas because Vaiṣṇavas accept the Supreme Personality as the supreme cause and want to serve Him, talk with Him and see Him, just as the Lord is also eager to see His devotees and talk, eat and dance with them. These personal exchanges of love do not appeal to the Māyāvādī *sannyāsīs*. Therefore the original purpose of the Māyāvādī *sannyāsīs* of Benares in meeting Caitanya Mahāprabhu was to defeat His personal conception of God. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, however, as a preacher, turned the minds of the Māyāvādī *sannyāsīs*. They were melted by the sweet words of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and thus became friendly and spoke to Him also in sweet words. Similarly, all preachers will have to meet opponents, but they should not make them more inimical. They are already enemies, and if we talk with them harshly or impolitely their enmity will merely increase. We should therefore follow in the footsteps of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu as far as possible and try to convince the opposition by quoting from the *śāstras* and presenting the conclusion of the *ācāryas.* It is in this way that we should try to defeat all the enemies of the Lord.”

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya-līlā,* Chapter Six, starting with verse 123, there is a long discussion between Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Sarvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya that runs as follows:

Thus for seven days continuously, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu listened to the Vedānta philosophy expounded by Sarvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya. However, Caitanya Mahāprabhu did not say anything and did not indicate whether it was right or wrong. He simply sat there and listened to the Bhaṭṭācārya.  
On the eighth day, Sarvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya said to Caitanya Mahāprabhu: “You have been listening to the Vedānta philosophy from me continuously for seven days. You have simply been listening, fixed in Your silence. Since You do not say whether You think it is right or wrong, I cannot know whether You are actually understanding Vedānta philosophy or not.”  
Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu replied: “I am a fool, and consequently I do not study *Vedānta-sūtra*. I am just trying to hear it from you because you have ordered Me. Only for the sake of executing the duties of the renounced order of *sannyāsa* do I listen. Unfortunately, I cannot in the least understand the meaning you are presenting.”  
Sarvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya replied: “I accept that You do not understand, yet even one who does not understand inquires about the subject matter. You are hearing again and again, yet You keep silent. I cannot understand what is actually within Your mind.”

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu then revealed His mind, saying: “I can understand the meaning of each *sūtra* very clearly, but your explanations have simply agitated My mind.” Thus Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu criticized Śaṅkarācārya’s *Śārīraka-bhāṣya* as imaginary, and He pointed out hundreds of faults in it. To defend Śaṅkarācārya, however, Sarvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya presented unlimited opposition. The Bhaṭṭācārya presented various types of false arguments with pseudologic and tried to defeat his opponent in many ways. However, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu refuted all these arguments and established His own conviction.

Thus we can see that the dealings are in a friendly manner filled with mutual respect. When this relationship of mutual respect is established the philosophy can be presented with full force and without any compromise.

If the preacher has the opportunity to present humbly the science and philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, this is the most preferable avenue of approach. This is visible in Prabhupāda’s own instructions in the morning walk conversation that follows, which took place in Nairobi, Kenya, on October 28, 1975:

**Prabhupāda:** Wherever you go, you gather people and induce them by flattering them, by falling on their leg—some way or other, induce them. *Dante nidhāya tṛṇakaṁ padayor nipatya* \[*Śrī Caitanya-candrāmṛta* 8.90\]. This is the process shown by Prabodhānanda Sarasvatī, “Taking a straw in my mouth and falling down on your lotus feet.” *Kāku-śataṁ kṛtvā:* “And I am trying to please you by so many flattering words.” *Kṛtvā ahaṁ bravīmi:* “I have got some submission…” This is the process. …  
So now, when he will say, “All right, you can say what you want to say,” then, *he sādhava:* “You are a great personality, nobleman.” He may be a loafer class \[laughter\], but give him all honor: “You are so great and so exalted,” *he sādhava,* “and so honest. … My only submission is that whatever you have learned, you forget. Whatever nonsense you have learned…” Don’t say “nonsense” \[laughter\]. But we must know that he is a *pakka* rascal, nonsense. \[laughs\] So don’t say directly, “nonsense.” Say, “You are the great personality. So kindly, whatever you have learned, forget.”  
“Then what shall I do?”  
*He sādhavaḥ sakalam eva vihāya dūrāt:* “Kick out whatever nonsense you learned.”  
“Then what shall I do?”  
“Now, *caitanya-candra-caraṇe kurutānurāgam:* Kindly be submissive to Lord Caitanya, and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa.”  
This is our program. No argument. Because he is a rascal, what is the use of arguing with him? He’s a rascal number one. You know that. You cannot expect any good argument from the rascals and fools. Where is the logic? Their logic is to beat them with shoes. That is the only logic. But that, if you do that, then you’ll not be able to preach. But otherwise that is the only logic, to beat them with shoes. *Argumentum vaculum.* You know this logic? …

**Cyavana:** That is Latin.

**Prabhupāda:** Yes. You know? What is that? *Argumentum vaculum?* With fools and rascals, with animals, there is no logic. Take the stick and beat them. The dog, if he is a dog, what is the argument with him? Take the stick and beat him; then he will go away. That is called *argumentum baculum*. That is, Sanskrit logic, also said, *murkhasya latau sadhi* (?) “One who is a fool, his only remedy is beating him with stick.”  
This is material. But we are not doing anything material. It is all spiritual. Therefore, especially in this age, Caitanya Mahāprabhu, *tṛnād api sunīcena taror api sahiṣṇunā amāninā mānadena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ*. So we should preach like this, any rascal, because anyone who has no information of Kṛṣṇa, he’s a rascal. That’s all. That we must know. But if you say that—”You are rascal...”—then you cannot preach. If you directly call a rascal a rascal, he’ll be angry. *Murkhayopadeśo hi prakopāya na śānta …* In this way preaching is practical. Otherwise it is not possible. Everyone is thinking he is the most exalted personality, scientist, philosopher, great man. That is the material disease … You are dealing with all rascals. So if we call them directly \[rascal\] they will be angry. Your preaching will not be successful. So follow the principles enunciated by Prabodhānanda Sarasvatī and Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Since ours is a preaching movement, it is of vital importance that we understand the proper methodology and spirit of presenting Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the public at large. My own personal understanding in this area is to evaluate the time and circumstances, then act accordingly. Occasions may arise that we have to fight, but the situation may also dictate that we exercise the utmost patience and tolerance.

In the intimate atmosphere of the morning walks on Venice Beach, in Los Angeles, Prabhupāda gave many instructions in this manner, such as the conversation quoted below, recorded on May 3, 1973:

**Prabhupāda:** So you protest, write in books. You are scientist. Prove scientifically. That will be your laurel of taking the doctorate degree. If you also become one of these rascal doctors, then what is the use of your Kṛṣṇa consciousness, that whatever they say you silently accept? No. Just become Babhrūvahana, Manipur Babhrūvahana, that the fighting is faced. Take assistance, you have got. Ask Dr. Rao to come, join. Make plan. Go from town to town, all over the world. This has to be done. … If you are mischievous, you are criminal. But if you tolerate mischievous activities, that is also criminal. Challenge these rascals. Stop their rascal theories.

These are strong signals implying that with the utmost seriousness we have to face the scientists, scholars, everyone who is against the science and philosophy of Kṛṣṇa. This can only be achieved by the blessings of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.22.7, in the purport, Śrīla Prabhupāda summarizes all these considerations beyond any shadow of a doubt:

This combination, the instruction of the spiritual master and the faithful execution of the instruction by the disciple, makes the entire process perfect. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura describes in his explanation of the verse in *Bhagavad-gītā, vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ,* that one who wants to be certain to achieve spiritual success must take the instruction from the spiritual master as to what his particular function is. He should faithfully try to execute that particular instruction and should consider that his life and soul. The faithful execution of the instruction which he receives from the spiritual master is the only duty of a disciple, and that will bring him perfection. One should be very careful to receive the message from the spiritual master through the ears and execute it faithfully. That will make one’s life successful.

On this most auspicious day I humbly pray for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s blessings so that I can be of some small help to His divine mission.

His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda’s most insignificant servant,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami  
May 1st, 1993, New Dvārakā Dhāma, Los Angeles

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My only visit to your room at 26 Second Avenue was sweet. Kīrtanānanda and Hayagrīva discussed with you the quality of cows they should purchase for New Vrindaban, Gaurasundar showed us, on your order, his cartoon drawing of Dr. Frog puffing up and exploding as he guesstimated the size of the ocean, and Umāpati nostalgically expressed that sitting in your apartment with you was “just like the old days.” You replied to Umāpati, “There are no old days in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” I lagged behind when the other devotees left. I wanted a special order from you and stood at your doorway and shyly asked for a personal service. You replied with grandfatherly kindness, “Take this Kṛṣṇa consciousness and be happy. *Jaya*!”

I have failed. I have no devotion and I am miserable.

Once, in your room in Melbourne, I sat before your low desk. You mentioned to the devotees present that one must remain a fool before one’s spiritual master. With false humility, superficiality, and a grin, I remarked that my only qualification was that I was a fool. You didn’t smile back. With a stern look, your head slightly nodding, you added, “Yes, but you should not remain a fool.” The others laughed and I laughed along with them.

I am still a fool.

Your Guru Mahārāja in his final days requested every tender-hearted member of the Gauḍīya Maṭha to be prepared to shed hundreds of gallons of blood to save a single conditioned soul. You were prepared. You selflessly spent your every drop to insure the homeward journey of the citizens of this planet. How satisfied your Guru Mahārāja is with you is beyond my estimation. Your love, devotion, service, and renunciation surely have purchased Kṛṣṇa.

As Kṛṣṇa spoke to His *gopīs* when they appeared before Him in Vṛndāvana’s dark forest in the dead of the night: “I am not able to repay My debt to you for your spotless devotional service, even within a lifetime of Brahmā.” (*Bhāg.* 10.32.22)

Only pure devotion at the level of *prema* truly conquers the unconquerable Lord and eternally indentures Him in the service of His devotees. The level of your service is so incredible that even the *gopīs* themselves glorify you as the most magnanimous personality.

*tava kathāmṛtaṁ tapta-jīvanaṁ*  
*kavibhir Īḍitaṁ kalmaṣāpaham*  
*śravaṇa maṅgalaṁ śrīmad ātataṁ*  
*bhuvi gṛṇanti ye bhūri-dā janāḥ*

**“These narrations \[of Kṛṣṇa’s glories\], transmitted by learned sages, eradicate one’s sinful reactions and bestow good fortune upon whoever hears them. These narrations are broadcast all over the world and are filled with spiritual power. Certainly those who spread the message of Godhead are most munificent.” (*****Bhāg*****. 10.31.9)**

The tolerance you exhibit by accepting my “service” and allowing me to continue amazes me. I know that I possess *anarthas* that pollute each of my endeavors. I know I do nothing without sneaking some cut. I have no devotion, I am miserable, and I am an undeserving fool who have no hope but your causeless mercy. Kṛṣṇa, the independent Lord, is yours. You have purchased Him. If you consider my qualifications or my achievements, I am lost. Could you please expend a small portion of Kṛṣṇa’s indebtedness to you by requesting Him to sprinkle a drop of His mercy upon me?

Nityānanda Prabhu and Caitanya Mahāprabhu encouraged all the Vaiṣṇavas of Their time by saving Jāgai and Mādhāi. Please consider how encouraged all of today’s Vaiṣṇavas will feel if one such as me makes rapid advancement by your special mercy.

I desire to serve you eternally.

Your servant,  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Girirāja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

The best way to get your mercy is through your books. Please make me your instrument.

Please make me your instrument to distribute them.

Please make me your instrument to discuss them.

Please make me your instrument to realize them.

Everything is in your books.

The other day I came across some Sanskrit terms I had never come across before. I thought, “Where will I get the definitions for these terms?” The next day I found the same terms defined in one of your purports in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

I then decided to teach the same book in VIHE, thinking the best way to learn your books is to discuss them with your devotees. Especially I want to learn with the book distributors and other devotees fixed in the service of your mission year after year who come to the *dhāma* for your association and mercy. I want to learn with your servants and get their mercy.

*rāja-vidyā rāja-guhyaṁ*  
*pavitram idam uttamam*  
*pratyakṣāvagaṁ dharmyaṁ*  
*su-sukhaṁ kartum avyayam*

**“The knowledge is the king of education, the most secret of all secrets. It is the purest knowledge, and because it gives direct perception of the self by realization, it is the perfection of religion. It is everlasting, and it is joyfully performed.”**

Please allow us to enter more and more deeply into your books.

Please bless us with both knowledge and realization.

Please keep us as  
Your eternal servants,  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāla Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On the occasion of the ninety-seventh anniversary of your auspicious appearance on this planet, I beg to offer my most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. Although I have absolutely no ability to glorify you, I am once again attempting to write a few lines. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya-līlā* 2.13-14) the author says,

*tā-sabā tārite prabhu sei saba deśe*  
*yogya-bhakta jīva-dehe karena ‘āveśe’*  
*sei jīve nija-bhakti karena prakāśe*  
*tāhāra darśane ‘vaiṣṇava’ haya sarva-deśe*

**“To deliver people in regions throughout the universe who could not meet Him, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu personally entered the bodies of pure devotees. Thus He empowered living beings \[His pure devotees\] by manifesting in them so much of His own devotion that people in all other countries became devotees by seeing them.”**

Purport: “…Unless one is empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, one cannot spread the holy names of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* throughout the world. Therefore they are sometimes called ā*veśa-avatāras,* or incarnations, for they are endowed with the power of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I can understand from the above that you are such a personality. You are an ā*veśa-avatāra,* who appeared just to fulfill Lord Caitanya’s prediction that the holy name of Kṛṣṇa would spread to every town and village on this planet.

The success in preaching that you accomplished has never been achieved by any other devotee in history. Within a short space of time you opened Kṛṣṇa consciousness centers in every corner on this planet. You made the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* a household word. Due to your purity and simplicity, everyone who met you was attracted to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You never resorted to the display of mystic power or magic to fool the mass of people. You convinced everyone that *śreyas,* or activities which are ultimately beneficial and auspicious, should be performed instead of *preyas,* or those which are immediately beneficial. You truly distributed that timeless wisdom of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone. You converted thousands of *mlecchas* into *brāhmaṇas*. You showed that regardless of birth, everyone can chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* and practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Just as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was able to cure the leper Vāsudeva, you cured the diseased tendencies of thousands by giving them the opportunity to serve in your ISKCON institution.

Your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is continuing to expand, not just maintain. You were the force that started this movement, and your are the force that is giving your disciples and granddisciples courage, strength, and enthusiasm to expand your movement. Because of your books, letters, conversations, tapes, lectures, videos, photographs, Deities, one is constantly feeling your presence. No other spiritual leader has left behind as many books as you have. Your instructions will give light to the people of Kali-yuga for the next ten thousand years. Your ISKCON movement is growing day by day and is now giving shelter to the whole planet. Your movement has even penetrated the most barren godless regions.

In the *Hari-bhakti-vilāsa,* it is stated that difficult things become easy to understand if one remembers Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and easy things become difficult to understand if one forgets Him. Similarly, when we remember your instructions, everything becomes clear and simple. Therefore I have to dedicate myself to your instructions and help expand the preaching effort, especially book distribution.

In order to protect Vāsudeva, Lord Caitanya advised him to chant and preach about Kṛṣṇa. You also wanted the members of ISKCON to dedicate themselves to the preaching work—especially book distribution. By distributing your books, we are giving the conditioned souls your association and an opportunity to appreciate your greatness.

On this most auspicious day of the anniversary of your appearance on this planet, I, your most unqualified disciple, beg you to please allow me to serve you eternally. Please give me guidance and strength to take shelter of your *vāṇī*. May I always want to please you through all may activities. Please don’t neglect me.

Your insignificant servant,  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Gour-Govinda Swami

*sakṣad-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sādbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“In the revealed scriptures it is described that the spiritual master should be worshiped like the Supreme Personality of Godhead; this injunction is acknowledged by all the devotees of the Lord. Thus let me offer my respectful obeisances to the lotus feet of the Spiritual Master.”**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda, Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
Jaya Gurudeva, Jaya Gurudeva, Jaya Gurudeva!

O blessed, dearest Gurudeva, Śrīla Abhay Charan Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupādjī Mahārāja, on this very auspicious occasion of the holy appearance of Your Divine Grace, this eternal servant would like to seek your kind permission to offer his most humble, respectful, and heartfelt obeisances at the holy dust of your divine lotus feet.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we especially feel proud to claim today that our beloved Spiritual Master has truly translated into action the sacred prophecy of Śrīmān Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu, namely, *pṛthivīte āśhe yata nagarādi-grāma / sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma:* “The glorification of My name will be spread to every town, city, and village of the world.” Thus Your Divine Grace is very correctly addressed as *gauravāṇī-pracāriṇe.* Similar is the cent percent truthfulness of Your Divine Grace being addressed as *pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe,* because who but Your great Divine Grace could so magnificently penetrate the hard-core hearts of the Western diehard materialists and instill therein the perennial, ever-nectarian, and ever-pure song of unalloyed Kṛṣṇa *prema,* and do it on such a large scale yet in such a short span of time. This is unparalleled in the history of the Kali-yuga.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, the immense love and affection that Your Divine Grace felt for the land and the people of Orissa, the holy land of Lord Jagannātha, is very well revealed from your continuous sixteen-day-long stay at Bhubaneswar during the long-ago days of 1977, in connection with laying the foundation stone for the construction of the temple of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma. Dear Śrīla Gurudeva, during that ceremony you prophesied that the then desertlike site would become the center of Bhubaneswar, which would become the built-up capital city of Orissa. This prophecy of yours has come true. Many important government, semi-government, quasi-government, and private projects are established or being established around the temple site, making the area very crowded, active, and lively.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, to honor and fulfill the holy desire of Your Divine Grace, who, seeing your dear disciples refused entry into the great temple of Their Lordships at Śrī Jagannātha Purī, had once emotionally expressed the wish to see the holy appearance of Their Lordships in the then proposed temple at Bhubaneswar, Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā Devī, out of Their causeless loving mercy, appeared in the temple, joining together with Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma. So did Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, who also, out of Their pure love for Their dear devotee, i.e., Your Divine Grace, volunteered to appear in the temple, making it Their own abode and joining with Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma and Śrī Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā Devī. Perhaps Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi appeared to express Their recognition for the matchless pure service rendered by Your Divine Grace to Their great cause in the contemporary degraded Age of Kali.

Admittedly true, however, is the fact, O dear Śrīla Gurudeva, that this last-founded temple of yours is still struggling to reach the long-cherished goal of fulfilling your noble wish to have it become one of the very best among all the ISKCON temples around the world in singing the glory of pure Kṛṣṇa *prema*. Hundreds of people from all over Orissa, sometimes from different parts of India, and even from abroad as well, are visiting this last-founded temple of yours every day and offering their respectful obeisances unto the divine lotus feet of Their Lordships. Thus the number of guests and visiting devotees increases day by day. On the last great auspicious occasion of the holy Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Janmāṣṭamī, about eighty thousand people visited the temple, making it (proportionately) a record gathering of devotees in comparison to other temples, since one has to take into account the relatively much smaller population of the city of Bhubaneswar. This gathering showed the successful onward march toward the implementation of your noble wish for the temple here becoming one of the very best among your temples around the world.

O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, even so, we still lag far behind in fulfilling the pure desire of Your Divine Grace. Both the structural aspect of the temple project and its effectiveness in preaching the ever *virjavati hari-kathā* need to be far advanced, solely resting on the pure blessings of Your Divine Grace.

Repeatedly pray for your causeless mercy to shoulder the responsibility in carrying out your order, and I offer hundreds and thousands of prostrated *dandavat pranāms* at the holy dust of Your Divine lotus feet.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your eternal yet most lowly servant,  
Gour Govinda Swami

## Guruprasāda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances and allow me to bathe in the *cintamaṇi* dust of your lotus feet.

Every year when I receive the brief opportunity to sit in your room in Vṛndavāna, I feel your presence in a special way. This time I visualized that the *samādhis* of the different ā*cāryas* had manifested in that most sacred shrine. On pilgrimage in Vṛndāvana I beg those exalted personalities to concede a glimpse of understanding about their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. But they are more accessible in your room because you are transferring their mercy to us. You comment in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that through the *paramparā* system one receives the accumulated mercy of each member. That is the immense benefit of being the servant of the servant of the servant. Therefore, I feel your room to be the hub of all spiritual understanding. By chanting there I can feel your blessings imbued with all of the ā*cārya’s* benedictions.

While glancing over your books in so many languages, I saw the unlimited compassion of the ā*cāryas* descending upon countless *jīvas,* who cannot fathom that they are being engulfed by the transcendental aura of Vṛndāvana. As I took the dust from the foot of your bed, I reflected upon your personal sacrifice. You relinquished the nectar imbibed by other ā*cāryas* who spent their last years in the holy *dhāma* relishing blissful *samādhi*.

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura transferred his mood of preaching to you. You took this as your life and soul and gave us perfect realization of the transcendental order of *guru*. Your ISKCON Society is a manifestation of that mood. You have inculcated the preaching spirit in our hearts by your instructions and example. That spiritual sentiment is the thrust of your *samādhi*. Please give this fallen soul the mercy of being able to participate in some way.

Now you are sitting in eternal *samādhi* in your Vṛndāvana quarters. You took this opportunity only after your physical departure. That *samādhi* is very special. It reflects the severity of Vṛndāvana before all the devotees. At the same time, in your unique “preaching *samādhi*” you continue to attract, inspire, purify, and liberate countless souls. Please allow me to enter into that sublime preaching consciousness. Seeing you there, I could not suspect even for a moment that you have left. I simply beg that by your causeless mercy you secure me eternally to your lotus feet. Don’t allow me to leave them, venturing into distant lands previously known to my mind, thinking how I can conquer them and be the master. Allow me to conquer any desire which leads me away from your lotus feet.

Your eternal servant,  
Guruprasāda Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my most humble obeisances thrown in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, who have instructed me in what it means to fulfill the desire of the spiritual master.

Before writing this year’s offering, I have been thinking about what it was that you did to teach me how to dedicate my life to fulfilling your instructions. My mind floats back to New York, in June of 1971, when I was working on your quarters making them ready for your divine appearance. Although I can hardly remember things that happened in the years 1974—-1976, when I was intimately connected with you as your secretary, servant, and BBT typist, I can somehow remember very clearly the few occasions in 1971 when I first met you. I remember how it was that you taught me to consider the wishes of the spiritual master first, above all other considerations.

You wanted an almirah, very similar to the ones that are popular in India. You didn’t mind if it was made out of wood or metal, but you wanted it immediately. You had asked the temple president to make this almirah one week before, but he forgot about it and neglected to inform us, the devotees in charge of constructing your quarters. Therefore you were angry and said to him, “I want that almirah by tonight when I take rest. If it is not here by then, I will go out and personally buy one with my own money.”

We—myself and Parīkṣit dāsa, heard your words from the temple president, who pleaded with us, “Somehow or other, make Prabhupāda an almirah by tonight. Please.” Luckily for us, you had made a drawing of exactly what you wanted, so we didn’t have to speculate about what to do. So, drawing in hand, we raced over to our workshop next to ISKCON Press on Tiffany Street and started to dive into the work.

Within a few minutes we realized that we were completely unequipped to do such a job in one day. We had no proper saws or tools for the job. We had some wood, but it somehow didn’t seem to be the correct material for an almirah. Neither of us had ever built a sophisticated piece of furniture before, and therefore within a half hour we were panic-stricken. What were we going to do? And Prabhupāda wanted that almirah before he took rest. That meant around 9:30 p.m. There was less than ten hours left to do this seemingly impossible task.

We were sitting there and lamenting when Advaita dāsa, the devotee in charge of the press, happened upon us and soon found out the cause of our despair. Within a few moments he was in the wood shop of Dave, our next-door neighbor, negotiating with him to help us build this almirah for you as devotional service for the spiritual master of ISKCON. Although Dave was an ordinary *karmī,* he happened to be an awfully nice guy, and he accepted this service as his personal service for your satisfaction. He had all the tools and even the proper board with which to build the almirah. So we started immediately and worked through the day without eating or resting.

We helped Dave by holding the boards and guiding them through the saw, holding the shelves in place while the glue was setting, and in various ways making ourselves useful while he worked quickly and wonderfully to produce an almirah in record time. It was a miracle, but we completed the almirah by 9:00 p.m.

But what to do? The shop was many blocks from the temple and we had only thirty minutes to bring the almirah to Prabhupāda’s room; otherwise he was going to spend his own money to buy a new one. How to get this closet, which was now weighing at least 450 pounds, to Prabhupāda in time?

There were four of us in the shop by the time the almirah was finished; Advaita dāsa, Candanācārya dāsa, Parīkṣit dāsa, and myself. There were no other devotees around. The closet was too heavy for the four of us to carry alone, and Dave was too old to help us. But we had no choice. So, screaming like fanatical banzai warriors, we lifted the almirah on our shoulders and struggled down the stairs into the street. The pain on our shoulders was incredible, and we all cried with the effort. Each devotee swore that he would drop the closet the next moment, but we were all encouraging each other to just go on “for Prabhupāda.” This worked for some time, but when we had gotten two blocks from the bridge, we all spontaneously collapsed and the closet fell to the street with a thud.

It seemed as if we were finished, but after a short rest we somehow or other, entirely by Kṛṣṇa’s will and energy, being empowered by your order, picked that monster closet up and ran it to your doorstep. It was 9:29 exactly. You were in your hallway preparing to go to your bedroom. You saw us, sweating and in pain, yet somehow incredibly blissful at having accomplished this unique mission, and you mercifully allowed us to bring you this offering of love.

We brought it to your bedroom, and you examined it carefully. You showed appreciation for the craftsmanship, and we asked you to bless Dave for this service he had performed when we were helpless and unable to serve you. You acknowledged his service, mentioned that you required a lock on the closet door, and then took rest. Needless to say, we were ecstatic that you had accepted this offering at the very last minute.

The next morning, when you went on your morning walk, I took your newly made almirah and brought it into the temple shoe room next to your room to install the lock and to paint it, for it was still just plain wood. The day before I had bought five-minute quick-drying water-based white paint and had arranged for about five devotees to help me paint the closet while you were out walking. The plan was to have it all painted, two coats, with the new lock, back in your bedroom before you finished your walk. But that day you came back sooner than expected, and while we were in a complete frenzy trying to finish the painting, you suddenly appeared in the shoe room. At first none of the devotees responded to you because we were so absorbed in painting at a furious pace that we didn’t even see you come in. But you walked up to the closet and grabbed the lock, pulled on it and then said, “Good lock,” and then went back to your room. We suddenly paid obeisances, some of us with our heads in the paint that had dripped on the floor.

When the almirah was finally established in your bedroom, you were exceedingly pleased at the effort your disciples had made to serve you according to your order. It was not really a terribly significant thing, for after all it was not something wonderful like going to a completely new country and bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness there for the first time, or distributing 1,000 books in a day, but it was the first real test you gave me, and I understood from this episode that my life and soul was fulfilling your order as you desired. I also realized that there was a certain kind of ecstasy to be found in getting a difficult instruction from the spiritual master and actually fulfilling it. I could understand what it meant to be a disciple.

I have understood I am your slave and you can use me as you like to accomplish whatever you think fitting of me. I am your tool and you may use me to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness wherever you feel it required. I have no independent existence outside of your will, your order, or your desire. Please use me as you like, eternally, for I am yours.

Let me always understand what is your desire and let me fulfill that desire even before you request it of me. I wish to always remember these initial pastimes with you, which, on the surface, are not really anything extraordinary, but which have created a deep and everlasting impression on my heart and mind and have brought me close to your lotus feet.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you for being with us and teaching us how to surrender to you. I bow down to you with great awe and reverence. Please save me.

Your humble servant,  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma, kevala-bhakati-sadma,*  
*bandõ mui sāvadhāna mate*

We must worship the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda with great care, since his lotus feet, manifest as his divine orders, are the abode of pure loving service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. We have no real home in this world but the lotus feet of such a pure devotee.

On the roads in Bengal, the “Caution” signs, in Bengali, say *sāvadhāna,* literally *sa-avadhāna:* “with caution, with attention.” On the road back to Godhead, we must observe all caution and take all care to strictly follow the orders of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*yāṅhāra prasāde bhāi ei bhava tariyā yāi*  
*kṛṣṇa-prāpti haya yāhā hoite*

All anxiety, all troubles are part and parcel of *bhava,* material existence, and we can free ourselves from all such problems by the mercy of Prabhupāda, for by his mercy, we attain Kṛṣṇa Himself.

*guru-mukha-padma-vākya cittete kariyā aikya,*  
*ār nā kariho mane āśā*

We must agree totally with the instruction that emanates from the lotus mouth of Prabhupāda, and we must aspire to nothing more.

*śrī-guru-caraṇe-rati, ei sei uttama-gati*  
*ye prasāde pūre sarva āśā*

The greatest goal in life is to become firmly attached to Prabhupāda’s lotus feet, and by his mercy all our desires will be fulfilled. This is a practical fact, and those who are developing their attachment to Prabhupāda’s feet experience this fully in their lives.

In this way, on the appearance day of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, let us meditate seriously and profoundly on his glories, that simply by unflinching attachment to his lotus feet, the most fallen soul achieves all perfection of life.

His eternal servant,  
Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful and *daṇḍavāt* obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, by whose mercy the hopelessly materially engrossed have been given the priceless jewel of transcendental devotional service. Just as Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda understood the inner desire of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu five centuries ago, Your Divine Grace also understood the desire of the Lord for the modern era.

Your Divine Grace’s unique position in representing the disciplic succession and Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī is revealed confidentially in the following verses of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā* 1.115—117

*rāya, bhaṭṭācārya bale,—”tomara prasāda vine*  
*tomāra hṛdaya ei jānila kemane*

*āmāte sañcāri’ pūrve kahilā siddhānta*  
*ye saba siddhānte brahmā nāhi pāya anta*

*tāte jāni—pūrve tomāra pāñāche prasāda*  
*tāhā vinā nahe tomāra hṛdayānuvāda”*

**After hearing this verse, both Rāmananda Rāya and Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya said to Caitanya Mahāprabhu, “Without Your special mercy, how could this Rūpa Gosvāmī have understood Your mind?” Crīla Rāmānanda Rāya admitted that previously Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu had empowered his heart so that he could express elevated and conclusive statements to which even Lord Brahmā has no access. “Had you not previously bestowed Your mercy on him,” they said, “it would not have been possible for him to express Your internal feelings.”**

Purport: Devotees acknowledge Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s special mercy upon Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī in the following words:

*śrī-caitanya-mano ‘bhīṣṭaṁ-*  
*sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ*  
*dadāti sva-padāntikam*

**“When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?”**

The special function of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī is to establish the feelings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. These feelings are His desires that His special mercy be spread throughout the world in this Kali-yuga.

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi-grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa  4.126\]*

His desire is that all over the world everyone, in every village and every town, know of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His *saṅkīrtana* movement. These are the inner feelings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī committed to writing all these feelings of the Lord. Now again, by the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the same feelings are being spread all over the world by the servants of the Gosvāmīs, and devotees who are pure and simple will appreciate this attempt. As concluded by Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī, however, those who are on the level of hogs and dogs will never appreciate such a great attempt. Yet this does not matter to the preachers of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s cult, for all over the world they will continue to perform this responsible work, even though persons who are like cats and dogs do not appreciate them.

As Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has committed to writing the inner feelings of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Your Divine Grace has practically spread them all over the world. Therefore we proudly address Your Divine Grace as Śrīla Prabhupāda, since you are representing this special quality shown by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. Your Divine Grace is also *śrī-caitanya-mano ‘bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitam* in an extraordinary manner.

We are so fortunate to be allowed to serve Your Divine Grace in this most confidential service of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. How unfortunate are those who miss this opportunity to assist Your Divine Grace in this inner service to Lord Gaurāṅga. Please bless us that we may always remain and may never be separated from Your Divine Grace’s service. May we become transparent instruments in Your Divine Grace’s hands to fulfill your desires, which are the inner feelings of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

I am most unworthy and a most fallen soul; therefore I pray at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet for your causeless mercy again and again.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant eternal servant,  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

**New World**

Who will survive?  
How will they live?  
Who will maintain them?  
Who maintains all eternally?

A plant grows from its roots.  
We have roots.  
Where?  
Find the root and find life.

Prabhupāda offered us life.  
Now we may take  
As much as we can grasp,  
And hold on.

Who will respond to the need?  
Come, play a part.  
Kṛṣṇa’s drama  
Unfolds.

Changes coming.  
Who knows how and when?  
Good for all,  
Mother Earth and her offspring.

Prabhupāda, please engage me,  
Fool that I am.  
Allow me some role  
That I may live.

Here is life.  
New world.  
Spiritual dimension on earth,  
And back to home.

Jagadīśa Goswami

## Madhusevita dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your divine appearance day.

When I sit down to write the Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I realize the degree of my forgetfulness of you. I may preach a lot about your being present in the form of your instructions, but a lousy and selfish being like myself has far too little time and energy to dedicate to the worship of the spiritual master. I dream of a ghostly-haunted-like state in which your presence is an indivisible truth of my life, but then I am a “realist,” a very practical person, when it comes to the illusory demands of my body and mind. You are situated differently. You look at us from the world of pure transcendental emotions, where there is no deceit and each one of your desires is absolutely real by the will of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. To make us perfect devotees, you compassionately infuse those transcendental desires into our hearts in the form of your instructions, but out of sheer laziness I avoid them, choosing mental lucubration. Stubbornness due to pride. This formidable enemy named pride does not let me admit that my spiritual survival rests on your desire to protect me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for even a moment without your care means sure spiritual death, either in the form of materialism or in that of impersonalism. In your presence pride is ashamed. It is actually not difficult to be with you; you are the inspiration in preaching, the carefulness in managing, the strength to resist sense gratification, the fortune in our daily activities, the intelligence in understanding the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the attention in the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* the desire to hear *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the sense of care for the other devotees, and the attachment to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Humility reaches its true meaning in the realization of your constant presence. I missed my appointment with you for so many lifetimes, and now that you have come back to rescue me I am so stupidly careless.

Dear Lord Nityānanda, please bless me with intense longing for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s association. He, the dearmost servant of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, has appeared in this world to relish the nectarean feelings of separation from his beloved Rādhā-Mādhava, and out of his causeless mercy he has distributed his devotional ecstasies to innumerable beings. May he intercede with Śrī Rūpa-Raghunātha for this hopeless case of a grown-proud servant.

This material world is a heart-hardening factory, but your appearance day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, has called for a holiday. So the factory worker Madhusevita dāsa is taking advantage of this holiday to relish the delicate presence of your lotus feet, and his heart is softening. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless me that every day is your appearance day so that I may be forced to give up my job in this factory of birth and death.

Your undeserving servant,  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mukunda Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your feet.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.2.5, purport), you write that one should “learn *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and make an all-around solution to all problems pertaining to social, political or religious matters.”

In another passage (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.14.20, purport), you write that “the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement was started to convince the general populace to adopt the best process by which to satisfy the Supreme Personality of Godhead and thus solve all problems.”

And on a walk in Māyāpur on April 1, 1975, you said, “Now, at least for me, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is no more theoretical. It is practical. I can solve all problems.”

As your movement grows and matures, I increasingly encounter the need to demonstrate how Kṛṣṇa consciousness can not only solve the problems of individuals, but also of society.

Of course we understand that until a *varṇāśrama* society is somewhat established in the world, many if not most of the present problems will persist.

Nevertheless, you have instructed that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not only *jñāna* but *vijñāna,* and that *jñāna* (knowledge) without practical application of that knowledge (*vijñāna*) “is useless.”

Within the modern trend of concern about the earth’s polluted environment, we have discovered that many modern-day “*jñānīs*” in this field are looking to spirituality for answers, realizing that modern society’s ecological crisis stems from uncontrolled greed and that the source of this greed is the precipitous decline of spiritual values.

Society has become utterly confused. Environmentalists espouse Darwinism and “abortion rights”; animal rights defenders support abortion; anti-abortion advocates eat meat; abortion backers champion religious freedom. *Kaler doṣa-nidhe rājan* (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 12.3.51). Despite the immense output of manpower, brainpower, time, and energy, no person, government, or organization has a solution to the pervasive economic, social, and political chaos that permeates almost every society on earth. *Saha-jaṁ karma kaunteya sa-doṣam api na tyajet* (*Bhagavad-gītā* 18.48).

As every form of human activity seems to be careening out of control at an accelerating pace, riding the edge of the precipice, about to plunge into the abyss, it becomes more and more clear to us that your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is this planet’s only hope.

You have charged us with the task of demonstrating in a *convincing* fashion that, improbable as it may seem to the ignorant, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and its realized application can bring order to this chaos, or, as the *Bhāgavatam* itself states, bring about “a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization” (*Bhāg.* (1.5.11).

You made the supreme sacrifice, giving your entire life to Lord Kṛṣṇa and suffering humanity, expecting that we, your spiritual progeny, would continue your unfinished labors imbued with the same spirit of tireless sacrifice that you showed us. You have bequeathed the great task to us, but we sometimes stagger at the thought of carrying on such a demanding legacy.

Sometimes I wish you were personally here with us to answer the many questions, or to solve the myriad problems, that your now many-faceted Society encounters. But I am hopeful that through our collective efforts and our determination to become pure devotees, we, your spiritual heirs, will embrace that spirit of sacrifice by which you lived your life and thus conduct ourselves in such a way that we can rightly be called your followers.

Your eternal servant,  
Mukunda Goswami

## Naveen Kṛṣṇa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As I survey  
The time ahead,  
From now until  
This body’s dead,  
The wisest thing  
I’ll ever do,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Is serving you.

Your life was meant  
For all, I see,  
To turn their pain  
To ecstasy,  
The kindest thing,  
I’ll every do,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Is serving you.

Lord Kṛṣṇa’s books  
On every shelf,  
The task you took  
Upon yourself,  
A deed more humble  
I’ll never do,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Than helping you.

Throughout your life  
so much duress,  
Ev’ry failure  
A pillar of success.  
No more courageous  
Act I’ll do,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Than serving you.

Disciplic line  
Coming through you  
Connects one with  
Lord Kṛṣṇa, too.  
No more fulfilling  
Act I’ll do,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Than pleasing you.

A dusty mirror,  
If cleansed by day,  
Reflects the evening’s  
Full-moon rays.  
The greatest thing  
I’ll ever do,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Is loving you.

Your servant,  
Naveen Kṛṣṇa dāsa

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī has stated:

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye ’nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

**“Kirāta, Hūṇa, Andhra, Pulinda, Pulkaśa, Ābhira, Śumbha, Yavana, members of the Khasa races and even others addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, for He is the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.4.18).**

The purport to this verse is still being written, and somehow, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are allowing me to bear witness. I’ve been watching you closely these past few years while you’ve been traveling throughout the former USSR, expanding Lord Kṛṣṇa’s glories into every town and village of this spiritually forsaken place from the past. I’m watching—how you’re changing people’s hearts, how you’re kindling new sparks of hope, and how you’re piercing through the numerous cultural barriers that laid dormant here for so long. One after one, you’ve been cutting through these fortified coverings, with the sole purpose of extracting the essence that lies within every living being—*bhakti*—or devotion to the lotus feet of your worshipful Lord. Your potency to transform these people’s lives is overwhelming, and oftentimes I find my bodily movements temporarily suspended while I, the observer within, sit back and watch your transcendental *līlā* unfold before me.

What kind of pious activities must I have performed to have been given the opportunity to be watching this now? But beyond that, what did I do to have been given the eyes to actually “see” you preaching like this? My understanding is that these are your pastimes, and just as the pastimes of the Lord can be seen only by those who have been given the eyes by which they can actually see, the pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotees are of the same transcendental nature. It’s undeniable. Somehow I’ve been able to see this—but, to me, it seems that it’s without any previous qualification. This could only be due only to your unlimited and causeless mercy. What else could it be?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books (or, identically—“YOU”) are igniting a spiritual revolution here. These people have certainly known about revolutions before, but this one, as we know, is of a much different nature. “On the other hand, that literature which is full of descriptions of the transcendental glories of the name, fame, forms, pastimes, etc., of the unlimited Supreme Lord is a different creation, full of transcendental words directed toward bringing about a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization. Such transcendental literatures, even though imperfectly composed, are heard, sung, and accepted by purified men who are thoroughly honest” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.11). Nārada Muni talked about this revolution, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the commander-in-chief. You are leading the battle, standing right in the forefront, and, armed with the sword of transcendental knowledge, you are releasing one by one those who’ve been held captive, birth after birth, under the tyrants of lust, envy, and greed. You are teaching by your bold example that we too must be bold if we want to win this war against *Māyā*. There’s no doubt that this revolution has started, and it’s already foretold that it will change the course of human events for the next ten thousand years. Thus we find still another verse with its purport unfolding!

For those who may challenge my statements by asking for the evidence to support them, we know that Gopinātha Ācārya dealt with the disciples of Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya by telling them that the statements of the authorized ā*cāryas* are themselves the real proof. Thus we have also presented such statements here as our evidence. But if they want more, then let this also be considered as evidence—a letter written by a seventeen-year-old boy from Byelorussia:

“Dear publishers,  
“I was told that you stopped printing the books that give the people an understanding of another, invisible world, an understanding we need so much in this world of relativity.  
“I am living in a village. My elder brother has a kind of mental disease. I have also a mother, younger brother (10 years old) and a younger sister (3.5 years old). My mother is ill, and she is working like anything to feed her children. I am working as a locksmith in the fur workshop, and I don’t have any possibility to buy your books or to have a teacher or master in this science. I can hardly leave my village.  
“I am writing to you so that you can understand that there are a lot of people like me in this country. I am not begging for alms. No, other people also don’t need any financial support. That is nonsense, a waste of money, which doesn’t bring profit to mankind. Why are people asking for financial assistance? Because they are spiritually poor. They don’t have any spiritual support.  
“Actually, nobody needs books on the culture and art of India. Who needs their culture? What’s that for? But the books you have been printing instruct the people about a bona fide path, better than it is done by other religions.  
“Recently I tried to order these books on Vedic science, and I came to know that they are not in print anymore. I cannot borrow books like *Bhagavad-gita* and The Science of Self-Realization and copy them. I don’t have enough time and strength for that. I don’t have a phenomenal memory either, so I can’t learn them by heart.  
“Dear publishers, I am appealing to you. The spiritual condition of our country is very low, so I beg you: Please don’t stop printing such books, because they give light to the people, and most people will agree with that.”

And here is another letter from a woman in Siberia:

“I live in a remote place of the country, but God didn’t forget this place because your books were distributed here. I’ve read *Srimad-Bhagavatam* and it was like a shock for me. I was overwhelmed with joy. I got both knowledge and spiritual satisfaction, and it was wonderful. But very soon I realized that people didn’t understand me. And now I’m practically alone. I’ve bought all the books. In my opinion, among all these books *Srimad-Bhagavatam* is the best. I’ve started to follow regulative principles, and everybody is wondering how is it possible not to eat sausage and drink alcohol. I’m sadly smiling at this instead of giving them an answer. Please send me the address of at least one person, I mean the master of Krsna consciousness. Also, I would like to express my heartfelt gratitude to you because you give out the most confidential of knowledge—the knowledge about God and His love.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, these letters and hundreds more like them stand as a true testimony that you are still actively preaching, and we are assisting. We have no other purpose in life than to remain your eternal assistants, birth after birth, as you continue to write your fresh purports for us to relish, translate, print, and distribute.

I pray to remain  
Your eternal servant,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances,  
At your holy lotus feet,  
They are our only shelter,  
So soft and pure and sweet.

By your transcendental mercy,  
You came to save us all,  
Who were rotting in a hellish life,  
At Māyā’s beck and call.

“Just surrender to Lord Kṛṣṇa  
And chant His holy name,”  
Direct from Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma  
As ambassador you came.

Your instructions were so simple,  
Yet we’re so harassed by lust,  
That despite your good intention,  
Many fell and bit the dust.

To maintain the spiritual platform  
Means to regularly chant  
The holy name attentively,  
And in the *kīrtans* dance;

To hear the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*  
And other books you gave;  
To constantly associate  
With devotees, meek and grave;

To worship Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa  
In His *arcā-mūrti* form,  
Residing at a holy place  
Like a temple or a *dhām*.

These principles of *bhakti*  
Are the pillars of ISKCON,  
And if they’re followed strictly  
They can save us from all harm.

So although it’s Kali-yuga  
And the world is steeped in sin,  
One who follows your instructions  
Is surely bound to win

The battle against Māyā  
And the senses and the mind.  
He’s sure to be enlightened  
And leave ignorance behind.

We must recognize our fortune,  
That we got this gift from you,  
And share it with all others  
For their upliftment too.

Thus just as Lord Caitanya  
Told Kūrma and Vāsudev,  
“Tell all about Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
This country you must save.”

We mustn’t be complacent  
But determined, brave, and bold,  
To go and preach your message  
And save the fallen souls.

“I live within my books,” you said,  
“I’m never going to die.  
If you publish and distribute them,  
You’ll go to the spiritual sky.”

This is the *ācāryas’* mission,  
And that is why you wrote  
Such transcendental literatures  
About what Kṛṣṇa spoke.

It’s the duty of your disciples,  
And granddisciples too,  
To increase book distribution  
As their service unto you.

So I’m praying for your mercy  
On this holiest of days,  
To get the strength from Kṛṣṇa  
To serve you in this way.

And I’m hoping that you will forgive  
The offenses that I’ve made  
To you and to your servants,  
So that I can be saved

From falling any lower  
Than I already am.  
O Prabhupāda! Just one more chance,  
I’ll do the best I can.

Your insignificant servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Rohiṇī-sūta dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

In one of your last instructions you said, “There is nothing new to be said. Everything is in my books. Therefore, if you want to know me, read my books.” This amazing statement shows us the importance of your books, because you were actually sent by Kṛṣṇa to write these books. As you said, “I didn’t write these books; they were dictated to me by Kṛṣṇa.” Therefore you’ve even read your own books. There is so much nectar in your books. The more I read them, the more I can realize that I have to learn so much about this Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy; it is unlimited, always fresh, and enlivening. What would we know without your books? You wrote the books for us so that we can read and study them very carefully, at least two hours every day. They are the lawbooks for the next ten thousand years. Therefore you gave instructions for the whole human society so that everyone can become happy by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 3.190), you give a wonderful guideline for the organization and expansion of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. So many people are getting your books and becoming interested in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and it’s becoming more and more difficult to know how to organize and engage them all. Especially here in Russia we can see that so many people started to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness at home simply by receiving some of your books. There are thousands of people writing letters, wanting to know what more they can do for this movement, or how to find a *guru*. Some of them are even ready to distribute your books. And the temples are full of devotees. Even the temple room has to be used for sleeping. The temples are always too small. But in your purport, you revealed the solution:

“The cult of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, is very nicely explained by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu authoritatively. It is not that everyone has to take *sannyāsa* like Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Everyone can execute the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness at home, as ordered by the Lord. Everyone can congregationally chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. One can also discuss the subject matter of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and install Deities of Rṁdhṁ-Kṛṣṇa or Gaura-Nitāi or both and worship Them very carefully in one’s own home. It is not that we have to open different centers all over the world. Whoever cares for the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement can install Deities at home and, under superior guidance, worship the Deity regularly, chanting the *mahā-mantra* and discussing *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. We are actually teaching in our classes how to go about this.
