One who feels that he is not yet ready to live in the temple or undergo strict regulative principles in the temple—especially householders who live with wife and children—can start a center at home by installing the Deity, worshiping the Lord morning and evening, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and discussing *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* Anyone can do this at home without difficulty, and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu requested all the devotees present there to do so” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 3.190).

This is such a nice program. And everything is based on your books. They are seeds to establish practically a temple in every house.

Now we, your followers, have to become first class devotees so that we can guide and educate these people. You once wrote in a letter that the temples are not for eating and sleeping but to train the devotees to become expert in preaching and distributing your books. In order to guide others and to preach we have to be convinced of this Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy, as you have shown us by your own example. Therefore on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā I pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I can become more sincere and determined to follow your instructions. And please bless all the members of your ISKCON to preach the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in every town and village. Only by your and Lord Caitanya’s mercy will the whole world be overflooded with this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. And then all the suffering living entities will float and drown in the nectar ocean of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi’s mercy, and they will attain pure love of Rṁdhṁ and Kṛṣṇa.

Your insignificant servant,  
Rohiṇī-sūta dāsa

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

As I write this year’s offering, an unexpected event coincides with its composition. It is the recent demise of my father, a good friend and well-wisher. The realizations evoked by the force of such circumstances supersede my planned composition. Therefore I sit and write briefly, narrating the history of events.

Death comes to everyone. You taught us that by your words, in your books, and finally by your personal example. My father had been ailing for three years. While in India, I had phoned my mother regularly to keep in touch. All things seemed to be going well.

At the end of March, as I had begun to settle in for a month of intensive *sādhana,* I received an unexpected call.

“I think you should come immediately if you want to see your father alive. He is waiting for you.”

No need to give the medical history. Suffice it to say that he was in hospital and not doing well.

Some events obviously transpire by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s will. Vṛndavāna to Montreal in thirty-six hours, without a ticket or reservation, was only possible through divine intervention.

Arriving in the early afternoon, in three feet of snow, we drove straight to the hospital. As I entered room 719 my mother was sitting by the bedside looking tired, but relieved. She woke him as I entered. “Peter is here.”

My father smiled and leaned forward. “Oh, you came too, all the way from India.” We talked very briefly. He mentioned that he was embarrassed. All strength was gone. He was not able to even eat on his own. “Things are becoming hazy. I have difficulty thinking straight.”

It was our last conversation. When I returned the next morning he was no longer conscious. There were some decisions to be made. Should we try some special treatment? No! Better let things take a natural course.

After some hours a nice, soft-spoken doctor met with us. He was honest and straightforward. “Actually there is nothing more we can do for him now.” It was obvious. There was nothing left to do. It was a matter of waiting for him to die.

He was an honest and dignified man. I do not want to narrate every detail of his last moments. It was a semi-private room. I sat beside him softly chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, occasionally speaking, but receiving no outward response. The hours melted into the afternoon. At first he was convulsive and uncomfortable. Then he became peaceful, and gradually his breathing slowed, like a metronome just about to stop, a clock that had run down.

Then his tongue protruded and his life air left his body. He died.

It was the first time I had witnessed someone leaving this world. My mother watched, resigned to the inevitable. I placed a Tulasī leaf in his mouth, a *mahā* garland around his neck, sprinkled the body with Ganges water, and offered some prayers for the departed soul.

That person I had known in his youth, associated with in his middle age, and lately watched struggling under the burden of old age, was no more. My mother asked, unprompted, “Where has his soul gone now?”

In the days to follow I had to manage with family grief and making the last arrangements. A year ago we had sat in the backyard, all three of us. My parents requested they be cremated at death, and their ashes taken to the Ganges.

“Where did you get such an idea from?” I had asked in amazement.

At the ceremony I spoke as per a previous arrangement. My mother had worried. “Don’t proselytize.” I didn’t. I explained the science of the soul and spoke to them about a perspective that I alone had known in my father. The spiritualist. That person who first asked me decades ago … “Do you ever think of God? ...You should. After all, we are all in His debt.” … And who at our parting last year, the last time we spoke together, said in private: “I am very happy with what you are doing. Now stay with it. Don’t leave Kṛṣṇa.”

At the gate we bid the guests good-bye.

“Thank you for that lovely ceremony,” someone said.

I left as efficiently as I had come. My father’s ashes as my only extra luggage. Having arrived back in Vṛndavāna, I spoke to my mother over the phone. She was in grief.

“Don’t you feel sad, now that he is gone?” she asked.

Having consigned his ashes to the Yamunā, my last obligation was complete. The story ends there. I am writing it as it happened.

It is my twentieth year in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It was to be a special year I had earmarked a long time ago. A generation, by the calculation of men. I had high expectations. Someone had told me the day I crossed the threshold to the temple that I would be a pure devotee by now. Somehow that was not to be. As a result, I was lamenting throughout the year. An existential crisis. “What has come of me in this time?”

My only awareness of pure devotion has been to recognize its rareness. My knowledge of Kṛṣṇa has revealed His elusiveness as a consequence of my reluctance to surrender.

But in this episode, at the eleventh hour of my second decade, well into middle age, you have given me another assurance in your usual pragmatic way.

Somehow, without my noticing, you have softened my heart, enough to care about an old man in his final days. I answered my mother, “Yes, I want to see him. I want to help him.”

Somehow, although penniless, I had acquired a priceless gift by which to repay a lifetime of kindness. “Yes, I came,” I told him in the hospital; “despite the odds, I came to be with you. Now just think of God.”

Somehow, to the amazement of others, you have taught me to be calm, free of illusion, in this ever-changing world, while all learned and affluent persons around me were bewildered.

Somehow you arranged for me to be the witness. It was true! That first teaching you had repeated to the very last day. I could see it. When the soul departed, what was left was no longer my father. I could see! I chanted to myself in amazement, *tathā dehāntara-prāptir …*

Somehow, because we follow your teachings, the greatest pessimists accept our authority. I answered my mother, “He was a good person, so he will go to a higher destination, closer to God.” She was pacified.

Somehow, your books sat on the basement shelf for eighteen years, unopened. Then when it was time to inquire about death, they were read. My mother replied, “It says so in those books you gave us.”

Somehow you have invested your potency in those who repeat your words. The most reserved audience I had ever spoken to, and they went away in appreciative disbelief. It was the Truth that had touched their hearts.

Somehow I replied.

“Yes I feel sad.”

“Then what do you do?” she asked.

“I have been trained for this for twenty years. How to understand the impermanence of matter and the eternality of the soul.”

A pause. “Yes, that’s right. And after all, what else counts,” she replied.

Somehow, that conversation was it, in a nutshell. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have so expertly opened my eyes with transcendental knowledge. Gradually I am beginning to see.

Somehow I have been waiting here, such a long time, hoping for oceans of nectar. I had great expectations. But first you opened my eyes, then you taught me to see, and now you are making me into a person. It was not what I had in mind. But you know best. “Be conscious, then Kṛṣṇa conscious,” you had said.

Somehow, no doubt, I will continue to hope, by following that line of training that you have left us, perhaps after the next twenty years, I will become the pure devotee, that I once came to you to be.

Somehow, once again I am here, prostrating at your lotus feet, to thank you for your mercy, from the core of my shallow heart.

Your insignificant servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The back of the young punk rocker’s head is tattooed: NO MORE PAIN. In fat bold letters his message tells the story of a generation in the New World Order (or dis-Order). The boy is fat. Health is not his problem. His mind is sick.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to us from a long way away. A distance of light years, more than I can count. How far away are Krsna’s lotus feet anyway? I cannot fathom the depth of your compassion. O Sāragrāhi Vaiṣṇava, please take away the pain. I think we're all saying that.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how little I know of your personality. Only impressions of those years when you were with us and we were young. Soft, gentle, pure, all-knowing, wise, steady, fearless, all-powerful—only impressions of you. If knowledge is power, then only you have the power to take away my pain because your knowledge is awesome.

That punk rocker wants to be a devotee. He’s afraid … To lose his pain he might have to leave aside his tattoo.

Should I preach to him, Śrīla Prabhupāda? How can I proceed when my tattoo is still there? It’s only faith in you, your word, your gift of devotional service, which keeps me going. Now I remember Jayānanda in San Francisco. “You just have to have faith in the name.” He always said it.

I don't have much faith in the name (no taste), so I just hang on to my faith in you.

Your servant,  
Śrīdhara Swami

## Suhotra Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Falling again and again like a stick at your lotus feet, I humbly beg another chance to render some insignificant menial service to Your Divine Grace. I am low. I am ignorant. I am offense personified. Gnashing my teeth and pounding my forehead in the dust sanctified by your holy footprints, I contemplate my inveterate sinfulness. All hopes of liberation fly away in the ten directions. My only hope is your tender compassion for fallen souls such as me. In a lecture, you made the following remarks about this wonderful quality of a pure devotee:

“That is the attitude of devotee … ‘My dear Lord, You have kindly come to deliver the fallen souls. Please fulfill Your mission. Take away all the souls, conditioned souls of the universe. Don’t leave them, not a single. Please take them away. And if You think that they are not eligible or some of them are not eligible, then please transfer their sinful reaction upon me. I shall go on suffering. But You take away all of them.’ Just see the attitude of a devotee. ‘You take them all.”’

These words so beautifully express an attribute that is seen in no one except that personage who is untainted by fear, pride, and envy, who is liberated by dint of his very unconcern for liberation, who lives only to love and serve Kṛṣṇa and His parts and parcels. How mind-bogglingly fortunate to have been born on this planet during the span of your pastimes here! That I saw you, heard you, and served you personally is a benison that, for me to even begin to value in proportion to its worth, will require me to take countless more births in this material world—for I am sorry to admit that in this life I am too gross and clumsy to receive what you have given without stumbling. I pray only that each of those births be opportunities to render more and more service to Your Divine Grace.

Alas, even in this attempt to express my unworthiness, my stubborn vanity is revealed! “I’m so fallen that I’ll have to take countless more births.” If it is your desire that the fallen souls be delivered, then who am I to say I’ll remain for countless births? May I never forget the lesson in genuine submissiveness before the spiritual master that Your Divine Grace taught me the day after you initiated me into the chanting of the holy names. I was among some hundred and twenty devotees who had come to the airport to see you off to New York. We sat on the floor chanting *japa* before you as you gracefully adorned a seat in the departure lounge. Suddenly, you looked right at me and said, “Oh, you are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa very nicely!” Mortified, my eyes dropped to the floor and I thought (not said, but only thought), “No, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am not chanting nicely.” Beaming like the sun, you lifted your finger skyward like a flag of victory and corrected my inward negativity: “Yes!”

Yes. What is the use of my counting up my next births? There is nothing but service to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. What is the use of wondering where my *karma* may take me in my next life? There is nothing but service to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. What is even the use of counting up my blessings? There is nothing but service to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. To hell with my nagging self-centeredness! It is that service to Your Divine Grace alone that can make “a dumb man speak, a lame man cross the mountains, and a blind man see the stars.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. May I be able to say to you these four words one day and really understand their meaning.

Your servant,  
Suhotra Swami

## Tamāl Krishna Goswami

**Prabhupāda’s Preaching Still**

“Prabhupāda’s preaching still!” I insisted. But my disillusioned Godbrother wasn’t convinced.

“Look at the shape the world’s in,” he argued. “And ISKCON’s not much better off.”

I prayed to Prabhupāda and immediately his answer came through my lips: “‘If all my disciples will fully cooperate with me, then we can take over the whole world in eighteen days.’“ That made him thoughtful. He knew it was Prabhupāda speaking.

I took advantage of his silence and said, “How many of us actually believe this?” His face was downcast. “Well, there are some who’ve never doubted. For them, Prabhupāda has never left. Do you know who I’m talking about?” I wanted him to remember the old days. “That’s right. The *saṅkīrtana* devotees, the book distributors on the front lines. They’re risking their lives while we sit comfortably at home.”

My words sank in. After all, he was an old *saṅkīrtana* hero himself. He knew the score. But fifteen years in retirement had weakened him. “It’s different now that Prabhupāda’s gone.”

“Gone? Prabhupāda’s not gone. How do you think our book distributors are still going out? You think that Prabhupāda’s not still leading them?” I could see in his stare that that’s exactly what he was thinking. “Look, I’ll prove it to you. I’ll show you that Prabhupāda is preaching still. Just like he did before, only now it’s through his books. He’s going everywhere, all over the world and is giving them the sauce, just like he always did.” My Godbrother’s ears perked up.

“How do you think communism collapsed? For years Prabhupāda has been pointing out the defect in Marxist philosophy, how its apparent idealism was flawed by Godlessness. Śrīla Prabhupāda has been traveling through his books throughout Eastern Europe, preaching *bhāgavata-dharma* as the ideal form of communism. Tens and thousands have heard him say:

The only possible remedy that can counteract the tendency toward communism is the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, which can give even communists the real communist society. According to the doctrine of communism, the state should be the proprietor of everything. But the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, expanding this same idea, accepts God as the proprietor of everything. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Ā*di* 8.20, purport\]

In response, they’ve gone wild, chanting congregationally in *nāmābhāsa* Lord Gaurāṅga’s glories. “Gorby! Gorby!” they chanted. And Lord Caitanya, their real benefactor, has understood that it was Him they were actually calling to become their leader.

“Remember Desert Storm in Iraq? In a courageous display of nonpartisan concern, Śrīla Prabhupāda shuttled between Washington and Baghdad, trying to convince former President Bush and Saddam Hussein that they were both on the wrong side. But when all diplomacy failed, then Prabhupāda fired this ‘smart bomb’:

Since the beginning of creation, the demons and the demigods, or the Vaiṣṇava, are always the two classes of living beings to dominate the planets of the universes. The floating of the plants in the weightless air is due to the inner constitution of the globes, and the modernized drilling of the earth to exploit oil from within is a sort of disturbance by the modern demons and can result in a greatly harmful reaction to the floating condition of the earth. A similar disturbance was created formerly by the demons headed by Hiraṇyākṣa (the great exploiter of the gold rush), and the earth was detached from its weightless condition and fell down into the Garbhodaka Ocean. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.7.1, purport\]

“AIDS is a big problem now, isn’t it? Well, although Magic Johnson was famous for slipping through the defenses of opposing basketball teams, he wasn’t able to evade the biggest opponent—Killer AIDS. Neither could millions of others. But it wasn’t a surprise to Śrīla Prabhupāda, nor should it have been to anyone else. For years he’s been warning them of the consequences their illicit habits will bring. While billions of dollars are being spent researching a cure, Prabhupāda keeps issuing the following warning and prescribing the preventative medicine of Kṛṣṇa’s name:

*Dharmāviruddho bhūteṣu kamo ’smi bharatarṣabha.* Sex is allowed only for the begetting of children, not for enjoyment. One can indulge in sex to beget a good child for the benefit of the family, society and world. Otherwise, sex is against the rules and regulations of religious life. A materialistic person does not believe that everything is managed in nature, and he does not know that if one does something wrong, he is witnessed by different demigods. A person enjoys illicit sex, and due to his blind, lusty desire, he thinks that no one can see him, but this illicit sex is thoroughly observed by the agents of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Therefore the person is punished in so many ways. … Those who indulge in it are punished life after life. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.14.9, purport\]

“Abortion was next on Prabhupāda’s ‘hit list.’ Pro-life advocates suffered defeat because they failed to fully avail themselves of Prabhupāda’s irrefutable arguments. Still, His Divine Grace refused to allow the pro-choicers to enjoy their ‘victory.’ He cut their party short with this sobering message:

The Supreme Personality of Godhead does not allow anyone to act against the stringent laws of material nature; therefore illicit sex is punished life after life. Illicit sex creates pregnancies, and these unwanted pregnancies lead to abortion. Those involved become implicated in these sins, so much so that they are punished in the same way in the next life. Thus in the next life they also enter the womb of a mother and are killed in the same way. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.14.9, purport\]

“Prabhupāda is preaching more vigorously then ever before. You know the problems the British royalty have been having? Queen Elizabeth said it was a year she preferred forgetting. No wonder. The British ‘commoners’ have had a lot more to fume about than the fire at Windsor Castle. But the royal family’s sordid dealings shouldn’t have surprised anyone, since Prabhupāda publicly declared:

Why this rascal, falling-down nation, British, they’re objecting \[to Rathayātrā\]? *Apat-kāle viparita-buddhi.* They are falling down, they will commit offense and discrepancies more and more so that they will be nowhere. I think after this Queen, this monarchy there, the so-called monarchy will be also finished. Because her son, her husband, both of them are hippies. The Queen’s husband and Queen’s son, the Prince of Wales, both of them are hippies. So this monarchy will be also finished. \[Morning walk, July 13, 1974, L.A.\]

“Down in Africa, where Somalians are starving to death, if warring tribes don’t kill them first, Śrīla Prabhupāda in the form of his books appeared as their Great Savior, declaring ‘In God’s kingdom there is neither scarcity nor want. Don’t blame God for the shortages.’ While Prabhupāda mass distributed *prasādam* to relieve their burning hunger, he told them who the real culprits were:

Presently there are many bogus institutions which are collecting funds from the public for the purpose of giving food to starving people, but these funds are invariably misused. According to Vedic instructions, the government should arrange things in such a way that there will be no question of starvation. In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* it is stated that a householder must see to it that even a lizard or a snake does not starve. They also must be given food. In actuality, however, there is no question of starvation because everything is the property of the Supreme Lord, and He sees to it that there is ample arrangement for feeding everyone. In the *Vedas* (*Kaṭha* Up. 2.2.13) it is said: *eko bahūnāṁ yo vidadhāti kāmān.* The Supreme Lord supplies the necessities of life to everyone, and there is no question of starvation. If anyone starves, it is due to the mismanagement of the so-called ruler, governor or president. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.22.45, purport\]

“Back in the ‘land of plenty,’ where everyone’s created equal, Americans reeled in the aftermath of the Rodney King beating. Prabhupāda sent a battalion of book distributors into riot-torn L.A. to deliver the following message:

In your country the black and white, they got equal rights, but why they fight something? Racial fight? Because it is on the material platform. So our point is that you come to the spiritual platform. Then this equality will be possible. If you keep yourself on the material platform, then artificially you may say, “We are equal,” but at last we shall fight. This is our proposition. Therefore we request everyone that you come to the spiritual platform. Then everything will be very nice. There will be no distinction because *brahma-bhūtaḥ prasannātmā.* When one is spiritually realized, then he becomes happy. \[Lecture, July 11, 1975, Phila.\]

“Americans are ready for a change and hoped the recent presidential elections would be the answer. As always, Śrīla Prabhupāda was present to sober them up with the truth concerning their ‘demon-crazy’ (demo-cracy) ideas. If voters faced a tough choice, they had no one else but themselves to blame:

In the Age of Kali in most cases rulers are impious, and therefore the citizens are also continuously unhappy. But in the case of democracy, the impious citizens themselves elect their representative to rule over them, and therefore they cannot blame anyone for their unhappiness. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.8.32, purport\]

“Our spiritual master is literally everywhere, in every major skirmish. In Bosnia, where Serbs, Croations, and Muslims are butchering one another in the name of ‘ethnic cleansing,’ Prabhupāda took great risk to personally go to Sebrinica to deliver the following message:

Anyone who is under the bodily conception of life, either human beings or dogs, they are the same. But that *upādhi* amongst human beings is stronger than the cats and dogs. The human being, being advanced in consciousness, they are making this nationalism. But it is nothing but dogism. Is it not? That’s all. The so-called national people are sacrificing so many lives, so many politicians, and these big, big leaders. But what is their business? Business is that doggish mentality: “I am this body.” So it is very difficult to give up this doggish mentality that “I am this body.” Very, very difficult. \[Morning walk, November 12, 1975, Bombay\]

“And finally, in India, Śrīla Prabhupāda has been a lone voice of sanity. While Hindus raised the flag of Hanumān in triumph, shouting for Rām Rāj, Prabhupāda calmly questioned, ‘But where is Rāma?’ Certainly not heading a government that invites McDonalds to franchise their restaurants all over India. When asked why he wouldn’t give his unqualified support to establishing Rām Rāj, Prabhupāda told them:

Lord Rāmacandra’s kingdom is still existing, and recently there was a political party in India named the Rāmarājya party, which wanted to establish a kingdom of Rāma. Unfortunately, modern politicians want the kingdom of Rāma without Rāma Himself. Although they have banished the idea of God consciousness, they still expect to establish the kingdom of Rāma. Such a proposal is rejected by devotees. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.22.63, purport\]

“Do you need any more proof?” I asked my Godbrother. He was pretty excited by now.

“You’ve almost convinced me,” he said with a big smile. “But there’s still a problem. How do I get involved again? I mean, okay. I agree that Prabhupāda’s in his books. But I’m not the same guy I used to be. I’m probably old enough to be the father of most of these book distributors. I’m not sure I could handle having them give me orders. You know what I mean?” He paused a moment to see my reaction. “Now, if *you* were to get into it again, like in the old days. If *you* would get back into your old mood. If *you* become my *saṅkīrtana* leader, well that’s a whole different story. Then I could see myself fitting in.”

Now it was I that was silent, looking down and shuffling my feet.

“C’mon!” he preached. “Actions speak louder than words.”

“But I’ve got so many other responsibilities,” I protested. “I’ve already made my plans for the year.”

“Then how can you demand that I make a change.” It was he that had the upper hand now.

I knew he had me on the run. Here I was, Mr. GBC, Mr. Ex-saṅkīrtana leader, etc., etc., about to be exposed as Mr. Big Bluff. If I wasn’t willing to make some sacrifice, my Godbrother’s faith would vanish as fast as it had become inspired. I prayed to Prabhupāda to give me the intelligence and strength to do what was right. When I spoke, both of us knew it was Prabhupāda who answered: “All right. I surrender. I’ll go back to America and try to do it again. But I won’t be able to do it the same way exactly. I mean, not with the same passion like, ‘I’m the doer,” ‘I’m Number 1.’ It’ll have to be Prabhupāda acting through me and Prabhupāda getting the credit. I’m not the same person I once was. But I’ll give it my best for Prabhupāda, and for you, and all the other devotees who want to see book distribution as big in America as it was before. It’s possible, I’m sure, if we have full faith in Prabhupāda’s desire. Because one thing’s for certain, Prabhupāda’s preaching still.”

Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Vīrabāhu dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories unto you, my only shelter, lord and master, the reservoir from which I get my spiritual and material nourishment.

On this glorious occasion my prayer is that I may always be able to express my love for you in a tangible way. I generally think that I have so many feelings for you, but I have learnt from your life’s example that such feelings should also have a visible product. Otherwise what is the proof that it isn’t only sentimentality, that it isn’t just “a ceremony of fruits and flowers”?

Your love for your Guru Mahārāja took the form and shape of a gigantic worldwide society of Vaiṣṇavas—ISKCON. You are spreading his name and fame all over the world, now and for all time to come. You are giving Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the whole *sampradāya* a most respectable place in all the histories of the world. Thus your love for Kṛṣṇa and for humankind has crystallized far beyond just sentiments or words.

As much as it can be compared, in my Godbrothers and -sisters and in your many followers I see a similar example. Their love for you is always manifesting in something substantial. Innumerable temples opened and Deities established; uninterrupted production of your absolute books and incalculable numbers of them distributed; thousands and thousands of lost souls becoming sane and devoted followers of yours. It is all going on by their true sacrifice. How to join in this grandiose homage? In your words we will always find all the answers.

“Brahmā, the original living being within the material world, taught us the way of sacrifice. The word ‘sacrifice’ suggests dedication of one’s own interests for the satisfaction of a second person. That is the way of all activities. Every man is engaged in sacrificing his interests for others, either in the form of family, society, community, country, or the entire human society. But perfection of such sacrifices is attained when they are performed for the sake of the Supreme Person, the Lord. Because the Lord is the proprietor of everything, because the Lord is the friend of all living creatures, and because He is the maintainer of the performer of sacrifice, as well as the supplier of the ingredients of sacrifices, it is He only and no one else who should be satisfied by all sacrifices.  
“The whole world is engaged in sacrificing energy for advancement of learning, social upliftment, economic development and plans for total improvement of the human condition, but no one is interested in sacrificing for the sake of the Lord, as it is advised in the *Bhagavad-gītā*. Therefore, there is no peace in the world. If men at all want peace in the world, they must practice sacrifice in the interest of the supreme proprietor and friend of all.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.6.28, purport)

You have described this as “the peace formula” for the world, and by applying this purport with you as the center, we get the peace formula for our movement and for each one of us. My prayer today is, therefore, that I may be able to do what you just described—to make my life a continuous sacrifice of my own interests for your satisfaction. This is my true interest, as I have come to know by your mercy. And true sacrifice is to follow your order in all circumstances of happiness and distress.

“In executing penance, one must be determined to return home, back to Godhead, and must decide to undergo all types of tribulations for that end. Even for material prosperity, name and fame, one has to undergo severe types of penance; otherwise no one can become an important figure in this material world. Why, then, are there severe types of penance for the perfection of devotional service? An easygoing life and attainment of perfection in transcendental realization cannot go together. The Lord is more clever than any living entity; therefore He wants to see how painstaking the devotee is in devotional service. The order is received from the Lord, either directly or through the bona fide spiritual master, and to execute that order, however painstaking, is the severe type of penance. One who follows the principle rigidly is sure to achieve success in attaining the Lord’s mercy” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.9.24, purport).

Please give me the determination to always be engaged in executing your order, even if sometimes it appears as a severe type of penance. My only desire is to become your instrument in the service of your mission and thus offer my love to you as something tangible.

All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the perfect example of sacrificing one’s life for the service and satisfaction of Guru and Kṛṣṇa.

Your lowly servant,  
Vīrabāhu dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti-bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Last year as I was leaving India to head west for summer preaching, I was required to go to the income tax office in Delhi for my tax clearance. One nice young clerk, about twenty-five years of age, cordially greeted me and quickly and thoroughly processed all my papers—an amazing feat in light of the usual Indian bureaucratic quagmire. As I thanked him and rose up to leave, he leaned over his desk and softly said, “I need to speak to you for a minute.” As we sat down he opened his office desk and lifted out a copy of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. He held it to his chest and told me that he lives only to read your *Gītā* with its brilliant commentary. After some minutes of enlivening preaching he leaned very close and said, “If I write Swami Prabhupāda a letter, will he write me back?” I swallowed deeply and told him I was sorry to inform him that you had finished your earthly *līlā* in 1977. He quickly sat down, his face filled with emotion, his eyes filled with tears.

It was the Prayag Kumbha-mela, 1989. Huge *harināmas,* book distribution. One morning a *sādhu* approached me. He was typical looking—long matted hair, beard, *kaupina,* ashes, *rudrākṣa* beads. I was very busy and tried to avoid him, but someone had told him I was one of the camp organizers. He came directly to me and said, “Please give me a book.” To avoid becoming entangled, I signed a note instructing the book *godown* to give him a small two-rupee book. Five minutes later he was back. He looked me in the eye and in fluent English said, “No, not this small book. I want Swami Prabhupāda’ṣ *Gītā*.” He continued to say, “It is the most brilliant commentary written on *Gītā* in this modern age.” Immediately, considering that he might be Nārada or Brahmā, I took him by the hand to the nearest bookstall, reached in my pocket, took out fifteen rupees, and presented him with a *Gītā*. Tears came to his eyes. He said, “I’ve seen and heard everything in this *mela*. No one can match what Swami Prabhupāda has done and inspired your people to do. No one shall ever match his efforts to give Kṛṣṇa *prema* to the world.”

By your grace, this year in Māyāpur I was awarded *sannyāsa* initiation. After returning to Vṛndāvana I was daily going for *parikrama* after Maṅgala ā*rati* and then returning for Guru-pūjā. Since I was going directly from the temple, I carried my *daṇḍa.* For two days I passed an elderly, tall, bespeckled *sādhu,* who greeted me with a resonant “Hare Kṛṣṇa.” On the third day I left my *daṇḍa* in the temple and later saw the same *sādhu*. “Oh,” he said, “where is your *daṇḍa*? Have you taken your vows in the line of Swami Prabhupāda?” Then he lowered his head and folded his hands, and with watery eyes he said, “I was acquainted with him. He was so exalted, your Prabhupāda, and you are so fortunate to be acquainted with him. Actually, you don’t know the depths of your good fortune.”

Simple examples, but they let us see how pervading your mercy is. It affects everyone in all walks of life, at every level. So much so that they simply think of you, your deeds, your writing, your character, and they become transformed and they swell with emotion.

But Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’m so long your servant. Why they and not me? Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow me to become transformed as well.

This will happen if I always meditate on you. Please let me be by your side forever by pleasing you. Let me be by your side by remembering your bold and fearless preaching, and let me preach as well. Let me live by your side by remembering your soft and gentle heart. Please enter my heart. Please let me live by your side by becoming a pure devotee through your blessings. Please let me pass the realm of *anarthas* and attain steadiness, a taste for the Lord’s name, and full attachment to the Lord. Then please bless me that pure goodness will come and herald the awakening of *bhāva* and *prema*. And if by your mercy you see me one day fit, please take my hand in yours and train me to render service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara in Vraja.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, the rarely attained fulfillment of these desires seems so far sway. So much unsteadiness and complacence fill the vast area between my present position and my aspired-for goals.

But it is by your abundant grace that such hopes have entered the heart of a person as low as myself. And it is only by your mercy that such desires may be fulfilled.

Today, and forever, I want to be with you and to serve you. But I’m not seeing you. Therefore, I’m feeling separated from you, and that separation is giving me some pain.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please today shower your mercy on me. Please allow one ray of your exalted love for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa to stream into my heart. Please once and for all transform my meager existence and engage me eternally in you pure devotional service.

Praying to remain eternally your servant, and the servant of your servants,  
Bhakti-bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

**One Hundred Years**

The world didn’t know what was coming its way  
When in 1896 on a late summer’s day  
A spark of life’s descent meant the end to an age of discontent,  
In a century of woes, victories, triumphs, and blows.

The nineties are now gone, and make way for a new dawn.  
The age of wheels, the silver screen and worldly appeals.  
A young boy ventures to his school, his comprehension transcends the golden rule.  
He sees peace turn to debate and a world tossed in war and hate.

Then the twenties come in roaring, the people are all scoring,  
Targeting the American dream, bursting through horizon’s seam.  
A young man has been told to reach that mighty fold  
And crush all sordid illusions and multifarious confusions.

It is greed’s backlash that triggers a monetary crash,  
The dirty thirties wreak depression, basic needs are under suppression.  
A second war’s fatal bomb tells that everything has gone wrong,  
While a meek man plots to erect a civilization more correct.

The forties “big band” sound pulsates its message all around.  
The fifties bring the “rock and roll” which sets the stage for “soul.”  
A pensive man upon his seat, prefers a different kind of beat.  
Indifferent to the world of noise; retreating to the world of poise.

An age of protest, base sentiment, of drugs, sex, and acts most violent.  
The psychedelic lot is bemused; the sixties’ people are confused,  
A stranger takes to ocean travel to see just what he can unravel.  
He docks into the jungle of stone and for some time struggles alone.

The earth is now most obsessed by lunar real estate no less,  
While the stranger sweeps the nations, working with his endless patience.  
He’s altering all of their outlooks, through the medium of his books.  
They explore a mystic chant, while some reveal they can’t.

In November of ‘77 our friend transcends heaven.  
It would be fair to say, we took for granted what had come our way.  
We pass through the eighties’ boom and embark on nineties’ gloom.  
But done are one hundred years of gain, his efforts have not gone in vain.

Your servant,  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
What Shall We give Back to You?

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most glorious day, your disciples, granddisciples, and even some of your Godbrothers are all thanking Kṛṣṇa for sending such a great soul as your self. Your presence on this planet was a sign of Kṛṣṇa’s great mercy on the conditioned souls. You so expertly preached in the spirit of Lord Caitanya distributing the sacred message of the holy names and love of God in every town and village. You did not care who was qualified or unqualified: you gave everyone a chance to connect with transcendental knowledge. Because you loved us all so much, you took great risk to make us gentle Vaiṣṇavas, even though many like myself had lifestyles very similar to those in the animal kingdom. You did not neglect or avoid us. It seems the only way we can attempt to pay you back is to become actual first class Vaiṣṇava. But how can we do that unless you continue to give us more blessings? We are totally a product of your mercy, and we are totally dependent on your mercy although we are unqualified.

It is because of you that we have been given a chance to know about the Absolute Truth. It is because of you that we try to chant sixteen rounds every day, follow four regulative principles, and reflect on transcendental literature. It is because of you that we are writers, cooks, seamstresses, *pujārīs, saṅkīrtana* leaders, teachers, renunciants, managers, and even *gurus*. Please do not let us forget this. It is because of you that we can consider ourselves in little ISKCON, medium ISKCON, greater ISKCON, or an ISKCON affiliate. It is because of you that we can present different shades of the philosophy or even seek the shelter of other Vaiṣṇava alternatives. It is because of you that we even know what the word “Vaiṣṇava” means. Please do not let us forget it, or once again we will become a mouse. In other words, you are responsible for any resemblances of a Vaiṣṇava that we are. It is all your mercy that we are made up of. Now that you have given us everything, what shall we give back to you? Shall we become, like you, pioneers who will live and die as compassionate spiritual warriors? You have given us so many fields to do your work in. There are lifetimes of work to do for all of us, but it seems that if we do not constantly remind ourselves that you are the source of our strength and achievements and that your love and mercy extend far beyond a few thousand devotees and even far beyond this planet, we will think small, act small, dry up, and simply take for granted, abuse, and misuse what you have given us rather than give back to you as you did to your spiritual master.

These days you seem to have so many souls who work tirelessly every day selling your books, distributing *prasādam,* making devotees, and trying to protect your society. Please give more of your mercy to my Godbrothers, Godsisters, your grandsons, and your granddaughters. So many have forsaken wealth, education, friends, family, security, and even prestige in order to fight in your army. Sometimes these great stalwarts get a little weak or distracted, but they are surely your soldiers. I may not be fit to have your direct blessings, but at least allow me to be blessed by some of these staunch servants of yours. As more of them in these days are remembering that your mercy is all that we are made of, perhaps even a lowly man like myself may eventually be affected and become a first-class Vaiṣṇava. If so, then even I can give back to you rather than misuse or take for granted your mission.

Please forgive us all for our shortcomings and give us more mercy, for now strong signs of auspiciousness are showing as we are learning fast from our mistakes.

Thank you so much for giving us so much. Now it is our turn to give back to you.

Your son,  
Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhsya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.”**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated and humble obeisances at your lotus feet!

The world is right now in a very precarious condition. There is fighting everywhere. The masses of people need Your Divine Grace more than ever before. I feel that I am not doing enough to deliver your mercy to them. Your Bhaktivedanta Purports are indeed the rising sun in the dense darkness at this present time. I can only offer my *dandavats* to those who daily distribute your books throughout the world, and I am praying for your mercy that I might be able to help in instilling this understanding deep in the hearts of our students in your wonderful movement. By your causeless mercy you showed me how much you favor these *saṅkīrtana* devotees. They are very dear to you because they follow in your footsteps by working hard and renouncing all personal comfort in order to deliver the conditioned souls. Let us all follow the uncompetable example of your tireless endeavor to spread Kṛṣṇa’s glory. Only then will it be possible to get your favor and in this way become recognized by Kṛṣṇa.

In one of your Vyāsa-pūjā lectures you tried to teach your disciples about the importance of this most auspicious day, but because of your unimaginable love for Kṛṣṇa you instead explained that your appearance day is also very auspicious because this day is celebrated as Nandotsava—a celebrated *utsava* put on by Nanda Mahārāja after Kṛṣṇa’s birth. You continued to explain that Nanda Mahārāja was a little elderly when Kṛṣṇa was born, and some of his friends came to congratulate him, “My dear friend, you have got a son in your old age,” and Nanda Mahārāja said amongst the friends, “How can you say I am old? An old man cannot beget a child. I am a young man.” At that time one *brāhmaṇa* came and spoke a beautiful verse:

*śrutim apare smṛtim itare bhāratam anye bhajantu bhava-bhītaḥ*  
*aham iha nandaṁ vande yasyālinde paraṁ brahma*

**“Let others, fearing material existence, worship the** ***Vedas,*** **the Vedic supplementary** ***Purāṇas,*** **and the** ***Mahābhārata,*** **but I shall worship Nanda Mahārāja, in whose courtyard the Supreme Brahman is crawling.”**

In a similar way, let me worship Your Divine Grace on the auspicious anniversary of your appearance because Kṛṣṇa performs His transcendental pastimes in the courtyard of your heart—nothing else will protect me from material existence.

Your humble servant,  
Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhaktivaidurya Mādhava Mahārāja

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this holy day in 1896 you made your appearance in this dark world. When you descended from the spiritual world with the shining torchlight of knowledge gripped firmly in your hand, the darkness of ignorance fled before your formidable presence, as hobgoblins and sprites flee the rising sun. How could it be otherwise? For you are truly the person *Bhāgavata,* dispelling the illusions and misinformation of the present age by giving authoritative knowledge of the Personality of Godhead.

Out of complete faith in the words of your spiritual master, you left Vṛndāvana to preach where no saint had ever gone before. Presenting the message of Godhead and the divine dispensation for this age—*harināma-saṅkīrtana*—you delivered the most fallen. You exhibited maximum compassion, tolerance, forgiveness, and patience in training the untrainable and unqualified persons whom you encountered, and you transformed them into qualified Vaiṣṇava *brāhmaṇas*. Like a swan gliding as it pleases on a lake, you wandered fearlessly upon the earth as the foremost representative of Kṛṣṇa.

Anyone could see you were empowered by God. It was evident in everything you did. Those who cannot see this have eyes like bats and moles. No one has ever preached, nor will anyone ever preach, as you have.

On this occasion of your appearance, after twenty-five years as your disciple, I am sorely embarrassed at how little I have accomplished for you. I therefore herein vow to intensify my efforts to purify my heart in Kṛṣṇa consciousness so that I can render you bigger and better service in the coming years.

Your eternally indebted servant,  
Bhaktivaidurya Mādhava Mahārāja

## Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You’ve gone and left me here alone.  
You are my shelter,  
I must see you,  
There’s no reason to go on.  
You have saved me from this material world.  
I’m your eternal servant,  
Lost and found by your grace.  
Without you I’ve got no place  
At the Lord’s lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please let me cook for you.  
This is my hope,  
This is my dream—  
Taking birth to assist you.  
Until then I know what you want from me:  
Serve you in separation.  
If you give me the strength  
To live here without you,  
Only then can I live.

Lord Caitanya,  
Nityānanda,  
Prabhupāda is now with you.  
I must reach him by Your mercy.  
Preaching is what I must do.  
Compassion he is,  
Personified.  
He has made my good fortune.  
I can never pay him back.  
I will try eternally.  
Serving him is all I want.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Can I ever reach you?  
You’re so far away,  
I’m unqualified.  
Please tell me what to do.  
You are in your books.  
When will I realize  
There’s no need to go outside?  
Every stage of my life  
You’ll be there by my side.  
You are here with me in sound.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Your servant needs to be with you.  
This world is empty,  
I’ve got no purpose  
Other than serving you.  
Wherever you are—  
That’s where I want to be.  
I don’t need liberation,  
Just to sit at your feet.  
That’s my only wish—  
Helping you to serve Kṛṣṇa.

Rādhārāṇī,  
Golokānanda,  
Prabhupāda is kind to me.  
You have come here  
‘Cause he’s called You,  
Saving me from misery—  
From the separation from  
His lotus feet.  
I’m assisting Prabhupāda  
In you worship.  
He so kind to me,  
I’ll pay him eternally.

Eternally at your lotus feet,  
Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

## Gaṇapati Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your travel-weary lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, the transcendental mendicant who is individually responsible for extinguishing the fire of material existence all over this earth planet.

Your own Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, has nicely described your transcendental characteristics in the following words:

“Only an empowered personality can distribute the holy name of the Lord and enjoin all fallen souls to worship Kṛṣṇa. By distributing the holy name of the Lord, he cleanses the hearts of the most fallen people; therefore he extinguishes the blazing fire of the material world. Not only that, he broadcasts the shining brightness of Kṛṣṇa’s effulgence throughout the world. Such an *ācārya,* or spiritual master, should be considered nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa—that is, he should be considered the incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s potency. Such a personality is *kṛṣṇāliṅgita-vigraha—*that is, he is always embraced by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Such a person is above the considerations of the *varṇāśrama* institution. He is the *guru* or spiritual master for the entire world, a devotee on the topmost platform, the *mahā-bhāgavata* stage, and a *paramahaṁsa-ṭhākura,* a spiritual form only fit to be addressed as *paramahaṁsa* or *ṭhākura*” (*Cc Madhya* 25.9-purport).

You are surely *kṛṣṇāliṅgita-vigraha—*always embraced by the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Therefore, the whole world is attracted to your divine form, qualities, and *līlā*. Please inscribe this one reality into my dull consciousness: the only reason I am able to attract anyone to Kṛṣṇa consciousness is because I have some relationship with you, due to your having so kindly adopted this wayward orphan as your spiritual son. Otherwise, I am a nobody. Please help me to always realize that following your order as a menial servant is my only safeguard from falling into the hopeless abyss of self-aggrandizement. And please shower upon my head just a little drop of your fathomless enthusiasm to glorify Lord Kṛṣṇa until the final point of death.

I am hoping that Your Divine Grace accepts me as your eternal servant.  
Gaṇapati Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

This year I am writing to you from aboard the M. V. Reyjumar, en route to Bahol, where we will hold the second leg of your ongoing Philippines Padayātrā this year. Over the past two and a half months the Filipino devotees have successfully completed a walk through forty-six towns on the southern island of Cebu. By your mercy, the devotees now plan to walk through another eighteen towns on Bohol Island over the next month.

We are twenty-five devotees in number, all being led by Your Divine Grace in the form of your large “Prabhupāda Deity.” Along with us on this small passenger ship are roughly fifty “ordinary folk” and a half dozen or so dogs. Looking around, I am repulsed by the activities of these people who pride themselves on being so religious. Most of them are engaged in eating meat, drinking alcohol, and smoking cigarettes, with whatever balance time they have left wastefully spent in playing computer games or sleeping. They, like 99.9 per cent of the world’s population, are ignorant of who they really are—as evidenced by their addiction to sinful activities.

For such simple-minded people as these, who are not so philosophically inclined, your instruction to organize *harināma saṅkīrtana* and *prasādam* distribution is really the only hope. Padayātrā is therefore a tailor-made preaching program for the Philippines. Thank you for giving us this most sublime method for becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others.

Reflecting more deeply, I am appreciating how extraordinarily powerful is the desire of a pure devotee. Many countries around the world you visited only a few times, yet your disciples and granddisciples are establishing and even expanding your ISKCON Society around the globe. This great power emanates from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. There is no other explanation. You desire to see Kṛṣṇa consciousness spread to every nook and cranny of every country on the face of the earth. And miraculously your desire is being fulfilled! We are simply puppets in your hands.

This is not mere sentiment or hyperbole. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura says the same thing in his *Mādhurya Kadambini*: “The Lord bestows His own *kṛpā-śakti* \[the power to give His mercy to others\] upon His pure devotee”. Therefore, the Lord’s mercy is obtained through the mercy of the devotee who bestows it. With your *śaktyāveśa* potency you are raining down the mercy of the Supreme Lord and thus drowning this whole world in love for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

These are my limited realizations of your unlimited greatness for this year. I want to thank you for … Oh, there’s something more to report, Śrīla Prabhupāda! One of your granddisciples just told me that the captain of his ship was attracted by your *mūrti* and began asking many questions about your movement and philosophy. His is impressed with what he has heard and has offered a 50% discount to all devotees traveling island to island in the future. Now he wants to purchase some of your transcendental books! Your desire seems to penetrate all barriers, and even so-called unfavorable situations become perfect arrangements for preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Thank you for all that you have kindly given me. I beg you to please allow me to continue serving your advanced disciples and granddisciples so that I may learn from them how to love and serve you more intimately. I pray that in future years I can come to the level of fully surrendering my body, mind, and words at your lotus feet and thus earn the privilege to be used by you in any way you desire, birth after birth. Your mercy is all I am made of.

Your eternal servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Guṇaṅrāhi Swami

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda

On the anniversary of your appearance, more than on any other day, the temple is permeated with words of appreciation for Your Divine Grace, and we become filled with gratitude. When offering you our homage at this time, we sometimes feel the need to apologize for expressing thoughts quite similar or even identical to those that have been expressed throughout the day or in years gone by. We ultimately know, however, that it is not originality or variety that measures the quality of our offering, but simple, heartfelt devotion. Thus, just as the *Bhagavad-gītā* can be read repeatedly with great taste throughout one’s life, and just as the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* can be joyfully sung for hours on end, so each of your glories can be heard and chanted everlastingly by your devotees with ever-fresh relish and absorption. And although this undeserving beggar falls far short of the proper standard of devotion and, thus, has nothing to offer through any attempt to glorify you, I must humbly request the liberty to submit the following prayer:

I would like to …

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for removing the fear and confusion. Your words have lightened my heart.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for having cared for us the way you do. We can now befriend and care for others.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your dedication to your Guru Mahārāja. We can thus understand the meaning of “disciple.”  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for revealing the difference between animal life and human life. We can thus abhor our lower natures.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us the *śāstra*. We now have an unchanging reality upon which to build our lives.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the association of the saints in our line. Who can fathom the benefit of their association?  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for showing us genuine humility. We can now see the extent of our false pride.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for conquering old age, disease, and death, mocking our bodily attachment.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for defeating all our doubts. We can now give light to others.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for establishing the Deity and proclaiming His glories. Impersonalism and voidism are finally defeated.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your perfect Vaiṣṇava behavior. Your all-attractive demeanor has endeared you to the world.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being the perfect judge of time, place, and circumstance. Who else could have made spiritual life so practical, relevant, and irresistible?  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for coming to America when you did. I couldn’t have held on much longer.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for leaving Vṛndāvana and risking your life for us. We don’t dare turn our backs on this world or your mission.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for capturing our minds and senses. There is no telling where they would have led us.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for always pushing us to preach. Your love shatters our complacency.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us the holy name, our only shelter in this world.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for indiscriminately giving your kind association. My sins were all I had to offer.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us the holy *dhāma*. The contamination of Kali would otherwise consume us.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for teaching love and trust. If we don’t take it up, envy will defeat us.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for not kicking this fool away. This above all is the greatest display of your mercy.

*Nānto ‘sti mama divyānāṁ vibhūtīnāṁ parantapa*. Since you are Kṛṣṇa’s empowered emissary, there is no end to your divine opulences, qualities, and achievements. Thus I fall at your feet, begging to live always absorbed in your remembrance and service, never tiring of chanting your transcendental glories.

Your unworthy servant,  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Hanumātpreṣaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“One cannot establish a friendship with the Supreme Lord Rāmacandra on the basis of material qualities such as one’s birth in an aristocratic family, one’s personal beauty, one’s eloquence, one’s sharp intelligence, or one’s superior race or nation. None of these qualifications is actually a prerequisite for friendship with Lord Śrī Rāmacandra. Otherwise, how is it possible that although we uncivilized inhabitants of the forest have not taken noble births, although we have no physical beauty, and although we cannot speak like gentlemen, Lord Rāmacandra has nevertheless accepted us as friends?

“Therefore, whether one is a demigod or a demon, a man or a creature other than a man, such as a beast or bird, everyone should worship Lord Rāmacandra, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who appears on this earth just like a human being. There is no need of great austerities or penances to worship the Lord, for He accepts even a small service offered by His devotee. Thus He is satisfied, and as soon as He is satisfied, the devotee is successful. Indeed, Lord Śrī Rāmacandra brought all the devotees of Ayodhyā back home, back to Godhead \[Vaikuṇṭha\]” (SB 5.19.7-8).

His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, by manifesting the above-quoted qualities of the Supreme Lord, identifies himself as being an empowered representative and manifestation of Kṛṣṇa. Something is known by its qualities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is not difficult to approach. One does not have to be a very great personality according to any of the above-listed qualifications. One may not be an influential speaker or a clever politician or competent technician, but if one actually loves Śrīla Prabhupāda then he can please him very nicely with even a little humble service. We simply have to try our best to understand Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ambitions in serving Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, Lord Caitanya, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara and then try with all the sensual and mental and material resources at our command to fulfill those desires.

Personally I realize myself to be one of His Divine Grace’s humblest disciples and suspect that this is due to my own insincerity rather than some higher plan of providence. I aspire to acquire the qualifications of all the sincere souls who are nicely serving His Divine Grace and see that co-operatively we are always seizing every opportunity to expand and expand and expand the *saṅkīrtana* movement as all these opportunities arise.

Your fallen servant,  
Hanumātpreṣaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you in this most auspicious celebration of your appearance in this world.

This year I write to you from Kag Beni, high in the Himalayan mountains, on assignment for *Back to Godhead* magazine. By foot we have traveled to 12,000 ft., chronicling historical sites such as the path to Muktinath, tread thousands of years ago by the Pāṇḍavas on their final journey north amongst the mountain peaks.

The harsh climate here, with temperatures well below freezing, forbids casual visitors to the secrets of this land. Thus we have the rare fortune to see things most men will never behold. These sacred mountains, immovable through eons of time, stand as formidable reminders of the opulence of the Lord. The holy Kali Gandhi River, nondifferent from Tulasī, constantly bathes the sacred *śālagrāma-śilās* found within her waters. The rare snow leopard, the yak, and the horned mountain goat have several times crossed our path as we journeyed through this desolate region of the earth, barren but for a few trees and shrubs. The ancient caves of *yogīs,* some with artifacts still intact, provide a penetrating look into the past. What wonders we have seen! But despite all this, the real miracle is yet to come in a village still further north.

Our narrow trail takes us along perilous mountain cliffs, further isolating this remote area of the world. Entering Mustang, a forbidden territory until only months ago, I wonder how people of this region know anything beyond this time and place. In the valleys below, we were told that the mountain people here have little communication with the outside world. They live primitively and are uneducated, much like their ancestors thousands of years ago. However, to my amazement, upon seeing us they suddenly raise their hands and begin chanting the holy names of the Lord:

Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa!  
Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare!  
Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma!  
Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare!

I’m astonished! How could they possibly know the *mahā-mantra* in this isolated region? As I look around incredulously my eyes catch a young girl clutching a book, and I move closer to look. Suddenly I see you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, smiling out from the cover of that book! We thought we were amongst the privileged few to visit here, but you have come before us through the medium of your transcendental literatures and have caused these people to imbibe a spirit of devotional service to the Lord.

As we enter the village people surround us, eager to know more. You’ve touched their hearts with your transcendental books. In our journey through this land, none of the wondrous sights we have seen can compare to this miracle of Your Divine Grace. Even the immovable Himalayas have given you passage to the top of the world!

May I have the privilege to continue assisting you in your mission, wherever in the fourteen worlds you may go.

Your eternal servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayadhvaja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you.

**Remembrance**

I remember when I came to your shelter. I saw you in a Ratha-yātrā program on TV in 1972 or ‘73. It was Friday, and the next day I met the devotees in downtown Buenos Aires. Your first mercy came in the form of a visiting card. Even though I was a big demon, I was still attracted to that card. Then I met your devotees, who took me to your temple. My first impression was that I had entered another world. I thought, “These people are different from other people; they are very pure.” Then I read one of your books, which cut off my attachment. I was trying to enjoy every day, but I was losing the taste of that enjoyment. Your mercy cut me away, and chanting the holy name got me into the addiction.

Moving into the temple was the only condition. The temple president asked me, “What do you want to do?” I thought of book distribution because I really liked your books. We were living in a small temple far away from you, but sometimes in the classes we used to hear news about you: “Śrīla Prabhupāda has finished the Fifth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, and he has worked day and night, absorbed in *samādhi*.” Your ecstasy was catching. “Go on *saṅkīrtana.* Go on *saṅkīrtana.*” The GBC was crying to clear the warehouse. One day the whole temple was full of commotion, and we were fortunate to receive your letter with great emotion: Prabhupāda is coming to our temple! I could not believe that your mercy had come. I felt like a new person. My *anarthas* became very light. Then you gave me the name Jayadhvaja dāsa. That was my short contact with you. But your eternal relationship with me became clearer.

I came to India in 1978 and found your *samādhi;* you had just left for the spiritual world, to play with Kṛṣṇa. Your presence I missed, lamenting for awhile, but when I met your sister Pishima I was reminded of your presence. What transcendental bliss! Your temples, your devotees, your Deities remain to bear witness to your glories for ten thousand years.

**Your Qualities**

*amānitvam adambhitvam*  
*ahiṁsā kṣāntir arjavam*  
*ācāryopāsanaṁ śaucaṁ*  
*sthairyam ātma-vinigrahaḥ*

*Amānitvam:* Your humility is shown by the fact that you fully surrendered your life to the holy name.

*Adambhitvam:* Pridelessness is fully manifested in Your Divine Grace, for you care nothing for personal worship and fame.

*Ahiṁsā:* Violence, as you said, is *ātma-hatya*—killing the soul. By preaching the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness with the tools of love, you have rescued the world, showing ideal nonviolence.

*Kṣānti:* Honor and dishonor you have overcome to preach the holy name. Thus you are the embodiment of tolerance.

*Arjavam:* You taught us simplicity by leading a devotee life. High thinking is our goal. Prabhupāda, you are so wise.

*Ācārya upāsanā: Guru-saṅga* you always sought. On *parikrama,* you never left when Bhaktisiddhānta spoke, but heard what he taught.

*Śaucam:* External and internal cleanliness you preached, training your disciples.

*Sthairyam:* You were steady in all circumstances, extending your mercy to *dhīras* and *adhīras* alike.

*Ātma-vinigraha:* Self-control. The bogus *yogīs* you rejected—artificial self-control without chanting the holy name. “I am Bhagavān,” they say. What is this nonsense! It is insane. The Bhaktivedanta purports, the real *śāstras,* are manifest.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me your association. Without it, I will simply seek illusion, without relief. I want to grow spiritually! But I don’t know what to do. Please give me your guidance; I want to learn from you.

Poor and insignificant is my condition. Please, Gurudeva, come take me away from this conflagration. *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Gurudeva! Many conditioned souls you have saved.

On this auspicious day let us glorify Your Divine Grace, who are more compassionate than Kṛṣṇa Himself. O Savior of the fallen! Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, my master and friend!

*Jaya* Jagat Guru! *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your fallen son,  
Jayadhvaja Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**The Heart of Our Spiritual Master**

I ask, O Lord, for Your permission  
And, beyond, Your inspiration  
To glorify our master’s mission  
In humble mood of celebration,

To touch his feet and write his heart  
As best as *mleccha* hands can do  
And play my designated part  
In serving him who’s dear to You,

And somehow feel it doesn’t matter  
Other men can do it better.

May every Vaiṣṇava reader  
Flick a little dust in my direction.  
Let the writer be the eater  
Relishing that Vaiṣṇava dust confection.

If preaching is the essence,  
Then the heart is the essence of preaching,  
A heart that is empty of nescience  
And filled with Lord Caitanya’s teachings,

A heart where cheap sentiments haven’t a chance  
And mental concoctions get boots in the face,  
Where Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa eternally dance  
And knowledge makes *māyāvāda* flee in disgrace.

Your heart, Śrīla Prabhupāda, pulls at our hearts,  
As Kṛṣṇa might pull on the rope of a cow.  
We’ve strayed from His herd and meandered apart.  
And kindly you’re pulling us back to Him now.

Kinder and kinder, bolder and bolder,  
You reach out with mercy for others,  
Your heart hard and brave like an English soldier’s  
And soft like a Bengali mother’s.

The ways that you deal with us pull us toward you,  
Invoking surrender, as you kindly request,  
“If you’re grateful to me, then please do as I do:  
Preach the message of Kṛṣṇa. Preach and be blessed.”

Jayādvaita Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

On the blessed occasion of your 97th appearance-day celebration, I am again meditating on your up-coming 1996 Mahā Vyāsa-pūjā, and this will be my meditation for the next three years. Already last year I had given you an overall account of our initial plans to celebrate your glorious 100th appearance day in Calcutta. Now I would like to update you further about the wonderful developments that are taking place gradually all over the world and within the hearts of the devotees.

During its 1993 annual meetings, your GBC Body decided to make your Centennial Campaign and Celebration the focus of all ISKCON’S activities through 1996. The resolution reads as follows.:

“Whereas the Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial has unique potential to accomplish the following ISKCON objectives:  
—appreciation and recognition of Śrīla Prabhupāda  
—unity and strength in the ISKCON family  
—developing and involving ISKCON’s congregation  
—expansion of *saṅkīrtana,* especially book distribution  
and whereas the Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial can function as a deadline for achieving certain agreed-upon goals, it is resolved:  
That the International Society for Krishna Consciousness shall make the campaign for and celebration of the Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial a focus of all its activities through 1996.”

Such a resolution certainly inspires the Centennial team, for now we can dive with doubled enthusiasm into the nectar of working for your glorification.

I had an idea to design a Centennial poster for all the temples entitled “Śrīla Prabhupāda wants you,” like the one the BBT once published, to inspire devotees to take part in the book distribution marathon. But as I contemplated further, I concluded that you wouldn’t personally take the initiative and encourage your followers to organize a grand show in your honor. Out of humility, in the early days you were even reluctant to accept *guru-pūjā,* what to speak of a Mahā Vyāsa-pūjā. So we’ll rather make a “Kṛṣṇa wants you” poster, for the Lord does want to engage everyone in glorifying his pure devotee.

This year in the Centennial Ministry report to the Deities in Māyāpur, I felt confident that Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava want to glorify you. With the hope that the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa is blessing our attempt, we have formed a ministry (continental, national, and local committees). The GBC is helping but they are counting on the Ministry to line up additional manpower and raise funds. As I stood in front of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava, feeling desperate for their help, I took the opportunity to pray to Their Lordships. Usually, we pray *na dhanaṁ na janaṁ na sundarīm,* but to make your Centennial Celebration most successful, I begged the Lord, *dhanaṁ dehi janaṁ dehi—*”Give us wealth, give us manpower.” The mood of this prayer may not sound “regular,” but the Lord knows our motivation.

On the eve of the Centennial Ministry presentation to the Governing Body Commission in Śrīdhām Māyāpur I dreamt of you. Some leaders felt that the dream, though very brief, was very significant. I would like to relate it here.

I saw Your Divine Grace traveling in a car. You were sitting on the back seat with Bhaktarūpa Prabhu, and I was in the front. I asked you, “Prabhupāda, are you pleased with the Centennial Campaign?” Protesting, Bhaktarūpa intervened, “How can Śrīla Prabhupāda know, we haven’t given him any report yet?”

But you did reply to my enquiry, although indirectly. As we kept driving, you pointed to the crop in the fields and said, “The quality and quantity of the grains of a crop depend on how much fertilizer and water you put on the root. As you sow, so you shall reap.” I took your words as a message, which meant that the success of your Centennial celebration depends on how well we prepare for it; it became even more obvious that we need to conduct a Centennial Campaign very vigorously and with a sense of urgency.

Campaign means build up, like the campaign for an election. And in a way, 1996 is like an election year for you. At the foundation of all our plans for the Centennial is our desire to enhance people’s perception of Your Divine Grace. We want to accept your Centennial as a challenge to promote your movement, inspire appreciation, and increase your following. The Centennial has a magic touch—whatever we are already doing can be expanded and enhanced by appropriate association with the Centennial.

The opportunity of the Centennial is most importantly an opportunity to preach and spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Our celebration should not be simply a flash in the pan that grabs the attention of the world and is then forgotten. It should build dynamic awareness and interest in your movement and carry it forward into the next century.

Last year our plans sounded grand. The Centennial master plan, shaped in a ten-petaled lotus, each representing a field of activities, still remains the guideline to prepare for your celebration. But we have now scaled down many of our initial ideas. Besides the many small and big events, the Bhaktivedanta Institute Conference and a World Traveling Sankīrtan Festival seem to have been accepted as leading events. And of course, through the vehicle of the Centennial, your books can truly reach new heights of acceptance throughout the world. Books are the basis of the Centennial, too.

However, this grand show will not be the most meaningful aspect of your Centennial celebration. While circulating the first draft of the Centennial master plan, we sent a circular asking devotees what they thought of our ambitious targets. Should we celebrate the event at all, or were we “barking up the wrong tree?” In response came the following sample replies, which give a hint of the right spirit of the Centennial.

““The Centennial has to be managed so expertly that the devotees are encouraged to surrender to *guru* and Krishna more and more,” wrote Bhaktarūpa dāsa.

““I see the Centennial as an opportunity to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda by uniting his disciples and granddisciples in a common purpose. In many ways our movement has been splintered since the disappearance of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Much healing has taken place in the last several years.
