And I see the Centennial as a culmination of this healing process,” said Ambarīśa dāsa.

“The Centennial includes an increase quantitatively and qualitatively of all services,” thought Mother Jadurāṇī.

“Whatever is done should portray the gravity and dignity of Śrīla Prabhupāda as a divinely empowered *ācārya,*” pointed out Rādhānātha Swami.

“If we could get back that international grand-scale spirit of book distribution that was there in the early 70’s and bring back as many as possible of the Prabhupāda disciples who have drifted away, surely it would please Śrīla Prabhupāda so much. Isn’t that the motivation? To please Śrīla Prabhupāda?” wrote Adityā dāsī.

“I think that this is a great opportunity to regain and show unity and to enthuse those who lost faith in ISKCON,” said Kṛṣṇa Prema Dasa.

And “We need to prove by our individual and group character strength how a society of people can cooperate, nurture each other, and grow ‘materially and spiritually,’“ explained Gopavṛndapāla dāsa.

This is their feedback, loud and clear: let’s get together and put the emphasis not mainly on a big show but on purifying, uniting, and strengthening the family of your devotees. You told us that our love for you will be shown by how much we cooperate together. Now we want to utilize the Centennial to make an extra effort to fulfill this most crucial and sacred instruction of yours. This will make us strong and will make you jubilant.

The success of your Centennial celebration depends on the availability of manpower, which is very inadequate if it is based only on full-time temple devotees. We feel that you are forcing us to reach out to all of your disciples and granddisciples, many of whom are now living outside the movement, as well as your uninitiated followers, friends, and congregational members. Now is the time to build sensibly and wisely by using our greatest strength. By directing our program towards our grass-roots support, automatically men and funds will come. We must involve our sympathizers and carefully study the needs, interests, and resources of our different target audiences in order to achieve success.

In our enthusiasm to put you in the limelight, we must be careful not to make you into an “Avatar.” To prevent this, we will present you as Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee, fully surrendered to the will of the Lord and acting as a true empowered representative of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. By His blessings and your complete surrender to your *guru,* Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Ṭhākura, you fulfilled the prophesy of Lord Caitanya to “spread the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* to every town and village in the world.” Although Jesus Christ is known for his saintly qualities, he did not establish hundreds of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa temples or initiate thousands of sincere and surrendered disciples. The followers of Christ made him famous on the basis of his qualities, such as compassion. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are fully endowed with all the divine qualities, and you use all these transcendental qualities to spread the mercy and teachings of the Supreme Lord all over the world for the benefit of all living entities. We want to show the world how your achievements are monumental and visible throughout the world. The Centennial is an opportunity to reveal your divine qualities and your compassionate work to the whole world. Let everyone in the world recognize your unique position as the spiritual master of the modern world.

The heavy responsibility of accomplishing this task will be done only if we actually work together. Again, I need the help of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa sitting in everyone’s heart, and I need your blessings also to obtain all the necessary inspiration. We are looking for 100% participation. No one should stand by idly and just witness. Active participation by all of your followers will be what is appreciated by the Lord. Your Centennial is Lord Kṛṣṇa’s project to glorify Your Divine Grace and purify us. Whatever is lacking, Kṛṣṇa will certainly provide, and whatever good qualities you have given us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the Lord will increase if we want to use them for your glorification.

Thanking you for this opportunity to serve your lotus feet, I think myself fortunate to be your servant.

Eternally yours,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Kṛṣṇa dāsa Swami

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

What words can I offer in praise of you? I feel so inadequate and unqualified, just like Dhruva Mahārāja did when he obtained the darshan of Śrī Viṣṇu. During the initial stage of the Lord’s appearance, Dhruva was just gazing at the Lord, unable to say a word although his heart spoke volumes. Lord Viṣṇu, understanding the dilemma of His devotee, touched Dhruva Mahārāja on the head with His divine conchshell. By this merciful act of the Lord, the humble child devotee, Dhruva, glorified Śrī Viṣṇu with the most beautiful prayers, thus describing the object of his meditation.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, right now I too am feeling that same sense of dumbness, unable to glorify you, who are the most confidential servitor of the Lord. Therefore, just as Dhruva Mahārāja received the mercy of the Lord’s touch, I also beg for His mercy so that I can suitably glorify His pure devotee.

The following beautiful prayer to the spiritual master—

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhsya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

very nicely describes how everyone is born in the darkest of ignorance, and how the *guru* removes that darkness by the torchlight of knowledge. That is the business of the *guru,* to pick up his disciples enveloped in darkness and place them in the light.

In this dark iron Age of Kali the whole world is influenced by *tamo-guṇa* and is suffering terribly due to ignorance. But the people are so foolish that instead of removing the cause of their suffering they are simply engaged in covering up the disease. Only you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are providing the real cure, but unfortunately there are many bogus so-called spiritual leaders out there leading various denominations who are misleading the people and increasing their followers' sufferings. They claim they possess the light of knowledge, but in reality they are completely covered in darkness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the light of all lights, and only you are the real hope for the suffering world. We say this because you have presented the message of Śrī Kṛṣṇa as it is. Thousands of years ago Śrīla Vyāsadeva recorded, in written words, the transcendental message of Kṛṣṇa by repeatedly saying *śrī bhagavan uvāca.* He never wrote, “In my opinion this is what Kṛṣṇa is saying.” As the greatest *dcaryas* down through the ages have presented Kṛṣṇa’s message without adulteration, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have presented the Absolute Truth in the same way for our welfare. You have been most compassionate to the suffering people of the world. Understanding their cause of suffering, you gave them the real medicine. Without a doubt you are the savior of this world, deserving the title Jagad-guru, the spiritual master of the universe.

Your mercy is limitless, Śrīla Prabhupāda. When I first started to serve you many years ago, I never imagined that one day you would engage me in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in far-away lands. I thought my service would be limited to the U.K. only. Last year to my surprise I was asked to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness to a very large gathering in a famous City Hall in South Africa. I was told after my address that years ago you had personally preached in the same City Hall, and after so many years’ gap, unqualified as I am, by your mercy only you engaged me in the same service of the Lord. I was very happy to have had the privilege to follow in your footsteps. For a disciple to follow in his spiritual master’s steps is his pride and joy and his only protection. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am very confident that if we follow in your footsteps we would surely trace them back all the way back to Godhead.

With all humility I offer my most respectful obeisances to my eternal well-wisher, friend, and spiritual master, who is also the eternal well-wisher, friend, and spiritual master of the whole world. Jagad-guru Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

In the service of your lotus feet,  
Kṛṣṇa dāsa Swami

## Mahānidhi Swami

**Śrīla Prabhupāda Mahimāmṛta**  
**(The Nectarean Glories of Śrīla Prabhupāda)**

A song sung to the tune of Jaya Rādhe, Jaya Kṛṣṇa, Jaya Vṛndāvana, Śrī Govinda, Gopīnātha, Madana-mohana.

*(1) jaya bāla-līlā jaya, saṅkīrtan-vilāsa*  
*jaya jaya nagar-kīrtan, gaura-prema-rāsa*

*(2) jaya jagannātha jaya, jaya ratha-yātrā*  
*jaya jaya hare kṛṣṇa, jaya mahā-mantra*

*(3) jaya sarasvatī ṭhākur, mahā-mukta-jana*  
*dāsānudāsa jaya, caraṇāravindam*

*(4) śrī bhaktivedānta jaya, ratna-dvija-rāja*  
*jaya bhāgavata jaya, bhakti-tikā-kavirāja*

*(5) jaya rādhā-dāmodara, śrī jīva gosvāmī*  
*sevā-kuñja jaya jaya, śrī rūpa mañjarī*

*(6) jaya śrīla prabhupāda, śrī vaiṣṇava ṭhākura*  
*jaya jaya jagat guru, jaya nitāi-gaura*

*(7) jaya bhavavad-gītā, jaya śāstra-pramāṇ*  
*jaya jaya pracār-kṛpā, patita-pāvaṇa*

*(8) jaya rādhā-kṛṣṇa-mandir, jaya sādhu-saṅga*  
*śrī kṛṣṇa-prasāda jaya, iskcon bṛhat-mṛdaṅga*

*(9) jaya śrī kiśora jaya, jaya śrī kiśorī*  
*jaya hari-nāma jaya, jaya b b t*

*(10) jaya kṛṣṇa balarāma, jaya gopa-gaṇa*  
*śrīla prabhupāda jaya, jaya śrī iskcon*

*(11) jaya nitya-līlā jaya, jaya vṛndāvana*  
*śyamā-śyama-caraṇa-seva, jaya govardhana*

*(12) jaya śuddha-nāma jaya, vraja-vāsa-āśa*  
*jaya guru-kṛpā jaya, dīna nitya-dāsa*

**(1) All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s childhood pastimes! All glories to his spreading the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. All glories to his chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***kīrtana*** **through the streets of Calcutta. All glories to his always relishing the mellow of pure love of Lord Caitanya.**

**(2) All glories to Lord Jagannātha. All glories to his blissful Ratha-yātrā festival, which Śrīla Prabhupāda began at the age of five. All glories to the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra,*** **which Śrīla Prabhupāda chanted while leading Lord Jagannātha down Harrison Road in Calcutta.**

**(3) All glories to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, the greatest among liberated souls. All glories to his disciple, Abhay Charanaravinda, who fearlessly and humbly acted as a servant of the servant of the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.**

**(4) All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, the crest jewel of all** ***brāhmaṇas,*** **who was honored for his pure devotion and Vedic scholarship with the title, Bhaktivedanta. All glories to the** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*** **All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, the king of poets, whose commentaries on the** ***Bhāgavat*** **are full of** ***bhakti*** **and his devotional ecstasies.**

**(5) All glories to Śrī Jīva Gosvāmī’s beloved Deities of Rādhā-Dāmodara, who were so dear to Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to Seva-kuñja in Vṛndāvana, where Śyāmasundara serves Kiśorī. All glories to Śrī Rūpa Mañjarī, the constant companion of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī.**

**(6) All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, the most wonderful Vaiṣṇava saint. All glories to Jagat Guru Śrīla Prabhupāda, who distributed Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world and all over the universe. All glories to the most merciful Lords, Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu and Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu.**

**(7) All glories to the** ***Bhagavad-gītā As It Is.*** **All glories to this scripture (*****śāstra*****), which establishes the proof about the Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s preaching mercy, which purifies and uplifts the fallen souls of Kali-yuga.**

**(8) All glories to the temples of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa that Śrīla Prabhupāda opened in 108 different cities around the world. All glories to the blissful association of Kṛṣṇa’s devotees found within these temples. All glories to the “big mṛdaṅga”—the global sound made by ISKCON’s continual distribution of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books to benefit mankind.**

**(9) All glories to Lord Kṛṣṇa (Śrī Kiśora). All glories to Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī (Śrī Kiśorī), the names of the Deities in ISKCON Chicago. A devotee asked, “Why does Rādhārāṇī’s name (Kiśorī) appear at the end, when everywhere else Her name comes before Kṛṣṇa’s, as in Rādhā-Govinda, Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, and Rādhā-Dāmodara?” Śrīla Prabhupāda smiled sweetly and said, “Why? Because we always save** ***the best*** **for last.” All glories to the holy names of Kṛṣṇa. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s BBT—the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.**

**(10) All glories to Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, the beloved Deities of Śrīla Prabhupāda that he personally installed in ISKCON Vṛndāvana. All glories to the cowherd boys of Vṛndāvana. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to the wonderful and beautiful ISKCON—the International Society for Krishna Consciousness—which Śrīla Prabhupāda founded to save humanity.**

**(11) All glories to Rādhā’s and Kṛṣṇa’s eternally flowing transcendental pastimes. All glories to Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, who eternally lives in Vṛndāvana, serving the lotus feet of Rādhā(Śyāmā)-Śyāmasundara. All glories to Govardhana Hill. Giri-govardhana is the most beautiful and glorious of all mountains. Govardhana is simultaneously the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa and also the best devotee of the Lord.**

**(12) All glories to the pure chanting of the holy names of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. All glories to the desire to reside in Vraja-dhāma. All glories to the mercy of the spiritual master. Only by receiving your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and by purely chanting the holy names of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa can this fallen eternal servant of yours ever fulfill his desire to live eternally in Vṛndāvana.**

Your fallen eternal servant,  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You are my spiritual master, the representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself. You have most mercifully picked me up from a hopeless life.

I have promised before you to chant sixteen rounds and three Gāyatrīs daily and follow the four regulative principles for my whole life.

You have given me such a wonderful life that I can never repay my debt to you. I am only living always in your shelter by the tolerance of all the devotees.

My humble service is to be the “ISKCON Regional Secretary for Nepal, Bihar, and East U.P.”, where there are so many beautiful holy places and rivers that I can always remember Kṛṣṇa whilst traveling around. I can also remember that these were the places of your earlier travels. Sometimes I am also kindly allowed to travel with His Grace Viṣṇu Mūrti Prabhu and the Bhaktivedanta Archives Book Party through all the temples and devotee homes of Europe, collecting donations for our third world projects. Sometimes I also join in your Prabhupāda book marathon in Europe, and every year I join the Māyāpur Festival, the amazing Navadvīpa-mandala Parikrama and the Kārttika festival in Vṛndāvana. This year I hope to join in the Vraja-mandala Parikrama also. In this way I am so happy in my service, always in a nectarean state by the mercy of Your Divine Grace.

I am very happy to have this service amidst so many truly qualified and sincere devotees, who accept even this envious soul as part of their team for preaching your ISKCON mission. Especially I am grateful to His Holiness Prabhaviṣṇu Swami and His Holiness Jayapatāka Swami for their personal supervision and encouragement despite my obvious failures.

By your mercy some disciples have come to me even, and I am trying to help them become strong and faithful servants in your ISKCON and so go back home to Godhead. They are encouraging and helping me with their sincere efforts. Because of you only, now I have also become a spiritual father.

So it is not possible for me to fully express my gratitude on this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

I am just praying for your mercy to become qualified to really be your representative. I pray to you to help me achieve something which will give you great satisfaction. I want to be able to preach in Hindi and Nepali and see some flourishing projects in your name, with books, temples, farms, schools, restaurants, festivals, and many devotees chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa ecstatically. However, I regret that I am always foolishly falling short and just feebly struggling, only just about a lame-uncle white elephant.

I am just praying that I may never lose my connection with you through Māyā’s strength. I am just praying that I can always feel your presence and pleasure in my insignificant service.

Your humble servant,  
Mahāviṣṇu Swami \[the younger\]

## Narmada Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so magnanimous and compassionate, and you have saved many fallen souls. You know that I am the most fallen; no one is more fallen than I. So please be merciful unto me. Your plan is to reclaim all the conditioned, fallen souls, but I assure you that you will not find a more fallen soul than me. Therefore, my claim is first.

I surely know that one who engages in the transcendental loving service of Your Divine Grace in Kṛṣṇa consciousness can immediately achieve the transcendental position on the spiritual platform, which is eternal and blissful. Therefore, kindly bless me so that can I can become fixed at your lotus feet and engage in your loving service. Please bless me with the six positive qualities—enthusiasm in practicing devotional service, firm faith in devotional processes, a strong desire to attain *prema-bhakti,* a favorable service attitude, avoidance of nondevotees, and appreciation of the company of devotees—so that I can make steady progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your most fallen servant,  
Narmada Goswami

## Paraṁ Gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most sacred anniversary of your appearance, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, everything takes on greater meaning and greater force. The desire to go on serving you has become more intense, and your powerful instructions have become more and more alive and significant. Meditating on each and every word of your Bhaktivedanta purports, understanding the urgency to put them into practice, knowing that they are the direct manifestation of the Supreme Lord through your lotus mouth, realizing that these words were not spoken lightly, that they are completely transcendental, powerful, and sufficient to deliver the living beings from the prison of forgetfulness of their real identity, sufficient not only to give them liberation but also to reveal to them the form of God, His activities, His qualities, and finally even their eternal relationship with Him, the Supreme Personality of Godhead—all of these become more intensely evident on this holy day.

In order to save humanity from the chaos of ignorance, we have to understand the urgency of your mission, to accept that mission as our own, and to work to carry on with it, just as you did. We have to be prepared to sacrifice personal desires for the benefit of the world, to be ready to tolerate all difficult situations. And above all, we must fight against Kali-yuga’s manifestations in quarrels, lack of unity, and breaking of the regulative principles. All of these imperatives become clear and essential, uniquely important, on this holy day.

To ever maintain fresh and new what you always told us—that we should go on distributing your books, making new devotees, maintaining an exemplary behavior so that human society can get inspiration from us, showing the world that we are not a “sect” but are giving real knowledge to human society, *sanātana-dharma,* as well as to have the desire to work more seriously to put these things into practice—this is my plea on the holy day of your appearance.

O my dear eternal father, protect me so that this desire can be maintained constantly within my heart, so that it will not be temporary like the momentary bathing of an elephant, just as Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura prays in his *Śaraṇāgati:*

*ātma-nibedana-bhāva hṛde dṛdha roy*  
*hasti-snāna sama jeno khanika no hoy*

**“May the mood of self-surrender to the Supreme Lord remain firmly fixed in my heart and not prove to be like the momentary cleanliness of an elephant after a bath.”**

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am praying at your lotus feet that I may be constantly aware of this renewal of strength manifested on your appearance day. May it go on every day of my existence, as if your Vyāsa-pūjā were going on eternally in my heart.

Your most fallen servant,  
Paraṁ Gati Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

A conditioned soul should not imitate a *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee’s activities, but must follow his instructions to reach perfection. In 1969, on the disappearance day celebration of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, when I saw you walking up the stairs to the temple room in Boston, the entire room turned into Vaikuṇṭha by your presence. All my anxieties disappeared. Sitting down on the *vyāsāsana,* you looked at me and I could see that your vision was perfect and clear. Your *samādhi* of emotional ecstasy that you experienced in your transcendental separation from Śrī Kṛṣṇa and His eternal associates cannot be imitated. However, in the association of your devotees, through your books I try to develop transcendental vision and try to serve you and thus achieve your blessings and causeless mercy. “Discussion of Kṛṣṇa is very potent, and if a fortunate person has such association \[of devotees\] and tries to assimilate the knowledge, then he will surely make advancement toward spiritual realization” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 9.1, purport).

You are compassionate upon the many conditioned souls suffering in the material world and know the ultimate solution to their problems—hearing about the Supreme Lord in the association of devotees and a bona fide spiritual master. “The other methods of purification may be helpful to come to this stage of life, but ultimately one has to come to this last point before he attains actual perfection” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.6.34). Therefore we must preach your message and create the Vaikuṇṭha atmosphere within our temples, so people can come and faithfully hear about Śrī Kṛṣṇa—that will solve the problems of this world.

Your aspiring servant,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rasānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Your are the life and soul of all the Vaiṣṇava devotees. Let me perform Śrī Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtana* under the shelter of your lotus feet

Let me participate in your plan for flooding the whole universe with massive amounts of books. Otherwise, what is the meaning of my *sannyāsa* order? *Sannyāsī* means to preach, and the best way to preach is not by my simple talks, but by your book distribution.

You already have a plan to flood the entire universe with your books. In many parts of the world the devotees are selling huge quantities of books. However, I am not participating in this book distribution, so I am frustrated. What can I do? I have no qualification. All that there is left for me to do is to cry and to try my best to become a recipient of your mercy. Everything is being done by you and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. If it is your desire, certainly it will happen.

There are some parts of the world that book distribution is dormant. I am sure you are not satisfied with it, but by your desire the fire will be lit at any time and you will give many, many devotees the opportunity to participate in it. Please put me on your list.

I am begging for your mercy. I need to perform devotional service for the welfare of all. Please, let me be an instrument in your hands. Let me fulfill your desire to inundate this universe with intensive book distribution; otherwise my life will be a great waste of time.

I hope your are well.

Your servant,  
Rasānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to you.

Today on your divine appearance day I strongly feel that you are interested to hear what I have realized during the last year. Please allow me to offer you some of my insignificant understandings in the attempt to glorify you on this day of your divine appearance:

1\) YOU INSTRUCT AND GUIDE YOUR DISCIPLES ETERNALLY, BEYOND THE LIMITS OF TIME.

One of the greatest wonders in my relationship with you is that, full of love and attention, you unfailingly turn toward me whenever I become conscious of you and seek your help. Although you stand on the other side of the river of death, you still stay with me. Such is your power as the pure representative of the Supreme Lord and my eternal father. If I hear directly from you in the form of lectures and books, the Supreme Lord in the heart becomes my **active** well-wishing friend and cleanses the dirty things from my heart. Your words carry such strength that you can make us the object of the mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead! That is because you are so dear to the Lord. (In regards to your words, I remember how we once asked Jayādvaita Swami for guidelines on translating your books. He answered by quoting you: “Don’t change a word; these purports are my personal ecstasies.”)

2\) THE BEST WAY TO INCREASE KṚṢṆA CONSCIOUSNESS IS TO DEVELOP A TASTE FOR CHANTING THE HOLY NAMES AND SHOW MERCY TO ALL LIVING ENTITIES.

As the years pass by, I have also been counted among the senior devotees in this movement. Since quite some time, however, I realize that unfortunately I am senior only by years spent in your arrangement to make me Kṛṣṇa conscious. So naturally I wonder how to improve my seemingly hopeless condition. The answer, which came through you during this year’s Māyāpur /Vṛndāvana Festival, is so relieving: *nāma-ruci* and *jīva-dayā*. The movement of Lord Caitanya is based on these two principles, and when one studies your life one can see that you stand as the most shining example of them.

3\) AS YOUR DISCIPLES, WE CAN OBTAIN FROM YOU THE GIFT OF YOUR DETERMINATION.

As we think of you, almost the first picture which comes to my mind is how you struggled to establish this movement with great determination, but at the same time stayed detached. You once wrote to one of your disciples: “When one becomes fixed up in devotional service he never allows anyone else or any condition at all to hamper his service to Krishna or discourage him in any way” I realized that you are always there to help us fight against the attacks of Māyā and support our struggle for the preaching mission. Once you assured us, “I can kick out all stumbling blocks immediately, provided you accept my guidance.”

4\) YOU ARE ALWAYS INVITING US TO ASSOCIATE WITH THE SUPREME LORD.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* it is stated, “To be elevated to the plat- form of devotional service, the following five items should be observed: Association with devotees, engagement in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the reading of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, the chanting of the holy names, and residence at Vṛndavāna or Mathurā. One’s dormant love for Kṛṣṇa gradually awakens if one is a little advanced in one of these five items and is intelligent” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 24.193-194). You have painstakingly given us all five of these processes and very precise instructions on all of them. You are forever inviting us to associate with the Lord through these most powerful processes: “One should accept this opportunity to return home, back to Godhead, very enthusiastically,” you once wrote. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy is unlimited, and your ability to administer this mercy is totally amazing. Although you sincerely want me to grow up, I still have remained your helpless son, totally dependent on you. And you know this very well. I know that on your divine appearance day you are especially mercifully inclined towards us. Therefore, I fervently beg you to please bless me to chant the holy names free from offenses and wander freely here and there distributing the unlimited nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone. Once you wrote, “You do not require to worry, as I shall never leave you. You are my disciple and I am your spiritual master, so there is no question, as long as you follow my instructions, then I shall never leave you. … If you think of me and work for me, then I am in your heart.” These assurances are very, very dear to us. They are guiding us. On this day, therefore, let me lay down in prostrated obeisances before your lotus feet and offer you my self. I am sorry that it is still covered with dirt. My only hope is that you use me nevertheless according to your desires. Thanking you very much, I remain

Your most humble servant,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

Looking at photos of Śrīla Prabhupāda can be a powerful way to meditate on him and to ease the pain of his separation. The photos can evoke memories of Prabhupāda and also help us meditate on his different moods. They can provide a stimulus to pray to Prabhupāda to keep us in his service and to reveal our eternal relationship with him. They can invoke in us the desire to love him.

\* \* \*

This morning I looked at an early photo of Śrīla Prabhupāda in Vṛndāvana. He is surrounded by a few disciples, and they appear to be on the verandah of the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple. The sun is bright. Śrīla Prabhupāda stands in the sunlight, his disciples in the shade. He is wearing that rust-colored sweater he often wore in India, along with a garland of orange marigolds, and he holds his bamboo cane at an angle in his right hand. You can see the usual wrinkles in his *dhotī* and *kūrta* (because his habit is to sleep overnight in the *dhotī* and change it after his noon bath).

There are four disciples with him. One looks like Professor Rao (later called Rāmānanda dāsa) from Gorakhpur. The other is, I think, a young man who later left ISKCON, maybe the one who became a Buddhist. Śyāmasundara dāsa and Subala dāsa are also there.

It is easy to see Prabhupāda’s dark golden hue and the red *candana* markings on his forehead. His beadbag is hanging around his neck to remind me today that I too should chant Hare Kṛṣṇa as his follower. They are standing under the arch with its Mogul or Vaiṣṇava decoration, hundreds of years old, the place of Jīva Gosvāmī. Hard stone.

So far I have described the externals. It’s a candid snapshot and it captures me, but when I look away I return to the present. The fact that Prabhupāda and two others wear sweaters means it’s probably Kārttika. The bamboo cane tells me it’s probably 1971 or ‘72. Even in the photo, the morning looks cold. Prabhupāda stands straight; I don’t think he was ill at this time.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I haven’t said anything about your desire to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, nor have I described why you are trying to organize and push your disciples to construct the Vṛndāvana temple. It is unusual to see you outside your own ISKCON building, but this was before Krishna-Balarām Mandir was built.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I think of you at Rādhā-Dāmodara before you came to America, writing your books, unknown, with no American followers. In this photo you have returned. Your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is growing all over the world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I see you in this photo, yet I don’t see you. When you were here, I was with you, but I could not fully understand that you were an eternal associate of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Even today, how little I understand as I look into the sunny archway of the old Vṛndāvana temple, and you look back with your little group of fragilely held-together devotees, most of whom have now dispersed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us carry out your will, although we are not lion-hearted like you. Please help us chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and follow you to Vṛndāvana, to your Krishna-Balarām Mandir. Please help us serve the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. Will you one day reveal to us further entry into Vṛndāvana-*dhāma?*

\* \* \*

Here is another photo, this time taken in Māyāpur. Śrīla Prabhupāda is ringing the bell near the altar. From left to right behind him are Brahmānanda, Jayapatāka, and Paramahaṁsa Mahārājas. Śrīla Prabhupāda wears wrinkled, shiny silk. His feet are bare. The photo is dark and Śrīla Prabhupāda’s clothes are bright. Everyone is barefoot on the marble floor. Ringing the bell, tugging the cord, singing Hare Kṛṣṇa *kīrtana.* Śrīla Prabhupāda looks down, grave and dark, with yellow *candana* on his forehead. I was there, trailing behind, when you rang the bell in the Māyāpur Chandrodaya Mandir. They still ring that bell to enact your pastime.

I am not worthy to speak of my spiritual master or of Māyāpur *dhāma,* but let me not dwell on my inadequacy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I love you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are so often imperfect. The world seems full of faults. Our moment to moment practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is also full of faults. Not only that, but we have the propensity to always find the faults (*kuṭi-nāṭi).* Please rescue us from that tendency. Let us hear the bell ringing loudly in worship of the Lord. Let us follow you and desire to serve you, although this *ahaṅkāra* rides on our backs. It makes us look dark, like the darkness of the photo.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when I look at the marble in the temple room, I see only marble. But you see Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You see Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s mission and the pleasure of your spiritual master. And somehow you see some good in us, although our faults are so prominent. Please reprimand us and push us to become better. No nonsense. Serve and chant.

If we ask you a foolish question like, “How do we control our minds when we chant?” you answer almost curtly, “What is the question of controlling the mind? Just chant and hear and the mind will be controlled automatically.” Prabhupāda, it is as if you see our uncontrolled minds as willful misbehavior, just as you recognized the dysentery of your followers in India as the result of voracious eating and laziness. The uncontrolled mind of a chanter is just more nonsense. Stop it. Hear Hare Kṛṣṇa. Control the mind. Hmmm. Of course you are right. I will go to my room and try it again.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were always getting things started. You spent your whole time preaching in this world digging hard earth, planting—harvesting too—but always working with raw materials, men and women and Indians and *sādhus* and construction crews and cement. When you spoke to us, you usually didn’t tell us directly that we were the mistaken ones you referred to in your books. You spoke of Māyāvādīs and mundane politicians and envious people, atheists and materialists. We learned from you who to avoid in order to cultivate our *bhakti-latā.* You played with us, defeated us when we took the role of the Māyāvādī or the atheist, and somehow, by your loving expertise, the fire of your words was directed back to us. You did it indirectly, but still … we began to see the arch-enemy lurking within our own hearts.

The defeat is ours, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We surrender. Please help us to serve you in your mission and to love you and to pray to you eternally. You are the only one who can comfort us through this life beset with doubts and trials.

Your servant,  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. You are my eternal guide and shelter. Thank you.

Last year I had the honor of serving you in your rooms at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Vṛndavāna for three months. It was austere, it was purifying, it was the fulfillment of a desire in my heart to try to come closer to you. In those rooms, in that *seva,* I felt at home, and many of your disciples and granddisciples came there to take your *darśana,* to get your blessings for their future spiritual life. The thought came to me, Why did I so specifically feel at home there? The plain answer was that it is your rooms, and naturally one would feel at home in one’s father’s house. Without recognizing my father, how could I recognize my home? As my father, you want me to come home—to guide me back home, back to Godhead.

You have built your greater house, ISKCON. Sometimes I might forget that it is your house, but it is, and therefore there I am at home. Not only I, but all your disciples and granddisciples to come. Finally we will be back and at home in the spiritual world, under your shelter. Because you are there we will also be at home there.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for giving me shelter. Otherwise I would have continued to be a homeless *jīva* wandering up and down in the merciless, giant wheel of repeated birth and death.

I beg to remain a humble servant of your divine lotus feet.  
Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

## Varṣāṇa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

After worshiping Śrī Kṛṣṇa Janmāṣṭamī, we next worship your appearance day, which falls on Nandotsava, the festival of Nanda Mahāraja. Of all memorable and worshipable occasions in our lives, your appearance is the greatest of all and the most blessed event. There comes a time in the life of everyone when hope fails to appear from anywhere on the horizon. The limitations of material protection and human resources become starkly apparent. The *jīva* seeks frantically to adjust to life’s crises, but the dark shadow of death dims every hope that this world can offer.

Religion assures us, “Call upon the Name of the Lord. He is most merciful and loving and He will save you.” We asked in return, “What *is* the Name of the Lord? Where is the evidence of His love and mercy? If our religious teachers believe in eternal life, then why do they fear death? If they truly love God, why do they mistreat other *jīvas*?” Our inquiries yielded disapproval and rejection.

Turning our sight to the heavens, we cried out to the Lord, “Why have You given us eyes if not to see Your form, ears if not to hear Your words, a tongue if not to sing Your Name, and sentiments of love if You are unknowable?”

As we implored the Lord for mercy, being tossed about on the ocean of despair, a humble cargo steamship plied the sea from East to West. The lone occupant of the passenger cabin endured seasickness, dizziness, and two heart attacks en route for our sake. Bearing a message from on high, a message of hope and love from our common father, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, tolerated such difficulties out of love for us.

We wondered, “How can we call upon the Name of God when He hasn’t revealed His Name?” and “How can we say He is merciful and loving, our best friend, if He won’t even tell us His Name?” We prayed for an act of God, an agent of God, if you will, to reveal Him to us, and you prayed along with us; Your prayer was the answer to ours.

“How will I make them understand this message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? I am very unfortunate, unqualified, and the most fallen. Therefore I am seeking Your benediction so that I can convince them, for I am powerless to do so on my own. Somehow or other, O Lord, You have brought me here to speak about You. Now, my Lord, it is up to You to make me a success or failure as You like. O spiritual master of all the worlds! I can simply repeat Your message, so if You like You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding. Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I am sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts they will certainly feel engladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life.”

When our lives had reached such a state of total desperation that only an act of God could save us, you disembarked from the *Jaladuta* and entered within our vision, chanting the Holy Names of Kṛṣṇa, dancing in a joy which was not of this world, captivating our hearts with devotion to the Lord and your compassion for ourselves. Taking shelter of you, we forgot the hell that surrounded us because when you came to us from Vṛndavāna you brought all of Vṛndavāna with you.

You adopted us into your family and introduced us to your best friend, Kṛṣṇa, telling us as much as our feeble minds could comprehend about Him. You engaged us in His service, providing our qualifications and carrying whatever we lacked. You wrote books for our guidance and thus assured us of your continual association. You gave us everything. The nectar of your words and the example of your life gave us hope, faith, and joy which no words or deeds of this world could ever match or describe. You awakened such a sweet remembrance of a distant past and a future hope of running and frolicking in the pasturing grounds in the early morning hours in a land made of gems and full of cows where all motion was a dance, and all sound was song. You assured us that nothing other than our own choice could ever separate us from the love of God.

You brought the monsoon cloud of Kṛṣṇa’s form to shower down rains of mercy which sprouted the seeds of *bhakti* that Lord Caitanya had planted five hundred years ago. He had entrusted the cultivating process to you. You re-introduced us to our best friend and assured us of a glorious reunion with Him, with you, and with all the best of saintly persons as well.

You painted such a beautiful vision of reality in our minds beyond anything that poets had imagined or sages had envisioned. And then, after extinguishing the blazing fire of *saṁsāra,* which had nearly devoured us, and inundating us with the nectar of devotion, you suddenly seemed to leave us. It was a rude awakening to say the least. Not that you hadn’t prepared us, but still, we were not ready for that. Suddenly all of our achievements became empty and hollow, our lives seemed vacant and void, the world seemed dark and dreary without you.

I’ll refrain from saying anything more on this topic since words of pathos should not be repeated unnecessarily. We have no doubt that whatever you do is perfect, and that separation makes the heart grow fonder until reunion becomes inevitable. You told us that our love would be demonstrated by our cooperation to push on the movement you initiated. And certainly we feel you and Kṛṣṇa present in that engagement.

You removed the cause of our dissatisfaction and then you removed your visible self. In this way you taught us practically what love is. We know that love in separation brings the highest ecstasy. But separation from the pure devotee is really the only unbearable pain.

And so we pray to you now, not as a man, but as the truth. The representative of the Absolute Truth who inherits the power of God through the disciplic succession and who imparts it in turn to his faithful followers. We know you live forever in the form of your instructions, and in this way we find some solace. You told us that if we are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa we are together in spirit and that for the soul there is neither birth nor death, but there *are* feelings of separation, fear of separation, and pain of separation in spiritual relationships also. These are instances of the divine energy which nourish, enhance, and intensify the relationships of soul to soul and soul to God.

And so we endure these difficult times, being sustained by the hope which you gave us. Hope that our memory of your past physical presence will be a harbinger of a not-too-distant reunion. The hope you offered did not arrive from the horizontal plane aboard some cargo steamship. As the sun and moon appear to arise from the eastern horizon and spread their influence toward the west, you also followed this trend set in motion by the Supreme Sun and Moon, Śrī Caitanya and Nitāi. But actually the sun and moon are always situated above. You too have descended from above to deliver a message of supreme hope, after which you returned from where you came. Since you told us about that higher realm and attracted us there, you have left us with hope. Hope that because of our love for you, as well as yours for us, we too may someday gain entrance there. I don’t know Kṛṣṇa, nor can I comprehend what is pure devotional service. But I do know that you are our best friend and ever well-wisher, and that knowledge gives us hope. Your life and teachings have left us a precious heritage for guiding the lives of erring society on the march toward the highest attainment. You may remove yourself from our sight, but you cannot remove yourself from our hearts.

And with that hope, we are somehow passing our days, struggling through these difficult times. Pulled forward by the attraction you have instilled in our hearts, inspired by the prospect of meeting you again, and guided by the instructions you left us. Trusting that when the illumination of your instructions dawns within our minds and shines into our hearts, we will actually realize that you live forever in your instructions and your sincere followers live there with you. I, too, want to be there with you, and most of all I want to serve you. Service to you is our only connection with Kṛṣṇa.

O hope of all the fallen souls, I just want to serve you. Wherever that service takes me, that will be my greatest pleasure. You are my Lord birth after birth. I pray only for the recollection that I am your tiny servant.

Your servant,  
Varṣāṇa Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Agartala

Most worshipable dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most unworthy obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet as we especially remember you on this most festive day of your divine appearance on this planet.

Whose attention can escape the most unprecedented and inconceivable wonders which are being increasingly manifest in the form of wholehearted reception of your divine teachings by people from all corners of the world and from all walks of life? The world of Kṛṣṇa consciousness created by you seems to be in the mood of melodious madness of Gaura-Kṛṣṇa *prema*. Still more heartening to see is its fervor of *saṅkīrtana* to inundate the rest of the world with the same madness.

What you have given us is the Absolute Truth, one without a second, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, yet the ways various recipients have perceived Kṛṣṇa consciousness, appreciated Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and allowed it to capture their lives have truly demonstrated the beauty its variegatedness. As you used to say, in relation to the Absolute Good, everything is rightly adjusted, with the final result being all-good, because no influence of mundane duality can remain in relation to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Absolute Truth. Similarly, although we are surrounded and bombarded by so many seemingly conflicting subject matters, when it comes to the subject of your glorification, all so-called personal differences transform into wonderful spiritual flowers of offering variegated appreciations to Your Divine Grace. More amazingly, since the very motivation of our lives is your pleasure, when we glorify you in a collective spirit, mutual appreciation and co-operation mysteriously manifest among us.

While struggling hard to become more determined and committed to your eternal service, we cannot but remember the following words spoken by Śrī Uddhava to Śrī Kṛṣṇa after the Lord had instructed him in *yoga.* These words perfectly convey our eternal gratitude to Your Divine Grace:

*naivapoyanty apacitiṁ kavayas taveśa*  
*brahmāyuṣāpi kṛtam ṛddha-mudaḥ smarantaḥ*  
*yo ’ntar bahis tanu-bhṛtām aśubhaṁ vidhunvann*  
*ācārya-caittya-vapuṣā sva-gatiṁ vyanakti*  
*(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 11.29.6)*

**“O my Lord! Transcendental poets and experts in spiritual science could not fully express their indebtedness to You, even if they were endowed with the prolonged life time of Brahmā, for You appear in two features—externally as the ā*****cārya*** **and internally as the Supersoul—to deliver the embodied living being by directing him how to come to You.”**

We eternally beg to remain loyal to the service of your servants Bhakti-svarūpa Dāmodara Swami and your granddisciples.

Your servants in Agartala, Tripura, India.

## Almviks Gard

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Contemplating your wonderful personality, we conclude, Here is a Vaikuṇṭha man endowed with all twenty-six qualities of a Vaiṣṇava, a dearmost servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, a strong fighter against impersonalism, and a glorious preacher of Lord Caitanya’s mission.

Your words enable us to understand for the first time in Western history who is God and what are His features. By your arguments and logic you are defeating all mundane philosophers and religious cults.

Listening to your purports and explanations, we can understand the Vedic literature as it is. Preparing for a complete change in society, you instructed us, “Please distribute my books.”

By always trying to stay close to you through reading your books, listening to your words, and remembering your wonderful example, we are encouraged to continue to use our lives for Kṛṣṇa’s service.

Thanking you again and again, falling down at your lotus feet, we thank the Lord for putting us in contact with you.

Your disciples and granddisciples at the Almviks Gard Farm, Sweden  
(offering written by Haraka dasa)

## Austin

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We are incapable of properly glorifying you. On this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā it is our duty to do so, but we honestly do not know how to begin. Whatever words we try to compose with our contaminated minds are completely inadequate to glorify your unlimited splendor. All we can do is fall down in the dust of your lotus feet and beg for your mercy. Please bless us so that we can nicely glorify you. It is stated that by the mercy of the spiritual master a dumb man can deliver eloquent speeches. So this, our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the mercy we are begging. Please allow us to always nicely and appropriately glorify you at every moment for all of eternity by our every thought, word, and deed.

In practical terms this means that we are begging your mercy that the preaching may go on very successfully here in Austin. We desire to distribute your books, engage people in chanting the holy names, and distribute *prasādam*. If we do these things nicely, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that the conditioned souls will step forward to take shelter of your movement. Now we are seeing many people coming to take advantage of the preaching programs we are holding here. We pray, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that they all may take full advantage of your teachings by surrendering unto them.

We are not at all great, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are the truly great devotee. But still we are hankering that we may able to accomplish by your mercy some truly great preaching victories here so that you may become very, very pleased with us.

Thank you for this opportunity to glorify you on this auspicious occasion. There is no doubt it is a very feeble attempt, but we hope that it will be pleasing to Your Divine Grace.

Your servants in Austin, Texas.  
(written by Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa Adhikārī)

## Bali, Indonesia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your merciful lotus feet.

All glories to your transcendental preaching and book distribution.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how indebted the people in the future will feel because of your endless endeavor to save them from the hellish condition! You are so merciful! Facing great risk, you never hesitated to try to save them from the hallucination of material life and guide them to the Absolute Truth, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

O great soul, you are so merciful upon us. How can we satisfy you? We are so foolish and unable to serve you and repay our debt.

O mercy personified, kindly give us the chance to remember your glorious activities and chant your blessed name.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you appeared just to distribute pure love of God and to save human society from its dream. You brought the bridge to cross the ocean of birth and death from Lord Kṛṣṇa’s spiritual abode, Goloka Vṛndāvana, to the earthly planet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the genuine messenger of Lord Nityānanda and Lord Gaurāṅga, and you are fulfilling their desire that every town and village will chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare and merge into the ocean of bliss and transcendental happiness.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
All glories to the *sankīrtana-yajña*!  
All glories to your most blessed appearance day!

Your insignificant servants on the island of Bali, Indonesia.  
(written by Sarvabhauma dāsa)

## Baltimore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Unlimited glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious festival of your appearance in this material world to deliver the conditioned souls who were lying unconscious in the lap of Māyā. You descended from the spiritual abode to relieve Lord Caitanya’s anxiety.

It’s scary to think where we’d be without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Perhaps it’s similar to the feeling one gets upon undergoing a near-death experience. “Whew! That was a close call. Everything was almost over for me.” At such an occurrence one is sobered and has some realization of his precarious condition in this body, and all his problems, pride, plans, for the future—everything—become insignificant. There’s a renewed appreciation for life.

Unfortunately, after the initial shock of a near-death experience we often forget our helpless position and again become covered over, playing in the material world like a child who loses himself in his activities.

For the devotee, forgetfulness of Kṛṣṇa or weakening of spiritual practices is the worst thing. Indeed, it is like death. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us to develop and maintain constant urgency for spiritual life, our real life, the life you gave to us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you set the example by leaving Vṛndāvana and going to the hellish West to preach. You were determined. You spent many nights translating books while the world lay asleep. And at an elderly age you traveled worldwide, tolerating all bodily ailments to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, saying, “You’ve had many lives, so give this one life to Kṛṣṇa.”

In Vṛndāvana in 1974 you said, “This chanting is Lord Caitanya’s special gift. It gives us life. And if we don’t get life from this chanting, then simply sex life.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, the ISKCON society of devotees, transcendental books, holy places of pilgrimage like Vṛndāvana, *prasādam,* and all the other components of a spiritual society. It is impossible to ever repay what you have given us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Our *guru-dakṣiṇa* will be to continue eternally assisting your mission.

Please allow us to always be your humble servitors, valuing the dust of your lotus feet and taking your order as all in all.

Your servants in New Kulinagrama, in Baltimore, Maryland.  
(written by Kartika-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This year, your servants here in this quiet English village will be celebrating the twentieth anniversary of the house which bears your sacred name—Bhaktivedanta Manor. Twenty years ago yesterday you installed the Deities of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda, thereby transforming a Victorian country residence into a corner of Vaikuṇṭha. This house is your home. Every room, every corner of the gardens reminds us of you in some way. You lived here and taught us here. In our mind’s eye we still see you, walking through the village, cane tapping gently on the tarmac road, outside the old post office, disciples tripping over each other to keep up with your brisk pace. We see you seated on a bright yellow cushion on the front lawn of the Manor, cane at your side, drinking cup in front of you, describing the beauty of Goloka Vṛndāvana. As you talk of white cows, heavenly flowers, and the beauty of Kṛṣṇa, someone in a tree by the lake is playing a silver flute.

We see you in your rooms upstairs translating *Kṛṣṇa* book at your desk, or giving *darśana* and laughing with your disciples. We so clearly see you on your velvet *vyāsāsana,* perfectly placed in a red carpeted alcove. The old ballroom seems then to have been specially designed for a pure devotee and his worshipable Lord.

But especially this year we see you pouring milk on the “Delhi Deities,” then installing them upon a painted wood altar in the old black-and-white-tiled conservatory. You offer *ārati* to Their Lordships and gaze at Their beauty before leading your disciples in an ecstatic circumambulation of the new altar.

In this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is impossible for your servants at the Manor to ever forget you. The very bricks, timbers, and oak panels of this building are in themselves a meditation on you. We have tried to look after things during these twenty years, Śrīla Prabhupāda. With the devotion you inspired in us we have continued to serve the Deities. Now your home is famous throughout the country, and busloads of visitors come from hundreds of miles away to take *darśana*. Generations of schoolchildren and university students have come to learn about Kṛṣṇa here, and all have taken Their Lordships’ *prasādam*. Countless souls have passed through your home these past twenty years and have transformed their lives by doing so. Some have stayed and gone on to become the disciples of your disciples.

Hundreds of young couples have been married here, and as the years pass by they have brought their children up to see you as the greatest spiritual master modern history has seen. This is the heart of your movement here in the UK, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and as such, it continually pumps inspiration, spiritual guidance, and mercy through the roads, towns, and villages of this tired old country of England. So intrinsic has Bhaktivedanta Manor been to the growth and expansion of your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement in this country, it is inconceivable that there would ever be a time when you, your devotees, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda would not reside here, or that all should not have free access. But that is the threat which day and night hangs over our heads. Our security and the future of your preaching mission is threatened by the prospect that politicians want Bhaktivedanta Manor to close to the public on 16th March 1994. We do not want to simply remember the past twenty years with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We want your memory to go on here, in this place, for another twenty years, and after that for another twenty, and so on.

We therefore pray to you on this most sacred and holy occasion, twenty years to the day when we first gathered around you in this spot, that you will kindly guide us in the following months. Please inspire our words and actions so that we may truly be your servants and do everything as you would wish it. For seven long years we have prayed daily in this room that we can keep Bhaktivedanta Manor open. It has been a long fight. There are those in this country who think they are in control, who assume that God is not entitled to live where He pleases and have His eternal parts and parcels come to see Him. They would rather we all packed up and went somewhere else (although where, they cannot say). Who will tell them, “You cannot send God packing!”? (Many have tried.)

With faith in your order, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that we will be successful. As we gather here again to praise you twenty years from now, we will remember not only your pastimes but how our faith and love for you and your kind words to Kṛṣṇa on our behalf made our campaign motto come true: “Bhaktivedanta Manor—Here to stay!”

Your worried yet determined servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor, England.  
(written by Kṛpāmoya dāsa)

## Bologna, Italy

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories unto you! Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

Already sixteen years have passed since the last Vyāsa-pūjā in your physical presence, in 1977. By the unlimited mercy of the Supreme Lord you came to the West and taught all the world how to serve the Lord of the heart. You trained the fallen souls with great patience and enthusiasm. Even though we were helpless, homeless, without peace, without any goal of life, you were very affectionate toward all of us, and with great care and concern you provided all that was necessary to make spiritual advancement. It will never be possible to repay even a single drop of this ocean of debt for the great work you have done.

Within I’m coming closer to a ripening gratitude for what you have done, but it remains only a theoretical realization. You went through many hardships to develop this wonderful ISKCON. For more than twenty-five years many devotees have taken great benefit from the substantial results of your preaching. You opened many branches, installed Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities in many countries, and above all, you have offered the vast Vedic culture as it is. This was possible to do only by the most exalted and pure person. As said in the Fourth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (4.20.3):

*sudhiyaḥ sādhavo loke naradeva narottamāḥ*  
*nābhidruhyanti bhūtebhyo yarhi nātmā kalevaram*

When Lord Viṣṇu appeared in the sacrificial arena, He told King Pṛthu, “O king, one who is advanced in intelligence and eager to perform welfare activities for others is considered best amongst human beings. An advanced human being is never malicious to others.” You are the best devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for you performed the greatest welfare activities. You taught us everything—to preach, to educate nations, societies, family, and children in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If we examine the many gifts created by you, we can see that there is no limits to your benevolence toward the entire humanity.

Unfortunately, I regret not doing anything substantial for others. This is my problem. Hesitation and selfishness. Even though I have brilliant examples of how you dedicated 100% in the preaching mission in the service of the fallen souls, the weakness of hesitation and egoism prevail. This type of *māyā* remains fixed in my heart. Therefore today, on this miraculous event of your sacred Vyāsa-pūjā, your appearance, your glorious descent, I wish sincerely to learn more about your greatness, to learn how to love you, how to serve you, to love you more than anything. As you mention in your purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 17.15, “The word *snigdha* (very peaceful) and the word *su-snigdha* (affectionate) are used in verses fourteen and fifteen, and they are also found in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.1.8): *brūyuḥ snigdhasya śiṣyasya guravo guhyam apy uta*. ‘A disciple who has actual love for his spiritual master is endowed by the blessings of the spiritual master with all confidential knowledge.’ Śrīla Śrīdhara Swami has commented that the word *snigdhasya* means *prema-vataḥ.* The word *prema-vataḥ* indicates that one has great love for his spiritual master.” By loving you all perplexities will go far away, and this will allow me to have more conviction to distribute your wonderful books. By book distribution all problems are solved—economically, mentally, intellectually. When I was full-time *saṅkīrtana* man the motto was “Book distribution is the only solution!” This is also true now, as it was in the past, and as it will be in the future also.

Determination—to concentrate one’s consciousness in fulfilling your great mission. Once in the L.A. temple you asked, “Kindly help me.” How can ignore this humble request of yours? Why the hesitation? Even though you could do everything alone, you want to give credit to all your sincere followers and reward them with the matchless gift—Kṛṣṇa consciousness. By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, many devotees have sincerely taken up the preaching mission.

One of your greatest qualities is forgiveness. This is your compassion. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, forgive me for having wasted so much time, for the many offenses at your lotus feet, for the many discrepancies in pushing on your *saṅkīrtana* mission. Please allow me to rectify my materialistic behavior so that I can remain under the shelter of ISKCON, the spiritual world, so that I may stay with your wonderful assistants, your disciples, and be able to hear from them how to love you, to get infected by the mercy you gave them. You trained them *personally,* you gave them responsibility, nations, serious commitment, the burden of love. How fortunate you are! From them I can learn to love you; by hearing your tapes, reading your books, seeing your photos, I will also increase my love for you within the heart.

I desire to be more helpful and to serve the great souls in your ISKCON—to help more the temple, the Deity worship, the book distribution. This is all your causeless mercy only, to help us become purified.

The association of Godbrothers is so valuable. By this I feel your presence is closer, I feel more the importance of your mission. In this way I will become more dedicated and have more conviction, I will worry more for the fallen souls on this planet. Simply by following your footsteps in some tiny way, I will begin to appreciate the great love you felt for all, and especially for me.

I thank you more and more and pray that I can love, by your mercy, your mission, the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, with all my heart and soul, by distributing your books. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your insignificant servants in Bologna, Italy.  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Boston

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day of your appearance.

I have been asked to write to you on behalf of the devotees here in Boston. I don’t feel that I am qualified to glorify you in the proper way befitting your position. I am your granddisciple and I can’t presume to know you personally. What I know of you, I have heard from others, your disciples. However, after thinking about it, we granddisciples are also able to know and glorify you in our own way. Each devotee in the wide world of ISKCON is a product of your ever-expanding mercy. That mercy has continued to grow more and more even after your return to Goloka. So I would like to glorify you according to my own personal realization.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, can I talk to you? Please do not take me as presumptuous. I know to approach you through your devotees who are my spiritual masters. But, do you know of me, as you know your own disciples? I have heard that you do know us and are enthusing us in our own devotional service, given to us in *paramparā* from you. How this is actually happening is not completely known to me, and it is very wonderful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you first came to America aboard the *Jaladuta,* you wrote a poem to Lord Kṛṣṇa. In that poem you tell of the mercy of Lord Caitanya spreading throughout the world, where in “every city, town, and village on the earth, from all the oceans, seas, rivers, and streams, everyone will chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.” You credit this great achievement to your own spiritual master, Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, who, by his strong desire, will make all this place. By his desire you acted. And acted in such a miraculous way that you fulfilled this mission on your own, even in your advanced age. When you arrived in Boston you wrote another prayer to Lord Kṛṣṇa, where you prayed that He would make you dance. You danced so wonderfully, and by that dancing the whole world soon followed.

When your movement was spreading rapidly throughout the world, and many temples were opening, and many devotees were joining your *kīrtana,* you yourself began to appreciate how amazing is the mercy of Lord Caitanya. Once, on the occasion of the installation of Gaura-Nitāi Deities, you were telling the devotees of the mercy of Lord Caitanya:

“Caitanya Mahāprabhu is so kind—*parama koruna, pahu dui jana—*two Lords: *nitāi gauracandra*. Nityananda Prabhu and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, They’re very kind, you see. They have appeared just to reclaim the fallen souls of this age. So, They’re more kind than Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa, He is also very kind, He comes to deliver. But Kṛṣṇa demands, ‘You first of all surrender.’ Caitanya Mahāprabhu does not demand, He’s so kind. \[crying\] So take shelter of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and be happy.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were so much overcome by pure love for Kṛṣṇa that you were unable to hold back the tears of ecstasy. You were seeing all the devotees gathered around you and seeing that these boys and girls had been transformed by this kindness of Lord Caitanya. When we hear of these emotions of yours, we also cry, feeling your strong presence. Even those of us who have never met or seen you are able to feel this emotion. To cry in love for the Lord or His pure devotee is not an ordinary thing of this world. How is it that we are able to feel so much for you? We were so entrapped in Māyā’s illusion, we had no hope of getting freed from her grips. We knew nothing of Śrī Caitanya, what to speak of His topmost teaching: *premā pum-artho mahān,* “Pure love of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa the ultimate goal of human life.” How is it that now we not only know of Lord Caitanya and Radha-Kṛṣṇa, but we are also growing in our desire to love and serve Them? This is truly amazing, considering how sinful we were.

The only answer to this puzzle, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that you are even more kind than Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu! You are the one who came to us. You came yourself and pulled us out of our ignorance and engaged us in the service of the topmost forms of the Lord. So when we hear you express you heartfelt ecstasies in praise of Lord Caitanya, we feel for you. Lord Caitanya appeared and delivered the *dharma* for this age. His disciples, the six Gosvāmīs, elaborated and explained the highest teachings of Lord Caitanya. Following them, the Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas* continued to elaborate on these same teachings. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura proved that every human being, no matter what social class he may have been born into, is fully qualified to attain the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Then you came, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and brought all these teachings together, armed with your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and proved to the whole world that Lord Caitanya is the *mahā-vādanyāya-avatāra*. And that His devotees are even more merciful than He Himself. We may hear statements concerning the position of the spiritual master that explain how he is the external manifested expansion of the Supersoul, how all the qualities of the demigods are within him, how he is the supreme personality of servitor Godhead. These statements are certainly true, and you possessed these qualities in full, but they don’t express the feelings that the disciples have for the spiritual master. We feel so much for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you came personally and saved us. Although there may be many devotees of God in the world, the one who came to us and pulled us out of material existence is the one we love and establish a very personal relationship with. And seeing you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, expressing your appreciation for Lord Caitanya, who empowered you to deliver so many people of this world—seeing you so overwhelmed with emotion that you were brought to the point of tears—this is why we have so much loving feeling for you. You did it out of love and compassion for the souls of this world. *Kṛpāmbudhir yaḥ para-duḥkha-duḥkhi:* “The Vaiṣṇava is an ocean of mercy to the fallen, conditioned souls; indeed, he is so compassionate that, although for himself he knows no unhappiness, he feels unhappiness on account of their suffering” (*Vilāpa-kusumāñjali* 6).

So even those of us who were not with you when you were displaying this mercy personally are able to take part in this mercy of yours. Even after you have left us, your mercy continues to spread, and we can know and feel true love for you and all you have given us. Your love is so strong that it was not for the few who knew and surrendered personally to you, but your love is continuing to guide and nourish all devotees of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, even today. If we stop and look around ourselves, we see that everything we have is due to your mercy. The floor on which we stand in the temple is due to you. The *arcā-vigraha* forms of the Lord are also from you. Our chanting, eating, sleeping, dressing, talking, preaching and praying all stem from you. Now in your absence we can still see you in these things if we are a little observant, and also your direct disciples are now guiding us and bringing us to you and to your topmost teachings, *prema-bhakti*.

Your ISKCON is your love for the world. The spiritual master bestows *prema-bhakti* upon the disciple. Pure love of God is not found anywhere in the Western world. It is the most confidential of all knowledge and the highest perfection for all aspiring devotees. As Lord Caitanya broke open the storehouse of this love of God, you distributed it on His order. He said that there is so much fruit of love of God that He could not possibly distribute it all; therefore His disciples in *paramparā* have taken up His order to preach this throughout the world, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have done this to the greatest degree. Not only have you given the ways and means to achieve *prema-bhakti,* but you have made a society to continue this same mercy for everyone. As Lord Caitanya made no distinction as to who was fit and unfit to receive this gift, you also made no distinction and gave freely to whomever would take it. Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura sings, *gṛhe bā vānete thāke, ‘hā gaurāṅga’ bo’le tāke, narottama māge tāra saṅga:* “Whoever can cry out ‘O Gaurāṅga, O Lord Caitanya’ with feeling and love, then Narottama says ‘I want to associate with him.’“ Whoever it may be, it doesn’t matter. He can be a *sannyāsī* or a householder, if he can feelingly cry out ‘O Lord Caitanya,’ he wants that person’s association. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have many, many followers who love you and who can very feelingly call out “Hā Śrīla Prabhupāda, *jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!” From them we can also learn how to love you. We have to find and attach ourselves to those devotees who have this strong love for you. It doesn’t matter who he is—a *sannyāsī,* a householder, a temple devotee, or someone who lives outside. A book distributor, a pot-washer, a GBC, or a new *bhakta,* anyone! All these devotees are pleasing you by serving your mission in whatever capacity they are able. We can likewise learn how to please you by pleasing them.

You once said that we will please you the most by becoming lovers of Kṛṣṇa. I believe that. So if someone is showing the symptoms of real love of Godhead and is able to teach others how to love God, and if they can feelingly cry out “*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda,” I want to be with them.

Your servants in Boston.  
(written by Mādhava dāsa)

## Boulder

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our aspiring, humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Somehow or other, by unexplainably good fortune, we, your grandchildren here in ISKCON Boulder, have been given a rare opportunity to serve your sons and daughters in assisting your mission. The unique gift of *harināma-cintamaṇi,* or the touchstone of the holy name, has been given to us by you.

We solemnly pray that this mood of ceaseless *bhajan* and serving the Vaiṣṇavas in a favorable, loving way may one day be our constant meditation. So many times you have said that by constant chanting and selfless service to the Vaiṣṇavas, all perfection awaits. We aspire to imbibe this mood by spreading Lord Caitanya’s benediction to the living entities through *harināma-cintāmaṇi* and your mercy. Thank you for this matchless gift.

Your eternally indebted servants in Boulder, Colorado.

## Brisbane

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dustof your lotus feet. All Glories to Your Divine Grace.

Another year goes by and we are drawn closer to the time of death, which is imperceptibly closing in on us like the cat catching the rat. We reflect on your glories and how you never felt the same fears as we do. For you the passing of time was nonexistent, as in the spiritual realm, where it is only noticeable by its absence. We, however, being conditioned souls, are forever identifying with this temporary material world, and time is everything. Since we are firmly gripped by the ideals of this world and its multifarious expansions, our illusions grow day by day. Nowhere are there any good qualities to be found. Everywhere we see the sufferings of this world expanding. As we sit by, watching ourselves dragged helplessly toward our inevitable end, we cry out in anguish for your grace. Although you have left us the legacy of your presence, we are so foolish that we cannot perceive what you have given us. Forever wanting we are left only with the miseries of birth, death, old age, and disease as companions. Afflicted by the modes of material nature, we are being tossed as on a gigantic ocean. Seeing all this, you made arrangements to engage our desires, our bodies, our minds; all we needed to do was turn to Kṛṣṇa and agree to fulfill His wishes. Giving us literatures, good instructions, and all facilities, you set us on the right path. Teaching by your own personal example, you gave us all we would ever need to accomplish the task of crossing the ocean of *saṁsāra*. Many came forward to see your marvels and applaud your deeds. Only a blind man would not have appreciated the miracles you achieved in only a short span of time. Now that the task is for us to continue, let us not try to make you our order-supplier. Let us not demand that you give us anything; rather, let us take to you the fulfillment of all the goals you set for us, and let all people of this universe glorify you as the one who saved us from a destination spanning from Brahmaloka down to Pātālaloka.
