Let our only desire be the pleasure of the spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa. Only then will this body be a worthy object for your pleasure.

Your unworthy servants in Brisbane, Australia

## Buenos Aires

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances unto the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Many times we have heard that you built a house in which everyone could live, and in one sense we can see this fulfilled in every temple, where we can find devotees coming from different social classes. But the true dimension of what this means could be realized in the last festival in India, in association with the family you created and in front of the vision of what ISKCON is: the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

I doubt that your work could find a more appropriate name. Since every living entity is originally Kṛṣṇa’s servant, each and every living entity can fit into the house you have created. Perhaps one could say that either not everyone is prepared to surrender to Kṛṣṇa or our society might not be ready to shelter all the conditioned souls of this world. But you have taught us how to accomplish all this through your books and other recorded instructions. And in due course of time—as long as we follow your divine instructions with enthusiasm, confidence, and patience—everything will be developed. This can be easily appreciated. Although Māyā is beating strongly, although Kali-yuga is advancing quickly, and although many of us come from the most degraded social classes—in spite of all this, your society is becoming stronger, larger, and more consistent because of Your Divine Grace and the efforts of your followers.

Therefore, we do not have the slightest doubt that you have built a house where everyone can live. Now it is our business to invite everybody to come and live inside, so that everyone has an opportunity to revive his original Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the intelligence to make your desire and that of all the other *ācāryas* a fact, a fact that sings your glories in all directions.

Your humble servants in Buenos Aires, Argentina.

## Chapel Hill

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious day!

Now this is the twenty-first time I have participated in your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, and I am getting older but not much wiser. At least, however, I can try to remember my impression of two lessons you have taught us:

1\. *Everyone is subordinate*. We all have to encourage one another with realizations and behavior that show how surrendering to Lord Kṛṣṇa’s service is the only enlightened self-interest. No other advancement for the sake of ego, mind, and body will every satisfy us—not being a potent controller, a responsible provider, or a psychologically liberated man or woman. We have to always be in terror of that worst of fates, “independence.” Especially the preachers and managers of our society need to inspire those under their care to become steadily more and more dependent on devotional service to the Lord, attracted and not repelled, engaged rather than abused; and to do this, they have to constantly remember that they also are only servants.

2\. *Everyone needs to be made a devotee*. ISKCON preaching is meant to be universal. Every devotee should join in it however he can, and every nondevotee has to be started back on the path of self-realization. This is a very bold project you have set out for us, not just working for our individual salvation or that of a few kindred souls, but revolutionizing the whole quality of human civilization. Even in the long, glorious history of Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas* in the last thousand years, no one else has tried anything so bold—founding an international society of Vaiṣṇavas to reestablish Vedic civilization around the world against all odds. To have much success in helping you with this endeavor, we are going to have to grow a lot in both strict purity and unprejudiced compassion; with even one of these two qualities but not enough of the other, we will only be self-righteous or complacent, and ultimately frustrated. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the perfect manifestation of purity and compassion together. Although not with us now in your bodily form, you are here in your books and in your disciples, irreplaceable assets that we should treasure and eagerly share with the world.

Maybe, in a small way, your servants who have just started their work here in Chapel Hill, North Carolina, can be of some use to you in the following year.

Your servants at the Chapel Hill preaching center and the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust Center for Sanskrit Studies.

## Chicago

**I Would If I Could**

All glories go to you, Prabhupāda  
for you teach how to love God.  
Although your glories are very bright,  
I’ve no desire to write.  
But with your mercy I surely might.

I’ve heard on this most auspicious day  
we should have much praise to say.  
And since respects should come from the heart,  
I’m quite reluctant to start,  
for my heart’s very dreary and dark.

I’ve heard in you true shelter I’ll find,  
but I’m not sure that’s my kind.  
So although you may have your big dreams  
of having me join your team,  
I wish to devise another scheme.

Your guidance I should appreciate,  
but instead I tend to hate  
that you look for my humility,  
not mundane ability.  
Perhaps that’s just my stupidity.

The whole idea of approaching God  
for me is just a facade.  
But you see who I really am.  
This good front’s merely a scam.  
My desires could break a great dam.

You say that this world is not for me,  
but I’m not sure I agree.  
I’m attached to being in this well  
though it has an awful smell.  
I’m still attracted to life in hell.

You tell me to stop sins of all kind,  
but you must keep this in mind:  
I’ve stopped sex, drugs, meat-eating, defense—  
all merely on a pretense.  
Round my *anarthas* there’s a steel fence.

Don’t you see that I would if I could  
and that I know that I should?  
But my thoughts are of nothing but sin  
as I play games I can’t win.  
Just see the predicament I’m in!

You say that I must humbly submit.  
However, I must admit  
that my heart’s full of envy and pride.  
So rather than join you side,  
from Your Divine Grace I hide.

You must see that I would if I could  
and that I know that I should.  
But I am busy searching for fame  
or at least for some good name  
in this mundane world of karmic games.

You invite me to follow you home—  
in this world no longer roam.  
But many doubts arise in my mind  
if happiness I will find.  
So in this world I’ll wine and dine.

You know I really would if I could  
and I do know that I should.  
However, my work is not yet done  
as I’m still searching for fun.  
And I won’t quit until I have won.

You beg me to grasp hold of your feet,  
good fortune I’ll surely meet.  
But there’s something which holds me back.  
Proper faith and trust I lack.  
So I’ll live in this flesh and bones sack.

You must believe I would if I could  
and that I know that I should.  
But although no one leaves here alive,  
I still really want to try—  
just shooting for the pie-in-the-sky.

You ask me to sacrifice my life,  
cut attachment with a knife.  
But I lack the determination,  
tuned to another station.  
I have too many aspirations.

You see I surely would if I could  
and I really know I should.  
But I want to control all I see,  
thinking it belongs to me.  
Aren’t I the supreme bird in this tree?

You request me to give Him my soul,  
make His lotus feet my goal.  
But my mind is not yet attracted,  
always being distracted.  
For me birth will be re-enacted.

Please believe that I would if I could  
and that I know that I should.  
But see what a grand fool I have been,  
my ears unwilling to lend—  
to hell the Yamadūtas will send.

You ask me to fully surrender,  
but this you must remember:  
that I am a slave of my senses.  
That’s the sastric consensus.  
My surrender’s only pretentious.

Actually I would if I could,  
and I believe that I should.  
But though I had much training in school,  
I still remain a great fool.  
And my brain’s made of nothing but stool.

To serve Māyā or else to serve you.  
I’m not sure which I should choose.  
But I heard that the Lord is most pleased  
when one serves His devotee.  
So please pour your mercy upon me.

I should follow your “don’ts” and “do’s.”  
For me there’s nothing to lose.  
Dear Gurudeva, hear my ardent plea.  
My life’s as useless as a tree’s  
if you fail to glance upon me.

And although I am not very wise,  
you can pry open my eyes.  
You can see my heart’s real desire.  
There’s no need to inquire.  
So please pull me out of this mire.

Māyā just keeps leading me astray,  
so I see no other way  
than to now fall at your lotus feet,  
holding some straw in my teeth.  
But my sincerity’s not that deep.

Although I’m an undeserving soul,  
I know your mercy’s the goal.  
I know that I have gone far astray.  
I’m a worthless renegade.  
Won’t you please save this soul anyway?

Your insignificant servants in Chicago.  
(written by Dānakelī-devī dāsī)

## Copenhagen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept our most humble obeisances. Kindly allow us to submit the following reflections.

**THE PAST**

Fame and influence? No. You are serving alone, only in the association of the order of your spiritual master. Uninvited, humble, and seemingly insignificant, you enter like a needle into New York’s Lower East Side hippie culture.

A few fortunate souls come to know of you. Very surprised to find such a pure soul in those surroundings, some of them become your followers. With three or four as trustees, you found an international society for God consciousness—or, rather, KṚṢṆA consciousness.

As you translate at night and preach in the daytime, traveling widely, Hare Kṛṣṇa gradually becomes a household word. Never before have so many religious books sold so popularly. Philosophical, political, scientific fools and rascals are boldly challenged. Under your order, devotees struggle to build temples in India.

Before your departure you start 108 centers, create a new generation of pure devotees, and establish yourself as a great new *ācārya* of our *sampradāya*.

**THE PRESENT**

“He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die.” Despite sceptics, you continue to live in your instructions. You live in your books and in the hearts of your intimate followers, who feel intense separation from you. These are your constant dwelling places. Thus you continue your mission uninterruptedly.

As a result, the whole world is feeling the weight of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement more than ever before. Books are being distributed by the millions. Everywhere, people who blindly accepted the moneymaking and sense-gratifying goals of the material society are now searching for new values of life. Millions get new hope in the hopelessness of the nineties.

Even here in Denmark, where most are blinded by material wealth, more people than ever are joining our society. And the countries where masses of people are embracing your teachings are already experiencing some major social changes.

**THE FUTURE**

Everyone is still amazed at the tidal wave of love of Godhead that flooded the whole earth by the turn of the century, especially through the far and wide distribution of your books. At a most critical point in time, when everyone expected the end of the world, you, one so-called old man, single-handedly caused a spiritual revolution. Thus a new golden age was ushered in, pushing out the forces of Kali.

Whatever masters, prophets, and divine incarnations the world previously adored now live only in the history books. Rejecting dogmatic teachings and stereotyped rituals, everyone has embraced the *saṅkīrtana* movement of pure devotion to Kṛṣṇa and the chanting of His holy names in ecstasy.

The celebration of your Vyāsa-pūjā day is no longer limited to a select few but has become a worldwide holy day, for you are firmly established as the spiritual master of the whole world.

Everyone treasures these prophetic words you once spoke, indirectly indicating yourself while commenting on Dhruva Mahārāja’s austerities in the forest:

“This example … clearly indicates that a devotee, by his personal devotional service, can influence all the people of the whole world to become devotees of the Lord. If there is only one pure devotee in pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, he can change the total consciousness of the world into Kṛṣṇa consciousness” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.8.80, purport).

\* \* \*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our words fall far short of adequately praising you. We can barely touch a drop of the ocean of your glories. Indeed, our efforts are insignificant compared to the words of praise that come from the greatest of sages and devotees from all over the universe on this day.

Nevertheless, we are very happy to take part in this annual celebration. We also pray to be able to remain somehow in the association of your pure devotees and to be able to render some service to you by helping to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement in this godforsaken part of the world.

Your eternal servants in Copenhagen, Denmark.  
(written by Lalitānātha dāsa)

## Dallas

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this auspicious day of your appearance.

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpa karena bhakta-gaṇe*

**“According to the deliberate opinion of all the revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā*** **1.45).**

This verse reminds me of the chapter we have been studying in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* “Gajendra’s Prayers of Surrender.” In this chapter, Gajendra the elephant, who was in a dangerous position, remembers prayers from his previous life and begins to pray to the Lord for deliverance. You write, “It is imperative, therefore, that all devotees in Kṛṣṇa consciousness practice chanting some *mantra.* Certainly one should chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* which is the *mahā-mantra,* or great *mantra,* and also one should practice chanting *cintāmaṇi-prakara-sadmasu* or the *Nṛsiṁha-stotra* (*ito nṛsiṁhaḥ parato nṛsiṁho yato yato yāmi tato nṛsiṁhaḥ*). Every devotee should practice in order to chant some *mantra* perfectly so that even though he may be imperfect in spiritual consciousness in this life, in his next life he will not forget Kṛṣṇa consciousness, even if he becomes an animal” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.3.1).

By your grace, every devotee chants the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* the Nṛsiṁha-deva prayers, and others each day. Thus you act as Kṛṣṇa’s representative to try to deliver us.

I am also reminded of one verse of Gajendra’s prayers:

“Since an animal such as me has surrendered unto You, who are supremely liberated, certainly You will release me from this dangerous position. Indeed, being extremely merciful, You incessantly try to deliver me…” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.3.17).

I am just like an animal, but still you are so mercifully trying to deliver me and every conditioned soul through your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Please let me always remember and praise you and pray for your mercy.

Your fallen servants at Rādhā-Kālachandjī Dhāma, Dallas, Texas.

## Debrecen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept this offering on your Vyāsa-pūjā day as a token of our gratefulness. Although every day of the year we remember you with love and gratitude, this day gives us an especially good opportunity to try to describe in words that which is inexpressible.

We are unable to thank you for the unlimited mercy with which you are flooding the entire world. You are flooding the world with the holy names of the Lord and your transcendental books, and out of your causeless mercy you are engaging us as well in the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Lord Caitanya. Out of your great compassion you have given us the opportunity to get free of material existence, which is full of miseries and ignorance, and you have given us the opportunity to dip into the ocean of nectar, the ocean of chanting the holy names. We will never be able to thank you enough, for out of your great devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa and your Guru Mahārāja you planted the seeds of devotional service in the Western world and sent your wonderful disciples here to eastern Europe. Out of their mercy we have now also had those seeds planted in our hearts. We will never be able to repay you for your great compassion upon us. There is just one thing we can do—constantly pray, “Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to give us your mercy.”

Your eternal servants in Debrecen, Hungary.

## Denver

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances on this most holy day. All glories to Your Divine Grace and to your divine mission, ISKCON.

In the introduction to *The Nectar of Devotion,* page *xx,* you state: “The author of *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu,* Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, very humbly submits that he is just trying to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, although he humbly thinks himself unfit for this work. That should be the attitude of all preachers of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, following the footsteps of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. We should never think of ourselves as great preachers, but should always consider that we are simply instrumental to the previous *ācāryas,* and simply by following in their footsteps we may be able to do something for the benefit of suffering humanity.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we’d first like to assure you that we are trying very hard to follow your basic full morning program, chant sixteen good rounds, and regularly read your books. In this way, by your mercy, we are confident we can decrease the contaminations of false ego so that we can more effectively execute the missionary activities you have entrusted to us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have enormous ambitions to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and if we honestly examine our own capabilities in the context of these ambitions, automatically we’re brought to our knees. Sometimes we find it difficult to maintain what you have already given us, what to speak of expanding the preaching. As you know, we have a very active Govinda’s Buffet in Denver. Offering palatable *prasādam* to our guests day after day, year after year, is definitely an austerity. We’re simply following through on something you conceived of many years ago, and we’re amazed and exhilarated by the positive impact Govinda’s has had on the people of Denver.

You said, “No one should go hungry within ten miles of any temple.” As a result of this desire of yours, our Food for Life program is now distributing ten thousand meals of *prasādam* every month to the hungry. It’s a really big endeavor, but by trying to fulfill your desire we are astounded at how appreciated our movement has become in Denver as a result of this program.

By your desire we have an opulent Deity standard. It’s a *yajña* that daily tests us, but the beautiful *darśana* of Their Lordships Śrī-Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, and Śrī Jagannātha, Śrī Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā are melting our hearts and alluring the minds of all who visit.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you stressed so much that ISKCON’S future depends upon the success of our *gurukulas*. We have had many failures in our society in this crucial area, but now we are trying very hard to make these failures the pillars of our future success in Denver. This year, by your mercy, a very qualified devotee is coming to the *yatra* to start a substantial *gurukula* with a Kṛṣṇa conscious curriculum and Kṛṣṇa conscious textbooks that he’s ambitiously writing. We are very hopeful, and thus the parents in the community are enlivened to work cooperatively to help establish this school. It is very difficult, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to do this properly, so please let us become your instruments in this effort to please you. We desperately need strong devotees, trained from the beginning of life, to make this planet Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, one of the most important instructions you gave us in your final days was, “You can show your love for me by how well you cooperate to keep this institution intact.” We are struggling to follow this instruction by working on our Vaiṣṇava relationships. Once you told devotees in the early days of the Boston *yatra* that when they cooperate nicely Kṛṣṇa will send new devotees. Kṛṣṇa is blessing us with several highly qualified new devotees, and so we are trying very hard to cooperate even more fully. We are also trying to achieve unity and a spirit of involvement within the greater community and congregation. Please bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to see “unity in diversity” and to establish a stable *varṇāśrama* community.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you know, devotees in the U.S. haven’t distributed books in recent years on the scale that you seemed to have envisioned. This is a serious discrepancy that the Denver devotees are working very hard to rectify. We have initiated a *mahā-saṅkīrtana* weekend, where we are encouraging every devotee to go out either on Saturday or Sunday. We will be putting full effort into the summer marathon and your Prabhupāda marathon in December. Even though by your mercy we remitted the most *lakṣmī* of any U.S. temple to your BBT this year, and even though also by your mercy we were the number one medium temple in book distribution in the U.S., we feel that we are falling way short of the mark in satisfying your intense desire to preach through the massive distribution of your books. To increase the book distribution sometimes seems impossible to us, but you often said, “Impossible is a word in a fool’s dictionary.” We are sometimes foolish, but we really want to increase book distribution to please you. We have strong faith in your promise that “everything will come if you simply distribute my books.”

Please give us your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because without it we cannot accomplish anything.

Your servants at New Badarikāśrama, Denver, Colorado.

## Detroit

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

This is a special year for Devasādhan Mandir. We are celebrating the 10th anniversary of the Bhaktivedanta Cultural Center, which is rightfully named in your honor. During the past decade, hundreds of thousands of conditioned souls have had the opportunity to experience your spiritual mission and receive the *darśana* of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjavihārī, Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā Devī, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. As we guide our guests through the opulence of the Fisher Mansion, we are able to expound your greatness as the transcendental person responsible for saving and developing this project. In the main foyer, our visitors stand in awe before a painting of Your Divine Grace. As they wander in amazement from room to room, they begin to feel affection for you as the mastermind behind this wonderful experience. As it began, the tour ends with your *darśana* in the master bedroom. The tour guide explains: “*Encyclopaedia Britannica* states, ‘Śrīla Prabhupāda astounded the entire literary world, writing over eighty-five books, all on India’s ancient spiritual culture.’“ And you are so much more.

Invariably, the guests ask all kinds of questions: “What is the meaning of ‘Bhaktivedanta’?” The tour guide is able to explain, “*Bhakti* mans devotion to God, and *vedānta* means the end of all knowledge. So Bhaktivedanta means that the end of all knowledge is devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.” And you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the perfect example of this profound truth. Please bless us to always remember that we are very small and dependent on your mercy. By serving your mission, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and following your guidelines, we will enter into the real understanding of Bhaktivedanta.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us follow your example and fan the spark of devotional service within each living entity we meet. Help us defeat the influence of impersonalism by learning to see Kṛṣṇa within the hearts of our Godbrothers and Godsisters. We are all your sons and daughters. And as you said yourself, our love for you will be reflected in our mood of cooperation. The extent to which others feel encouraged in devotional service is the measure of our own success.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us develop genuine humility. How can we chant the Holy Names properly without this? And help us become warriors who will never give up serving you. Please help us always keep you in the center of our lives.

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, we miss you.

Your servants in Detroit, Michigan.  
(written by Bhūśāya dāsa and Lekhaśravantī-devi dāsī)

## Durban

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

At every step the disciple is always fully dependent on the spiritual master. There is never any question of independently developing a relationship with Kṛṣṇā or advancing in Kṛṣṇā consciousness.

Sometimes a devotee may feel that he came to Kṛṣṇa consciousness because previously he acted piously or was attached to a philosophy which in some respects was similar to Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy. Or perhaps we may feel that we received Kṛṣṇa’s mercy or even that we were just lucky.

But none of these ideas are really true. To whatever extent we may have become Kṛṣṇā conscious, it is all simply your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Prahlāda Mahārāja states, “Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.5.32).

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came and engaged us in devotional service despite our material attachments. You did not have to do this, but out of your great kindness toward the conditioned souls you took it upon your self to do so. Perhaps one could argue that you were acting on Lord Caitanya’s behalf, or that you were ordered to do so by your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, but that does not in any way reduce the significance of the fact that it was *your* decision to do so.

For example, when Aṅgirā Muni came to visit King Citraketu, the *Bhāgavatam* (6.14.14) describes that he came “by his sweet will.” Actually, what to speak of the Lord not interfering with the desires of His great devotees to serve Him as they like, the Lord even feels obliged to satisfy their desires. Kṛṣṇā says, “I am completely under the control of My devotees. Indeed, I am not at all independent” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 9.4.63).

And now that we are engaged in devotional service, we are still dependent on your mercy. It is only through our relationship with you that Kṛṣṇā may accept our humble efforts to serve Him. Kṛṣṇā says, “What to speak of My devotee \[being very dear to Me\], even those who are devotees of My devotee are very dear to Me.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 9.4.63). He also says, “One who says he is My devotee is not My devotee, but one who is the devotee of My devotee is actually My devotee” (Ā*di Purāṇa*).

The greatest kindness we can show to conditioned souls is to bring them close to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. If we can help people appreciate you, then they will be appreciated by Kṛṣṇā. Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva says to Prahlāda, “One who always remembers your activities and My activities also, and who chants the prayers you offered, becomes free, in due course of time, from the reactions of material activities.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.10.14).

Kṛṣṇā is bound to accept anyone who accepts and serves you—He feels He has no choice. So let us all work concertedly to present you to everyone and in this way help them in the greatest way.

In that way we will also succeed in getting Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, by your mercy.

Your servants in Durban, South Africa.

## Finland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories unto you.

To describe your glories is possible only for a few.  
May I make a try to glorify you,  
Who always keep instructing us  
How to surrender, to give up our own view  
And go to travel with the *saṅkīrtana* bus?

Whether you were healthy or sick,  
Your spirit remained constantly the same.  
And demons thus got a steady kick.  
Demons, being always completely insane,  
Could never guess your next trick.

And your *prasādam*—what a perfect hook!  
To make us taste it you never needed a stick.  
For when a pure devotee starts to cook,  
Only sweet nectar comes out, and it is thick.  
And every single one of your divine books  
Smashed impersonalism like a sharp brick.

In whatever towns and villages you did appear,  
There was no end to your preaching and fight.  
Relieving people from their big nightmare,  
You instantly made their future bright.  
This made you, to Kṛṣṇa, very, very dear,  
And thus Kṛṣṇa never stayed out of your sight.

All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Finland.

## Gītā-nāgarī

**If He Walked Through That Door Now**

*The scene is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s milkhouse at Gītā-nāgarī. Hours after the Vyāsa-pūjā festival has ended, Sureśvara dāsa lingers by the bulk tank when the door opens and in steps Kaulinī Mā, the cows’ milkmaid and a resident of Gītā-nāgarī since 1976.*

**Sureśvara:** Mother Kauliṇi.

**Kauliṇi:** Hare Kṛṣṇa.

**Sureśvara:** I was sorry we didn’t hear an homage from you today, you were so busy in the kitchen. What would you say to Śrīla Prabhupāda if he walked through that door now?

**Kauliṇi:** \[*laughs*\] I’m very small amongst devotees, hardly anything, so I think what can I say to Śrīla Prabhupāda? How can I glorify him? I can’t. The feelings are inside, but they’re insufficient for what he deserves.

But then I think deeper, like why do I even stay here? It’s so much struggle, it’s not an easy life. So many ups and downs and struggles, and I can only come to one conclusion. Prabhupāda. I can’t say it’s because of my love for Kṛṣṇa or any qualities I have, it’s only because of Prabhupāda.

And because I’m so small, because I don’t have much to offer, the only thing I can offer Prabhupāda is service, you know what I mean? I can’t do anything great, but I can serve because service is natural for the living entity. If you’re gonna serve, why not serve the greatest, Prabhupāda?

As far as Prabhupāda coming through that door—and that is actually the door that Prabhupāda came through—we shouldn’t think that Prabhupāda came and left. Prabhupāda came and he never left. Prabhupāda’s here. I try to remember that in everything I do because, especially here, you get lonely, you know. If I didn’t think Prabhupāda was here, I’d be very lonely, actually.

When I was a brand-new devotee, somebody taught me that whatever you’re doing, think that Prabhupāda could come in at any second and see you. That Prabhupāda is here and you might not know it but he’s looking after you, and I really liked that. It really stuck with me, and I try to pass it on to other devotees, because I think it has really helped me through all the ups and downs. Prabhupāda is the impetus to stick and keep going because wherever Prabhupāda is, there is only auspiciousness. There’s nothing bad if you’re connected with him. Nothing negative, really. If you’re connected with Prabhupāda, you’re connected with Kṛṣṇa and that’s wonderful. It’s so nice to think like that.

**Sureśvara:** Prabhupāda used to say that, how he felt his spiritual master was always with him.

**Kauliṇi:** Right, how could you go on? I can’t think of any other person … what else could keep you going? Especially in our position. Maybe if you had some big position in the world, you could think it was something else keeping you going, but especially here, what else could it be but Prabhupāda? He’s always encouraging; even if you feel completely unfit and unworthy, you know that Prabhupāda doesn’t see you like that. He sees you as someone very special and dear to him. We don’t really know anybody else like that, you know what I mean? Nobody else like that but Prabhupāda.

**Sureśvara:** That’s why we’re attracted to him.

**Kauliṇi:** Because that’s what everybody’s looking for, a loving relationship and a meaningful relationship, and you’re not gonna find that anywhere else. It’s just illusion if you think that somebody is going to fulfill those desires, because those desires are actually spiritual desires. And Prabhupāda is spiritual. Our relationship with Prabhupāda is spiritual.

**Sureśvara:** Yeah, because people like you and me, for example, we had very little, hardly any physical association with Prabhupāda, but we still feel like that because the relationship is spiritual.

But, Mātā, you’ve been speaking *about* Prabhupāda. What if he were to actually walk through that door? What would you do? What would you say?

**Kauliṇi:** \[*laughs*\] I think probably what I would do, I would just apologize to him for everything. That’s what I would do, because he deserves so much more—

**Sureśvara:** Forgive me, but you’re still saying “he.” Can you say something directly to Prabhupāda?

**Kauliṇi:** Then if I think I’m talking to Prabhupāda, then I’d just cry because … that’s probably what I would do actually … and then I would ask … how I could serve him.

**Sureśvara:** Prabhupāda said that’s the only question he asked his spiritual master.

**Kauliṇi:** I know. What else do you say?

\[*A black cat jumps onto Sureśvara, who pushes him away*.\]

**Kauliṇi:** Bheema! Come … come on, Bheem.

**Sureśvara:** I don’t mind, it’s just that his claws went right through my overalls. Does he do that to you?

**Kauliṇi:** \[*laughs*\] Yeah, but I’m used to it. Sit down, Bheem.

One thing is, I would definitely tell Prabhupāda about Gītā-nāgarī too. I would say, “Prabhupāda, Gītā-nāgarī is still here. \[*laughs*\] You came here and you wanted this, an example of simple living and high thinking, Gītā-nāgarī. You wanted us to protect the cows, and we want to dedicate our lives to that desire of yours.”

And it may not be the greatest thing in some people’s eyes, you know, but we can’t give up, we can’t abandon it; it was Prabhupāda’s desire, so I can’t let him down. This is me personally, you know, I can’t go somewhere else, although \[*laughs*\] sometimes it sounds pretty attractive. I think, let me do my little part, because you have to dedicate some part of you to Prabhupāda. I have to make some dedication to him.

**Sureśvara:** Some commitment.

**Kauliṇi:** Some specific—

**Sureśvara:** And this is yours.

**Kauliṇi:** Right, I think so. This is my link. By serving Gītā-nāgarī, I know Prabhupāda; this is his desire—that Gītā-nāgarī flourishes. Because when you think about it, Prabhupāda is so much involved with Gītā-nāgarī: his original Gītā-nāgarī scheme and his coming in ‘76 and sitting on that same *vyāsāsana* that’s in the temple and his wanting to come back here in ’77…

**Sureśvara:** And wherever he was in the world, he would very often—

**Kauliṇi:** Speak about Gītā-nāgarī, yeah. And just by that alone, even though we have had our struggles and everything, I know that it will be successful. Because Prabhupāda came here and it has his blessings, so how could it not be successful?

\[*Sureśvara sees some writing on the back of Kauliṇi’s hand*.\]

**Sureśvara:** Does that say “milk” on your hand?

**Kauliṇi:** It means I have to culture the yogurt.

**Sureśvara:** Oh, *d-a-h-i,* means yogurt. You wrote that?

**Kauliṇi:** \[*laughs*\] Yeah, to remind myself. Because I always look at my hands.

You know, Prabhupāda so much liked the atmosphere here when he came, and so many devotees who came here say this place has the mood of Vṛndāvana, Gītā-nāgarī does. That’s special.

**Sureśvara:** That’s funny, you know, you’re right. When people came here, they say this place is really amazing, and the devotees are so grave and kind, and I’m thinking, “Boy, this place is a mess.”

**Kauliṇi:** \[*laughs*\] Yeah, but they come here and they specifically say it’s like Vṛndāvana in the West. I’ve had so many devotees tell me that, who have been to Vṛndāvana and who’ve come back and they say, “The closest thing to Vṛndāvana in the West is Gītā-nāgarī.”

So we want to glorify Prabhupāda, and we want to glorify Gītā-nāgarī because you can’t separate the two. Not just because we live here, but because Gītā-nāgarī *is* Prabhupāda. Gītā-nāgarī is Prabhupāda, no question about it. Just look around—you see the cows, and when Prabhupāda left here, he even talked about how the cats and dogs were eating out of the same bowl. The mood was there’s a harmony here at Gītā-nāgarī. Any other stuff, negative, that’s not Gītā-nāgarī, that’s just the material energy bouncing off the walls, our conditioning and everything. But Gītā-nāgarī, the way Prabhupāda envisioned it, and the way it actually is…

**Sureśvara:** Like Vṛndāvana. Prabhupāda writes about how in Vṛndāvana the tigers and the deer get along and …

**Kauliṇi:** Embrace.

**Sureśvara:** Yeah.

**Kauliṇi:** And Prabhupāda saw that here, you know. Like Vṛndāvana. Not the faults that we see. The faults in the crazy mind. On a *bhajana* tape I heard in the kitchen, Prabhupāda said you have to make friends with your mind. You have to engage your mind in something greater … how did he say it? You have to have—

**Sureśvara:** Something better to do.

**Kauliṇi:** \[*laughs*\] Right, right. And what could be better than doing something for Prabhupāda, you know what I mean? See, I don’t know how to speak very well, that’s my problem, one of my problems. I don’t know how to express myself. I know what I’m feeling and thinking, but I don’t know how to . . .

**Sureśvara:** Kauliṇi Mā, I think Prabhupāda just spoke right through you.

**Kauliṇi:** It’s actually simple, huh?

ALL GLORIES TO YOU, ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA, AND YOUR PURE-HEARTED SERVANTS!

Your disciples and friends in and around Gītā-nāgarī Village, Port Royal, Pennsylvania.

## Guadalajara

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our grateful obeisances at your divine and radiant personality.

Today we have the wonderful opportunity to meditate more deeply on you and what you have done for us. This is Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement for glorifying His pure devotee. At the same time the Lord is giving nectar to His devotees by giving them a chance to relish their eternal relationship with their spiritual master, and in this way He is showing us how He loves everyone who is on the path of devotional service.

It is with great gravity that we consider what we are doing for you and your mission, Śrīla Prabhupāda—how we are co-operating among ourselves, and how much we love you, really. We are examining our sincerity as we write this offering. This letter will reflect our true devotion for you. Are you pleased with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda? This should be our real preoccupation. Are we appreciating the service of your devotees?

Any hopes we have for preaching, managing, building temples, etc., are all useless if we are not able to have nice relationships with our Godbrothers. It appears that this is the biggest obstacle on the path of spiritual life. We cannot live peacefully, neither in this world or the next, if we fail to build friendship with your devotees and serve them. In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.19.21), Lord Kṛṣṇa states that one of the elements in achieving supreme devotion for Him is *mad-bhakta-pūjābhyadhikā,* “performing first-class worship of My devotees.”

Also, Lord Śiva told the goddess Durgā in the *Padma Purāṇa:* “My dear Devī, although the *Vedas* mention worship of the demigods, the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is topmost and is ultimately recommended. However, above the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is the rendering of service to Vaiṣṇavas, who are related to Lord Viṣṇu.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, at this moment here in our temple, we are intensely realizing the difficulty and importance of following these śāstric injunctions.

We thus beg you to help us soften our hearts, which are so hard. Please help us deal humbly with your nice devotees and appreciate the valuable service they are doing for you.

All glories to you, the life and soul of our exsitence!

Your servants in Nueva Nīlācala Mandir, Guadalajara, Mexico.  
(written by Hari-kathā dāsa)

## Guyana

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Kindly accept our most fallen and prostrated obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

We feel so unworthy, lower than the lowest amongst our many spiritual masters, God-uncles, -aunts, -brothers, and -sisters, that we can only hope that by your causeless mercy you will accept this, our humble offering, on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

It just seems obvious that with Centennial Celebration fever sweeping through the entire ISKCON world, almost everyone will be relating the goals they intend to accomplish by 1996. In our own little way, in our insignificant country, we are also trying to make plans and set goals that can bring you satisfaction and thereby provide us with your most cherished blessings.

We’re planning to formally launch this most ecstatic celebration through various media—newspaper, radio, and television—so that the almost 700,000 Guyanese will come to know of your greatness and achievements. We hope many will give their support to aid in realizing our goals.

First, we want to get all initiated devotees, along with the many congregational members, to join in this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā observance. Following this, we want to conduct a national Śrīla Prabhupāda re-union celebration, in which devotees from the various regions in Guyana will assemble for association and Vaiṣṇava entertainment, and, most important, will allow us to promote individual undertakings to accomplish something substantial for your Centennial Celebration.

In some of our Centennial Committee meetings, devotees came up with some exciting ideas. Already in our temples we have made it compulsory to read from your biography fifteen minutes daily after *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class. Recently we started a weekly meeting to discuss your Centennial Celebration, and we are trying mightily to re-organize temple functions as per your instructions the GBC resolutions.

Other Centennial plans and goals are:

1\) To have your photographs and /or writings based on your teachings on T-shirts, plastic bags, students’ exercise books, stickers, pencils and pens, etc.

2\) To conduct Nāma-hatta and *pāṇḍāl* programs in the hundreds, and to conduct local and national *pada-yātrās,* as well as lectures in schools, hospitals, and other institutions. In this way we want to spread your glories. We want people to know you, who gave us Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and to know the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa.

Ultimately, we beg for your kind and causeless mercy, inspiring and encouraging us through your transcendental reciprocation, to make us worthy and qualified to accomplish these ambitious goals.

We pray in earnest, longing to please you in all our thoughts and activities, beseeching you to show pity upon us and keep us protected under your lotus feet through the guidance of your dear servants.

Your granddisciples and aspiring followers in Guyana.

## Heidelberg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Please accept this meager attempt to glorify Your Divine Grace on this transcendental occasion.

In your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā,* Chapter 5, Text 7, you write: “He \[the pure devotee\] does not like to hear anything except topics relating to Kṛṣṇa; he does not like to eat anything which is not offered to Kṛṣṇa; and he does not wish to go anywhere if Kṛṣṇa is not involved.” As your followers and as aspiring devotees of Kṛṣṇa, we similarly find that we don’t like hearing anything unless it relates in some way to you or service to you; nor do we like to eat anything not approved by you as offerable to Kṛṣṇa; nor do we wish to go anywhere if service to you, Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee, the Founder-*Ācārya* of ISKCON, is not involved. But, as you so often invitingly challenged, “What is the difficulty?” Because our lives are dedicated to your service, in fact there is no difficulty to act exclusively in relation to you and Kṛṣṇa—provided, that is, we can feel your presence among us and in our hearts.

One way that we feel your presence is by seeing and remembering the various and numerous places where you visited or resided. All of the places you lived in or visited are special places of pilgrimage for us (even Calcutta, that anomaly of a city in which you so kindly appeared!). We like to remember you in these places, even when you apparently are not “personally present” in them. Even places where you took morning walks, what to speak of the rooms you occupied in Los Angeles, Bombay, Bhaktivedanta Manor, Māyāpur, and in many other temples—all remind us of the fact that you are with us, patiently instructing and encouraging us. And as you stayed in so many physical locations, making pilgrimage places of wherever you went to spread Kṛṣṇa’s glories, so you have taken up residence in each of our hearts, making pilgrimage places where we can always go, taking shelter in remembering and serving Your Divine Grace.

I think that for all of us who are now trying to follow in your footsteps—disciples, granddisciples, and friends—your first “temple” in America at 26 Second Avenue in New York City holds special significance. At least for me, this modest storefront is a symbol of your personal victory, a symbol of your undaunted commitment to the order of your spiritual master. It is a symbol of the victory of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement over the unmatched odds of Kali-yuga materialism, which has been gnawing away at human culture for the last five thousand years.

I never saw you personally at 26 Second Avenue, but I like to think of it as your permanent place of residence, a place of pilgrimage from where your mercy expands into my heart. Indeed, over the years, trying to serve you and become Kṛṣṇa conscious, I have come to feel your presence in that particular scene: My heart—a New York City, a concrete and plastic jungle, a vast morass of doubts, delusions, and depressions, contains a relief agency, a “26 Second Avenue,” your “preaching center,” where you are seated, patiently offering me the matchless gift of your sublime association. I can come any time into your storefront, where I can find you, the real matchless gift, Kṛṣṇa’s matchless gift of mercy. You are always there, always available to accept my humble obeisances and service, and to preach to me, freely giving your inexhaustible inspiration.

From you, from your preaching center of matchless gifts in my heart, I gradually gain confidence in the process of devotional service. I *will,* by your mercy, one day overcome the modes of material nature; I *will,* by your mercy, one day know what it means to take full shelter of Kṛṣṇa. As from a nondescript storefront on 26 Second Avenue in New York you launched the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, which continues to spread like concentric waves throughout the world, so also from the “26 Second Avenue” of my heart you are spreading faith in Kṛṣṇa to the farthest reaches of my being. Eventually there will remain (I would like to be able to say “Now there remains . . .”!) no space left for anything but your mercy. At that time I may, following in your footsteps, challenge other conditioned souls with full confidence, “What is the difficulty” to always remain engaged in Kṛṣṇa’s service?

No, we don’t like to do anything not related to your mission; we don’t like to go anywhere without you being present—in your books, your instructions, your service. Please therefore always stay with us in the preaching centers *of our hearts,* and continue forever to preach to us and show us how to preach Kṛṣṇa’s glories to others on your behalf.

Your servants in Heidelberg, Germany.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra dāsa)

## Iceland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

When I see a picture of you, I see someone who is simultaneously satisfied and dissatisfied. You are satisfied as Kṛṣṇa’s eternal servant on the topmost platform of perfection, satisfied to give your whole existence towards satisfying the Lord by engaging the fallen conditioned souls in His service. But at the same time you are always dissatisfied that there is not enough being done for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You would never sit back and say, “Yes my dear disciples, now you have done enough.” No. You would say, “Very good, but now … double it!” You said that you had one disease, one problem, that you just couldn’t think small, you could only think big. You would always want more and more for Kṛṣṇa, and one can easily see that you would never stop pushing Kṛṣṇa consciousness on to increase it more and more. Even if the whole world took to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you would undoubtedly continue to push everyone to attain newer and newer heights of spiritual enlightenment. How can anyone following in your footsteps ever sit back and feel satisfied that he has done enough?

Therefore we are dissatisfied by seeing the unlimited amounts of work to be done, but we are very pleased that we may engage in satisfying your most merciful desires. And we know that there is no better way for us to satisfy you than by distributing your books. Your own Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Goswami, used to be so pleased when one of his disciples would distribute just one small magazine. And you told your disciples time and time again in so many ways, “Preaching is the essence, books are the basis,” and “There is no more direct way to preach than to distribute my books.” Although you left your body, you are still present with us in your books and lectures. We are always reading your books and hearing the tapes of your lectures, and this gives us much enlivenment and spiritual enlightenment so that we can carry on in your ecstatic mission with great enthusiasm and determination. These books and lectures are an eternal asset for the whole human society. Even long after our present material bodies have perished, the books we are distributing now will remain. And someone in the distant future may pick up one of these books and start distributing them to others. Thus your instructions and your mission are eternal, and we can use our temporary material bodies to spread that eternal message to others.

The best way to glorify you is by distributing your books, because these books are nondifferent from you, and anyone who understands these books must understand your glories. One man here in Iceland, after reading the Icelandic *Īśopaniṣad* (the first of your books in this language), said to me, “It was a very good and wise man who wrote this book.” So the people are appreciating your work. One lady told me that we should give this book to the government. The world is degrading day by day, as we can see by the atrocious activities going on in places like Yugoslavia and even in the so-called peaceful metropolises of other countries. The world is being constantly shocked by this horrifying news, and more and more people are becoming alert to seek a solution. And your books give the perfect solution for peace and prosperity for all the world. Therefore your books are like a shower of life-giving rain in the desert to soothe the parched hearts of this world’s misdirected civilization.

We have a large stockpile of mercy in the form of your books here in your small preaching center, and we love pouring them over this fresh, new preaching field. Gradually their ice-cold hearts will become melted and the influence of this winter age dispelled. Nothing pleases us more than seeing that stockpile reducing every day, because we know that this is very pleasing to you. Please bless us with your mercy so that we may go on very strongly in giving your mercy to others, and so that we can surrender more and more to Kṛṣṇa and do more and more, better and better in devotional service without stopping.

Your eternal servants in Iceland.

## Johannesburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Lord Caitanya, while visiting Vṛndāvana, became ecstatic upon meeting a certain *brāhmaṇa* at the birthplace of Lord Kṛṣṇa in Mathurā. The *brāhmaṇa* was a disciple of Mādhavendra Purī, and when the two met they chanted, danced, embraced, and sang the glories of Mādhavendra Purī in pure ecstatic love. Then Lord Caitanya spoke these famous words,

*dharma sthāpana-hetu sādhura vyavahāra*  
*purī gosāñira ye ācaraṇa, sei dharma sāra*

**“A devotee’s behavior establishes the true purpose of religious principles. The behavior of Mādhavendra Purī Gosvāmī is the essence of such religious principles” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **17.185).**

In discussing further with the *brāhmaṇa,* Lord Caitanya quoted from the *Mahābhārata,* establishing the exclusive importance of a devotee’s behavior:

*tarko ‘pratiṣṭhaḥ śrutayo vibhinnā*  
*nāsāv ṛṣir yasya mataṁ na bhinnam*  
*dharmasya tattvaṁ nihitaṁ guhāyāṁ*  
*mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ*

**“Dry arguments are inconclusive. A great personality whose opinion does not differ from others’ is not considered a great sage. Simply by studying the** ***Vedas,*** **which are variegated, one cannot come to the right path by which religious principles are understood. The solid truth of religious principles is hidden in the heart of an unadulterated self-realized person. Consequently, as the** ***śāstras*** **confirm, one should accept whatever progressive path the** ***mahājanas*** **advocate.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are perfect in following in the footsteps of the previous *ācāryas,* and you expect the same of us. Your heart is revealed in your Bhaktivedanta purports, which are your personal ecstasies, and it is our duty as disciples to capture your mood. Thank you for allowing us to dive and surface in the nectar of your ambrosial words, which allow us to execute devotional service that is not a matter of sentimental speculation or imaginative ecstasy but whose substance is practical activity.

Your are advocating the path of *uttama bhakti,* and by the performance of such devotional service one enters into your association. We pray that we can always take shelter of your lotus feet.

Your servants in Johannesburg, South Africa.

## London (Soho)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

The festival of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā is that time in the year when we pause from the mayhem of our everyday lives to reflect on the greatness of the Lord’s representative and our good fortune in being blessed with his association. By the kindness of both *guru* and Kṛṣṇa we now have an opportunity to rid ourselves of the diseased condition of material life and enter the loving company of the Lord and his devotees. When we hear your glories, a natural appreciation awakens in our hearts. Such an attitude is absolutely essential for our progress in spiritual life. Unless one is mindful that one’s spiritual standing is dependent on the favor of *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, one cannot progress at all: *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, one is struck by great awe looking at your personal achievements. Where is that person to equal you in faith? Against all the odds you labored to develop this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. What a battle! And your reward? More hard work. You would not rest, satisfied with your success. Knowing that you were pleasing your spiritual master fueled your enthusiasm to make Kṛṣṇa’s teachings available and understandable to all people in all corners of the world. By your saintly example you inspired (and continue to inspire) thousands of souls to dedicate their lives to serving Kṛṣṇa without reservation.

When we realize what it takes to convince even one or two people to take Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously, or what it takes to barely maintain a temple financially, then we get some glimpse into the enormousness of your achievements and the depth of your personal character. Although we have been transformed from those social misfits we were before your mercy reached us, there are evidently many things that we have yet to learn—individually and collectively—if we are to serve your mission effectively: how to serve together in a spirit of unity, how to implement Kṛṣṇa consciousness in a broad social context, and how to emulate you in the way that we deal with each other—with kindness, respect, patience, straight-forwardness, and humility.

When we consider the mission you have left for us to complete, it is quite daunting. But then our task is considerably easier than the one your spiritual master entrusted you with, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You built up this wonderful society of Vaiṣṇavas simply out of your faith in the principle of disciplic succession. In your prayer *Mārkiṇe Bhāgavata Dharma,* you humbly declare that though you are named Bhaktivedanta you have neither *bhakti* nor *veda* but only faith in *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, and you request Lord Kṛṣṇa to reveal the actual meaning of *bhaktivedānta*.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā-deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

It was not merely your organizational ability, clever innovation, or philosophical erudition that enabled you to be so successful in your life’s mission. There was your sheer dedication, your unswerving commitment to please your spiritual master without any other interest—unalloyed determination. It is our duty to find such resolve within ourselves in a humble effort to repay you. It will never be possible to settle our account, but it is only right that we feel obliged to strive for others as you have sacrificed for us. If you are pleased with our efforts, then our life will become successful.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your kindness upon us, particularly for engaging us in the service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśsvara.

Your humble servants at the Soho Street temple in London, England.

## London (south)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* Prahlāda Mahārāja says:

*naiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghriṁ*  
*spṛśaty anarthāpagamo yad arthaḥ*  
*mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekaṁ*  
*niṣkiñcanānāṁ na vṛṇīta yāvat*

**“Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for His uncommon activities. Only by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.5.32).**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we, your grandchildren at the South London *yātrā,* are still very attracted to the materialism offered by this city full of sense gratification. But as Lord Caitanya says to Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī (Cc. *Madhya* 19.151), *guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja.* Somehow, by the grace of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, we have been able to smear the dust of your lotus feet on our bodies in the form of your mercy—the sublime movement that you have established, the transcendental teachings that you made available to us in your books, the temples that are truly an oasis in the midst of this Age of Kali, and the delicious secrets of Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*. Now by your mercy and that of your faithful disciples, who are diligently looking after this Society in your absence, we are able to taste the nectar that comes from serving Śrī Hari and His devotees.

If we even try to contemplate a life without you we can only shudder in fear. We would be drowning in the ocean of material existence, trying to grasp onto the logs of temporary enjoyment that float past. The *śāstras* enjoin:

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatam*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all good qualities of a human being.”** ***(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22)**

In this day and age of questionable spiritual leaders, there is no one who could be a better navigator for this journey out of this material world than yourself, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are so kind to let us onboard the flagship of ISKCON even though we have no qualification or sincere desire. But as your *Līlāmṛta* describes, you always have open arms for everyone, however fallen—*patita-pāvaṇa—e*veryone is welcome to join the spiritual family that you gave birth to.

And your dear child, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, is that family. You said that our love for you will be shown by how we cooperate together to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are still young neophyte devotees, and fault-finding is almost second nature with us. Hence, we beg you to forgive us for the offenses that we have committed. If we follow your example, we will realize how much you tolerated just to obey the order of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Mahārāja. His instruction to preach the message of Godhead in the West was your life and soul, and even in the face of hardship your resolution never once faltered. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for remembering us wretches in the Western world.

We owe you so much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we know that this debt will never be repaid with our insignificant attempts at following your instructions. But as your centennial year approaches, let us take this opportunity to step up our devotional service in honor of you. By 1996 let our temple be strong with ecstatic programs, a full congregation, superb *prasādam—*just the way that you meticulously set up each and every temple all those years ago. Let us honor the 100th anniversary of your transcendental appearance by sincerely trying to follow your teachings.

In the *Śrī Gurv-aṣṭaka,* by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, the last verse states, *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prāsado yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi:* “By the grace of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master one cannot make any advancement.” You are the spiritual master of the whole world—*jagad-guru*—and you are so kindly allowing fallen souls like ourselves to engage in *bhakti-yoga*. We are so grateful for this, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we just pray that we never let go of your lotus feet and slip back into the misery of material life. It is said:

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghāyate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāriṇam*

**“By the mercy of the** ***guru,*** **even a dumb man can become the greatest orator, and even a lame man can cross mountains.”**

You are our eternal *guru* and spiritual father, and although just like children we commit countless mistakes and offenses, please forgive us. We beg that you will continue to give us your mercy.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your worthless granddisciples at the ISKCON center in South London, England.  
(written by Rādhā-Dāmodara-devī dāsī)

## Malmoe

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

As the Supersoul guides the soul out of the body, similarly your radiant personality lights up the path toward Kṛṣṇa for the bewildered souls of this dark age. The path you lit up is still lit, and the method you taught is still fresh. Youngsters who were born after you left this incarnation of yours are being attracted by that method and becoming joyful by touching it, just as we have. The same items and activities are there as always—the books, *kīrtana,* Deity worship, and *prasādam*—and new people from all different nationalities and religions are coming and partaking of the transcendental pleasure they emit. Truly you built a house in which the whole world can live.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for coming and taking the trouble of establishing ISKCON, translating all your books, guiding your disciples in all aspects of life, recording transcendental music, and inspiring so many people to engage their skills in the cause of Kṛṣṇa. The fruits of your transcendental trouble are still nourishing all of us. Thank you for training your disciples to continue the *guru-paramparā* and give shelter to us, who unfortunately never got the chance to meet you in person. By their mercy we are nevertheless linked up with your mission, and we try to do our small part in this regard.

We pray that by your causeless mercy we shall be able to always serve you and your disciples and engage in the ever-blissful activities of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This will perfect our lives. Please never allow us to swerve away.

Your insignificant servants at the preaching center and restaurant in Malmoe, Sweden.

## Manchester

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your wonderfully inspired service.

Once more we are trying to find appropriate words to properly glorify you. Somehow we have left to the very last minute this most important service. Without doubt, a sad reflection on the true state of our hearts. But please accept these few words and forgive our ingratitude, which is surely the most abominable of all bad qualities.

It is ISKCON which has saved, is saving, and will save us from the misery of repeated birth and death. Without your starting this movement, we would now be solidly in hell. We want to serve ISKCON and will not hear any criticism. We would rather die than lose our faith in your movement. We would sooner stand face to face with a Bengal tiger than with an envious critic of ISKCON. We would rather be cast down to the lowest of the low depths than be expelled from ISKCON. And we would prefer to be hurled into a blazing fire than take birth where the glories of this divine limb of the Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu tree are unknown.

This testimony is to your real and lasting glory. We were all previously untouchable and unseeable. We had no desire for religious life and no intention of giving up sin. How is it that we are now so fully imbued with the above sentiments? Even the demigods, indeed even Devarṣi Nārada himself, are astonished to see such wonderful effects. Even if just that—the very fact that such degraded beings as ourselves have taken to Kṛṣṇa consciousness—redounds to your glory, then we can consider our lives perfect.

Praying at your lotus feet that we may attain that position as our highest desirable goal, we are  
Your fallen servants in Manchester, England.

## Mayaguez

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Mayaguez, Puerto Rico, is a new addition to your growing family of international centers for propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Those of us pioneering this new field on behalf of your sacred mission are most fortunate and very grateful for the opportunity. We pray that we may please you by our humble efforts.

Our longing is to become sincere preachers and followers of Lord Caitanya’s teachings. In your absence, though, the path has become more difficult and obscure, and consequently we often become discouraged. Our good fortune is that your powerful instructions have left profound impressions on our hearts. Your grace has made it possible for us to perceive that material existence is an unnatural and temporary condition of life, where there can never be genuine love, peace, or happiness. And similarly we have realized that material existence may be rectified by the mercy of a pure devotee representative of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In this conviction we find sufficient hope and encouragement to go on in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Our dependence on you to engage in service to Lord Caitanya is clearly understood. Devotional service can be both the easiest and most difficult thing in the world. You once described it as being “easy for the sincere,” but “impossible for the crooked.” In this inauspicious age, though, who is not crooked? I, for one, am quite crooked, and if I am ever to be free from the many unwanted material desires that plague my heart, it will be only by our divine intervention. The Kali-yuga is like a dangerous jungle teeming with ferocious animals. As a lost lamb would have no likelihood of surviving such peril, we have immeasurably little hope of surviving the overwhelming dangers of the current age and finding our way back home.

It appeared to many of us that somehow spiritual life was much easier and less complicated in the past. As you yourself often described it: “dancing, chanting, feasting, and philosophy.” I suppose this is almost always the case in the beginning, and especially it was the case during your presence. Nonetheless, as we have come to learn, life’s most coveted perfection cannot be acquired without sacrifice and surrender. Its “ease” lies in proportion to our sincerity, and becoming genuinely sincere about *unmotivated* devotional service may prove to be the severest ordeal of our lives. Still, our hope abides in you. Before calling for the mercy of Kṛṣṇa, intelligent devotees call for the mercy of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, for She is the embodiment of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and the one whose request on behalf of a devote Śrī Kṛṣṇa cannot refuse. In a similar spirit we are calling out to you, asking that you pray to Lord Caitanya for our deliverance from the muck and corruption of this degrading age. In consideration of the awesome service you’ve rendered Lord Caitanya, we feel He will not refuse.

As the turbulence and severity of the times rapidly increase, we become more acutely in need of your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Hard work and patience we know we must practice, because in genuine spiritual life nothing is cheap, romantic, or impractical. Content we are to struggle against the many obstacles on the path, and to bear with whatever hardships are ordained for us by Kṛṣṇa’s divine arrangement. Our humble prayer to you on your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration is that you keep us always engaged in the missionary activities of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Your servants in Mayaguez, Sabana Grande, and western Puerto Rico.

## Melbourne

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The sun appears to take its birth  
In the morning time above the earth,  
And then it seems to pass away  
In the evening, leaving gloom and grey.

Yet we all know from experience sure,  
And from spotless *Purāṇas* clear and pure,  
That the sun eternally doth shine  
Far beyond the horizon’s line.

In a similar way you have appeared  
In *mlecchas’* lands that were ne’er revered  
For piety, nor cleanliness,  
Nor mercy, nor for truthfulness.

How kind of you to come to places  
Where the sinful live, whose darkened faces  
Indicate passion, wrath, and greed.  
Yet to your words they’ve taken heed.

Bright-faced now, from hearing you  
Their lives remolded, fresh and new.  
They see you always within their heart,  
And so for them you ne’er depart.

Prabhupāda, your steps were never wasted—  
Every spot a *tīrtha,* nectar tasted.  
Your glories dominate the mundane sphere,  
Reflected in *bhaktas’* hearts so clear.

Let us pray that every place  
Where you have held the preaching mace,  
And every word that you have spoken  
Be revealed by devotees now awoken.

Can I measure or fathom your mercy sweet,  
Your oceanic compassion? An impossible feat!  
So I stand on its shore and serve without stop—  
I, Kūrma dāsa, pray to taste but a drop.

Your servants in Melbourne, Australia.

## Mexico City

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

We offer our obeisances at your feet again and again thousands of times because we will never be able to repay your unlimited mercy and kindness toward us. We don’t want to even think what our lives would have been without Your Divine Grace. Without your grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no hope for us, so today we fall at your transcendental feet and beg you, please allow us to fulfill your desire.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your prescription is so simple: “Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy. Take Kṛṣṇa *prasādam,* and always serve Kṛṣṇa.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the strength and sincerity to fulfill your desire—to bring to your divine shelter, the shelter you constructed as ISKCON, all the conditioned souls wandering in this most polluted place in the world, Mexico City. Please let us convinced them to follow you, to know you, to obey you, and to make their lives happy by serving you. Then they will come to the right conclusion—that you are the most magnificent person.

You once said to a disciple, “If you follow my instructions, you will always be happy.”

In those words are the secret for the highest success one can achieve: not to deviate even an inch from your divine instructions, which are nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa’s desires. In this way Kṛṣṇa is showing us the way to come back to His supreme abode, where everybody is so blissfully serving Him.

So please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the intelligence to serve you and work together to fulfill your desire to bring all the fallen souls to your house. You built this house in which the whole world can live by serving Kṛṣṇa’s dearmost servant, His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your most fallen servants in Mexico City, Mexico.

## Miami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day, the glorious celebration of your appearance in this world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have mercifully taught us a most valuable lesson, one that even the greatest mundane teachers of this world cannot understand. You have taught us that the highest perfection of life is to become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Practically everyone in this artificial, misdirected, dog-eat-dog society thinks that the perfection of life is to advance in materialism. Thus the so-called success of a few people is based on their ability to exploit the rest.

You have delivered to the world a unique philosophy that is not based on materialism and exploitation. It is based on the principle that spiritually we are all equal and that we all have an equal opportunity to perfect our lives through Kṛṣṇa consciousness. By establishing this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, you have built a house in which the whole world can live peacefully. There is room in this house for everyone. You want everyone to feel himself part of the Kṛṣṇa conscious family because in fact everyone is already a part. He has only forgotten. Not only does the whole world have an equal opportunity to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, but for those of us who are following the path, we also have an equal opportunity to do wonderful things for spreading the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. Our success in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness does not depend on any material considerations, such as high education, wealth, managerial ability, or anything else. It only depends on our sincere desire to carry out your order to preach.

In the ordinary sense, how could you, as an old man, have single-handedly spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world? Your success was made possible because of your dedication to your spiritual master. Lord Caitanya empowered you with every ability necessary to spread this movement. Similarly, as we become increasingly dedicated to carrying out your order to preach, Lord Caitanya will certainly use us instrumentally to do wonderful things that we could never have done otherwise.

Actually, by your teaching us to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, you have taught us the art of being instrumental. Lord Caitanya has already concluded that Kṛṣṇa consciousness will be spread all over the world. Thus, our success in preaching is guaranteed. We need only show our love for you by working cooperatively with this movement and doing our best to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere. Then, as you showed us by your personal example, Lord Caitanya will use us to do wonderful things according to our purity. And that ability to become pure is possible for everyone, not just an elite few. This is the only true movement of equal opportunity. All others are artificial, being based on the bodily concept of life.

Thus we pray again and again at your lotus feet that you will help us to work cooperatively among ourselves to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere. To work cooperatively among ourselves and instrumentally under your order will guarantee that Kṛṣṇa consciousness will be spread all over the world as it has never been spread before. In just a few short years we will be celebrating your one hundredth anniversary. Before that time, let us learn to work cooperatively and to convince our congregation to also work cooperatively to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere. That will be the ultimate perfection of life for everyone.

Your humble servants in Miami, Florida.

## Milan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, our only shelter in this dark material existence.

On this holy day of your appearance, words of gratitude and glorification rise spontaneously in our hearts for the most precious gift that you have so mercifully bestowed upon all of us.

I was wandering within this material world, wasting my human form of life, when you started this movement and the distribution of your life-saving books. I was caught by your mercy, and I was saved like thousands of other conditioned souls. Thus I was able to use my youth in Kṛṣṇa’s service and create such a good fortune for myself and for others.

And, by my continuing to engage in devotional service, giving all my life to Kṛṣṇa, this material existence gets further and further away.

*tulayāma lavenāpi na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam*
